一个小小的身影把一幅金锦的网披上阳台栏；一个小小的貂皮与朱红的身影登上栏来，安放一座小小的三重十字。总助祭红衣主教用扩音喇叭放声宣告：
“我向你们宣告大喜的信息。我们的教宗，是英格兰的玫瑰之主乔治，祂给自己取名哈德良第七。”
他让位给另一个小小的身影，银金两色，在太阳里生辉。一线清细的嗓音唱道：“我们的帮助在主之名。”
留声机录下洪亮的应唱：“祂创造了天与地。”
哈德良七世举起手，再唱：“愿全能的天主，✠✠✠圣父，✠✠✠圣子，✠✠✠及圣神，降福你们。”
这就是赐予城市与世界的宗座降福。

## IV / 四
===Original===

Now things went briskly. There was a brain which schemed and a will to be obeyed. The hands began to realize that they would have to act manually. Dear deliberate Rome simply gasped at a Pontiff Who said “Tomorrow” and meant it. The Sacred College found that it had no option. Naturally it looked as black as night. But the Cardinal-Archdeacon could not refuse point-blank to crown; and, when Hadrian announced that His incoronation would take place in the morning on the steps of St. Peter’s, futile effort suggested difficulty preventing possibility. That was the only course open to the opposition. Three cardinals in turn alleged that there would not be time to give notice of the ceremony, to arrange the church, to issue tickets of admission. Hadrian swept these ideas aside, as rubbish. Another courted catastrophe saying that there was no time to summon the proper officials. He heard that there were sixteen hours in which to summon those who actually were indispensable. A fifth said that, owing to the antichristian tendencies of the times, no representatives of the King of France, of the Holy Roman Emperor, of the First Conservator of the Roman people, were forthcoming; and he politely inquired how the quadruplex lavation could be performed in their absence? The Pope responded that He was capable of washing His hands four times without any assistance, in the absence of legitimate assistants: but the General of the Church was not to seek: the modern Syndic of Rome was the equivalent of the ancient First Conservator: the Austrian Ambassador could represent the Empire: while, as for wretched kingless unkingly France﻿—let someone instantly go out into the streets of Rome and catch the first Christian Frenchman there encountered. Anyhow, the quadruplex lavation was accidental. The essential was that the Supreme Pontiff should sing a pontifical mass at the high altar of St. Peter’s, and should receive the triple crown. These things would be done at eight o’clock on the following morning. All the doors of the basilica were to be fixed open at midnight; and so remain. No official notice need be published. And that was all. Then the Pope shut-up Himself in His predecessor’s gorgeous rooms, inspecting them till they gave him a pain in His eyes.

===Chinese===

于是诸事雷厉风行起来。有了一个出谋划策的头脑，和一个必须服从的意志。众手这才醒悟：它们得当真动手了。亲爱的慢性子罗马，被一位说“明天”就算数的教宗惊得直喘。神圣枢机团发觉自己没了退路。自然，它黑得像夜。可总助祭红衣主教无法断然拒绝加冕；而哈德良宣布祂的加冕礼明晨在圣彼得台阶上举行时，反对派只剩“费劲设阻以蔽可能”这一条徒劳的路了。三位红衣主教先后声言：来不及通知典礼、布置教堂、印发入场券。哈德良把这些主意像垃圾一样扫开。又一位自招祸事，说来不及召集合乎规程的官员。祂答说：召集不可或缺之人，现有十六个钟头。第五位说，时世背教，法兰西国王、神圣罗马皇帝、罗马人民首席执政官皆无代表到场；进而客气地请问：四重盥手礼缺了他们怎么行？教宗答：缺了正任辅礼者，祂完全能自己把手洗上四遍，不劳旁人：教会的将军却不难寻：现代的罗马执政官即古时的首席执政官：奥地利大使可代表帝国：至于那可怜的无王无王相的法兰西——派人即刻上罗马街头，捉住碰见的头一个信基督的法国人就是。总之，四重盥手是可省的枝节。要紧的是：至高教宗当在圣彼得高祭台唱一台教宗弥撒，并受三重冠。这些事定于明晨八点举行。大殿所有门户午夜起一律敞开；不再关闭。无需发布公告。就这样。随后教宗把自己关进前任那几间金碧辉煌的屋子里，看着看着，看得眼睛发疼。

===Original===

Luckily He had secured his pouch-full of tobacco and a book of cigarette-papers: He smoked, and thought, looking out of the windows over Rome.
After sunset, He ate some cutlets and a salad: placed two chairs face to face near the right-hand window; and sent for the Bishop of Caerleon and a large jug of milk. His interior arrangements were as disreputably healthy as those of a schoolboy.
Dr. Talacryn came, and observed the forms. Hadrian sent him to clear the antechambers and to close the doors. He returned and remained standing. The Pope was sitting in one of the splendidly uncomfortable red chairs.
“We have sent for Your Lordship because We have occasion for your special services.”
“I am at all times very ready and willing to serve Your Holiness.”
Hadrian was attracted to this bishop. Lots of his acts He loathed: but He liked the man, and believed him honest. The bishop was attracted to the Pope. He liked Him: but he could not understand Him, and was a little frightened of Him: but still﻿—it was as well to know all that could be known and that might be useful.
“We placed this chair for Your Lordship,” said Hadrian.
Dr. Talacryn was astonished: but not more than much. His trained placid nature stood him in good stead at a mark of favour which would have abashed many, and rendered others presumptuous.
“I thank Your Holiness,” he simply said. It appeared that the ship was cleared for action.
The Pope continued in His usual concise monotone. He spoke in the key of E♭ minor, very quickly indeed, slurring the letter *r*, clipping some words and every final *g*, enunciating others with emphasis, in a manner curiously suggestive of fur and india-rubber and talons. As for His matter, He seemed to be arguing with Himself by the way in which He arrayed His ideas, disclosing His process of thought.
“We have very much to do, and We are confronted by the physical impossibility of carrying out Our schemes. We find Ourself surprisingly placed at the head of affairs. We believe that We should not have been placed there unless the service, which We are able to do, had been deemed desirable. Therefore We feel bound to act. But, though We know (or shall know) what to do, yet We cannot do it with this one pair of hands. We must have assistants with whom we can be intimate, and who themselves can be sympathetic. First of all, We wish to have Your Lordship.”

===Chinese===

幸亏祂早已把那一荷包烟丝和一本卷烟纸抓到了手：祂一边抽烟，一边望着窗外罗马的灯火想事。
日落后，祂吃了几片肉排和一份色拉：在右手窗边对摆了两把椅子；着人去请卡尔勒昂主教，外带一大壶牛奶。祂的内部运转，健康得像学堂里的小子一样不成体统。
塔拉克林博士来了，行礼如仪。哈德良打发他去清空前厅、关好门户。他回来侍立。教宗坐在一把华贵而不舒服的红椅子里。
“我们召请主教阁下，因为吾等有用得着阁下专长之处。”
“我随时乐意为圣下效劳。”
哈德良被这位主教吸引。他的许多行径祂厌恶：但祂喜欢这个人，相信他诚实。主教也被教宗吸引。他喜欢祂：可他看不透祂，还有点怕祂：不过——多知道一点能知道的、兴许有用的，总归是好的。
“这把椅子是吾等为阁下设的，”哈德良说。
塔拉克林博士吃了一惊：但不至于大惊。他素有的静定功夫，替他接下了这许多别人会受宠若惊、另一些人会忘乎所以的恩宠。
“谢圣下，”他简单地说。看来，这条船已清舱待战。
教宗继续用祂惯常的简促单调讲话。祂的嗓音是降E小调的调门，确实说得飞快，把字母*r*含糊带过，有的词掐头，一切词尾的*g*尽数砍去，另一些字又咬得极重，那味道古怪地叫人想起毛皮、橡皮和利爪。至于内容，祂陈列念头的架势，像在同自己辩论，把祂的思路摊给人看。
“我们要做的事极多，而吾等面对着无法亲力亲为的物理不可能。吾等发觉自己出人意料地身居万事之首。吾等相信，若非吾等所能效的劳被认为可欲，吾等不会被安放在此。因此吾等觉得责无旁贷。可是，纵然吾等知道（或将知道）该做什么，仅凭这一双手也做不成。吾等必须有能与吾等贴心、而他们彼此又能同声同气的助手。头一个，吾等想要阁下。”

===Original===

The bishop was quite honest enough to get a little rosier with pleasure.
“Very pleased, whatever,” he said.
“Next, We need information. Do you know the circumstances which led to Our election?”
“In the main they are known to me, Holiness. Indeed, I may say that they are generally known﻿—except to the Supreme Pontiff Himself,” the bishop added, with an episcopally roguish smile.
Hadrian enjoyed the point. “Please bear this dogma carefully and continually in mind:﻿—the Pope well-informed is wiser than the Pope ill-informed. Remember also that Hadrian at all times desires to know everything. At present He wishes to know what you know about His election. Briefly: the details can be given later.”
“Briefly, the Conclave found no Pope by the ordinary means; and committed the task to certain Cardinal-Compromissaries. These chose Your Holiness.”
“But why?”
“Cardinal Courtleigh﻿—”
“Was he a Compromissary? How many were there?”
“He was one of nine. The others were﻿—”
“Never mind their names for the moment. Now We take it that these nine cardinals are well-disposed toward Us?”
“Most assuredly, Holy Father.”
“Good! Nine! The names please?”
“Courtleigh, Grace﻿—”
“Archbishop of Baltimore. Yes?”
“Saviolli﻿—”
“What is he? He formerly was nuncio or something in America, was he not? Please give the status of each.”
“He was Archbishop of Lepanto and Pontifical Ablegate to the United States of America. Now he is one of the curia. Then came della Volta, formerly Majordomo, also of the curia: he, by the by, is Your Holiness’s Double, according to Cardinal Courtfield.”
“How delicious!” Hadrian vivaciously put in.
“Mundo, who led the Compromissaries, is Patriarch of Lisbon. Nefski is Archbishop of Prague, poor fellow﻿—”
“Why ‘poor fellow’?”
“Oh he was nearly killed by the anarchists.﻿—Well then, Ferraio is Archbishop of Milan: Gentilotto is Prefect-General of the Society for the Propagation of the Faith, and Fiamma is Archbishop of Bologna. The two last were candidates at first, but gave it up by consenting to become Compromissaries.”
“These, you say, are well-disposed to Us?”
“Yes, Holy Father.”
“A Celt: an American: a Portugal: five Italians: and a Pole.”
“No, a Bohemian, Holiness.”
“Oh?” Hadrian directed the bishop to a writing-table. “Now, whether this be in accordance with regulations or not, We neither know nor care.

===Chinese===

主教为人够诚实，闻言泛起一点得意的红。
“乐意之至，别的且不说，”他说。
“其次，吾等需要情报。阁下知道吾等当选的原委吗？”
“大略我知道，圣下。说实在的，这些原委已是尽人皆知——只除了至高教宗本人，”主教补上一句，带着主教式的促狭笑。
哈德良赏识这个俏皮。“请把这条教义审审牢记、时时牢记：——教宗消息灵通，胜过教宗消息闭塞。还请记住：哈德良无时不欲知晓一切。此刻祂想知道阁下所知的祂当选始末。拣要紧的说：细节日后再详。”
“拣要紧的说：秘密会议循常法选不出教宗；便把差事托付给了几位全权红衣主教。他们选出了圣下。”
“可为什么？”
“考特利红衣主教——”
“他是全权代表？一共几位？”
“九位之一。其余是——”
“名字暂且不忙。那么吾等可以认定，这九位红衣主教对吾等怀有好意？”
“万分确实，圣父。”
“好！九位！请道名姓？”
“考特利、格雷斯——”
“巴尔的摩总主教。嗯。”
“萨维奥利——”
“他是做什么的？从前在美国当过教廷大使之类，是不是？请把各人的职位说来。”
“他原是勒班陀总主教、驻美利坚合众国教廷特使。如今属教廷院内。其次是德拉沃尔塔，原任大总管，如今也属院内：顺带说一句，照考特菲尔德红衣主教的说法，他是圣下的‘分身’。”
“妙极了！”哈德良活泼泼地插了一句。
“芒多，全权代表们的领头人，是里斯本宗主教。涅夫斯基是布拉格总主教，可怜的人——”
“为什么‘可怜的人’？”
“哦，他险些叫无政府主义者杀了。——再说，费拉约是米兰总主教：真蒂洛托是传信部总长，菲亚马是博洛尼亚总主教。后两位起初是候选人，因允诺出任全权代表而退出了。”
“这些人，你说，都对吾等怀有好意？”
“是的，圣父。”
“一位凯尔特人：一位美国人：一位葡萄牙人：五位意大利人：还有一位波兰人。”
“不，是波希米亚人，圣下。”
“哦？”哈德良指点主教到写字台前。“那么，这是否合乎规章，吾等既不知道，也不在意。

===Original===

Please write”﻿—He sipped a glass of milk; and began to dictate﻿—“ ‘Hadrian VII﻿—Bishop﻿—Servant of the servants of God﻿—wills that you immediately shall come﻿—to Him﻿—in the Vatican Palace﻿—at Rome. Nothing﻿—except the gravest physical inability﻿—or your duty to your family﻿—if such there be﻿—is to impede you. All Catholics﻿—are to afford you﻿—the comfort﻿—conveyance﻿—and assistance﻿—of which you may stand in need.’ Please sign it with your own name and make five copies of it.”
The bishop, sighing for his typewriter, diligently wrote in an angular oblique almost illegible hand. Electric lights sprang up in the City. The Pope lighted candles, closed the curtains, and rolled a cigarette. Then He came and sat by the table, looking at the manuscripts﻿—considering the huge ring on His Own index-finger. Smiling to Himself, He took a taper and a stick of sealing-wax; and produced the Little-Peter-in-a-Boat at the foot of the six sheets.
“Address them,” He continued, “to the Reverend George Semphill, St. Gowff’s, North Britain:﻿—Reverend James Sterling, Oakheath, Stafford:﻿—Reverend George Leighton, Shorham, Sussex:﻿—Reverend Gerald Whitehead, Wilton, Warwick:﻿—Reverend Robert Carvale, Duntellin, Ayrshire:﻿—and﻿—yes, do you know that eighteen years ago he had the most exquisitely beautiful face and the most exquisitely beautiful soul and the most exquisitely horrible voice of any boy in the college﻿—address the sixth to Percy Van Kristen, 2023 Madison Avenue, New York.”
While Dr. Talacryn was closing the envelopes, the Pope Himself wrote on a sheet of paper which, also, He sealed:

> Hadrianus P.M. VII dilectissimo filio Francisco Talacryni Caerleonis Episcopo.
> Te in cardinalem Designamus et Approbamus: quod tamen sub silentio tenebis donec tempus idoneum aderit.
> Datum Romae. Sub annulo Piscatoris. Anno pontificatus Nostri I, AD VIIII Kal. Mai.

===Chinese===

请写”——祂呷了一口牛奶；开始口授——“‘哈德良七世——主教——天主众仆之仆——意愿尔等即刻前来——到祂那里去——在罗马——梵蒂冈宫。除最严重的身体不能——或对家庭的责任——倘有此责——外，不得有任何阻碍。全体天主教徒——均应向你提供——你所需的——慰藉——运载——与协助。’请以你自己的名义签署，缮五份。”
主教叹念着他的打字机，勤奋地用一手棱角歪斜、几不可辨的字写下。城里华灯次第亮起。教宗点起蜡烛，拉上帷幔，卷了一支烟。随后祂走回桌边坐下，看着那几页手稿——端详着自己食指上那枚硕大的戒指。祂对自己笑了笑，取过烛芯和一支封蜡；在六张纸的末端盖出“小彼得乘舟”的渔人印。
“请寄往，”祂接着说，“北不列颠圣高夫堂可敬的乔治·森普希尔：斯坦福郡奥克希思可敬的詹姆斯·斯特林：苏塞克斯郡肖勒姆可敬的乔治·莱顿：沃里克郡威尔顿可敬的杰拉尔德·怀特黑德：艾尔郡邓特林可敬的罗伯特·卡维尔：还有——对了，你知道吗，十八年前，他是全学院里脸最美、灵魂最美、嗓音也最要命地美的男孩——第六封寄给珀西·范·克里斯坦，纽约麦迪逊大道2023号。”
塔拉克林博士封信封的当儿，教宗亲笔在一张纸上写下（同样加了封印）：

> 教宗哈德良七世，致爱子法兰西斯·塔拉克林，卡尔勒昂主教。
> 兹定立尔为枢机，并予核准：然尔当缄默此事，以待相宜之时。
> 发自罗马，盖渔人指环。吾等任教宗元年，五月初九日。

===Original===

“Now please come and kneel here,” He said.
The bishop looked an inquiry: but he came round the table, and kneeled before the Pope, Who addressed him in these words:﻿—
“Well-beloved son, Francis Talacryn, Bishop of Caerleon, We appoint thee to, and confirm thee in, the cardinalature. But thou shalt not disclose the fact until the proper time.”
So saying, He lightly pinched-together the bishop’s lips, putting the breve into his hand.
“Silence,” the Pontiff continued. “Now will you yourself go to San Silvestro﻿—not to the post-office here﻿—and stamp and post those letters. One thing more. There will be no hitch tomorrow? Right. Then, after leaving San Silvestro, will you find Prince Pilastro and Prince Orso, and tell them﻿—We certainly shall have the support of these nine? Good.﻿—Well, quite informally let those princes (as Princes-Assistant at the Pontifical Throne) know of Our insuing incoronation. When you have named that to Prince Pilastro, say, also informally, that the Supreme Pontiff wishes the Syndic of Rome to know that, when He has received the crowns, He intends to go to Lateran to take possession of His episcopal see. No. There is to be no fuss. We will go as simply as possible and on foot. Will you always be quite near? We name you trainbearer; and will make your office a sinecure. God bless you. *Da b’och a dibechod.*”
Hadrian remained standing at the antechamber-door, watching the bishop’s big figure disappear along the corridor. He thought it a pity that a tendency to corpulency was not checked by healthy physical exercise. A detachment of the Swiss Guard stood armed and motionless at regular intervals. “For me,” was His plebeian thought. A small man appeared, bowing. He had a servile air. Hadrian’s second glance recognized him. “Is there an apartment on the top storey above this?” He inquired.
“But yes, Holiness, a large apartment of smaller rooms not having the altitude of these.”
“You will cause them to be emptied by noon tomorrow. Now you can go to bed. Please take care that no one comes inside this door until the morning.”
The Pope closed the door: and returned through the antechambers and the throne-room to the table where He had been working. He sat on the edge of the table for about an hour, swinging a leg, thinking, and sipping milk. Then He took a candle, and went into a dressing-room with huge oak clothespresses.

===Chinese===

“现在请过来跪在这里，”祂说。
主教投去疑问的一眼：但还是绕过桌来，跪在教宗面前。教宗对他说了这样一番话：——
“爱子法兰西斯·塔拉克林，卡尔勒昂主教，吾等任命你进入枢机之位，并予核准。但在相宜的时辰到来之前，你不得泄露此事。”
说罢，祂轻轻捏拢主教的双唇，把诏书放进他手里。
“缄默，”教宗接着说。“现在请阁下亲自去圣西尔维斯特罗——不要去本地的邮局——把这些信贴票寄出。还有一件事。明天不会有变故吧？好。那么，出了圣西尔维斯特罗，请去找皮拉斯特罗亲王和奥尔索亲王，告诉他们——这九位吾等肯定可以倚仗了？好。——那么，请以完全非正式的方式，让那两位亲王（以宗座御前襄礼亲王的身份）知道吾等即将举行的加冕。对皮拉斯特罗亲王讲完这件事后，也顺带非正式地说一声：至高教宗愿罗马执政官知道，祂受冠之后，拟赴拉特兰，接管祂的主教座。不。不铺张。我们尽可能简朴地、徒步前往。阁下可愿始终随侍左右？吾等任你为袍摆司；这份差事将是有名无实的清职。上帝降福你。*Da b'och a dibechod.*（巴斯克语：白日与黑夜。）”
哈德良立在前厅门口，望着主教高大的身影消失在走廊尽头。祂想，可惜纵胖之势没有用健康的体育锻炼加以遏制。一队瑞士卫兵持枪肃立，间隔匀称。“为我而设，”是祂那平民气的念头。一个小个子男人出现，鞠躬。一脸奴相。哈德良第二眼认出了他。“这上面顶层有套房吗？”祂问。
“有的，圣下，一整套小间，不及这些层高。”
“你让他们在明天正午前腾空。现在你可以去睡了。请留心，天亮之前任何人不得走进这扇门。”
教宗关上门：穿过前厅和觐见厅，回到方才办公的桌前。他在桌沿上坐了约莫一个钟头，晃着一条腿，想事，呷牛奶。随后祂端起一支蜡烛，走进一间立着巨大橡木衣橱的更衣室。

===Original===

Opening their doors, He looked for a cloak among piles and festoons of new clothes. There were several of crimson velvet. After vainly searching for something plain, He put on one of these and proceeded to the outer door, taking a breviary from the table on the way. Out in the corridor, He signed to the nearest guard. The black-red-yellow-and-steel figure came and kneeled.
“Do you know the way into St. Peter’s?” the Pope said.
“But yes, Most Holy Father.”
“Procure what keys are necessary and conduct Us thither, son.”
“But securely, Most Holy Father.”
The Swiss went on before. Hadrian followed, feeling annoyed by the salutes with which He was received along the way. He had been so long unnoted that notice irritated and abashed Him. Life would be unbearable if trumpets and quaint halberds greeted every movement. He had not the stolidity of born personages. Presently, He threw back His cloak and kept head and hand raised in a gesture which petrified. They passed through innumerable passages and descended stairs, emerging in a chapel where lights burned about a tabernacle of gilded bronze and lapis lazuli. Here He paused, while His escort unlocked the gates of the screen. Once through that, He sent-back the guard to his station: but He Himself went-on into the vast obscurity of the basilica. He walked very slowly: it was as though His eyes were wrapped in clear black velvet, so intense and so immense was the darkness. Then, very far away to the right, He saw as it were a coronal of dim stars glimmering﻿—on the floor, they seemed to be. He was in the mighty nave; and the stars were the ever-burning lamps surrounding the Confession. He slowly approached them. As He passed within them, He took one from its golden branch and descended the marble steps. Here, He spread the cloak on the floor; placed the lamp beside it: and fell to prayer. Outside, in the City and the World, men played, or worked, or sinned, or slept. Inside at the very tomb of the Apostle the Apostle prayed.
At midnight, bolts of great doors clanged, and fell. A cool air crept in. Subsacristans set up iron candlesticks, huge, antique, here and there upon the marmoreal pavement. The burning torch of each made a little oasis of light in the immeasurable gloom.

===Chinese===

祂打开橱门，在一堆堆、一挂挂的新衣里找一件斗篷。绯红天鹅绒的有好几件。素净的找不着，祂便披上其中一件，往外门走去，顺手从桌上拿了日课经。走廊里，祂朝最近的卫兵一示意。那黑红黄三色配钢盔的身影走过来跪下。
“你认得进圣彼得的路？”教宗说。
“认得，至圣的圣父。”
“去把必要的钥匙弄来，引吾等前往，孩子。”
“包管稳妥，至圣的圣父。”
瑞士人前头引路。哈德良随后，一路所受的敬礼叫祂心烦。祂太久无人注目，如今受人注目反叫祂气恼、窘迫。倘若每个动作都要迎来号角和古戟，日子就没法过了。祂没有天生贵人的那种木然。过了一会儿，祂把斗篷向后一撩，头和手一直高举着，一个把人钉住的手势。他们穿过数不尽的廊道，下了楼梯，进到一座小堂：镀金青铜与青金石的圣体柜四周燃着灯。祂在这里停步，随行的卫兵去开屏门的锁。一过屏门，祂便遣卫兵回哨：自己继续走进大殿浩瀚的幽暗里。祂走得很慢：黑暗那样浓、那样广，仿佛祂的双眼蒙上了清晰的黑天鹅绒。随后，极远的右方，祂看见仿佛一圈幽暗的星光在闪烁——在地上，看着像。祂已置身巍峨的中殿；那星光，是环着告解墓永远燃着的灯。祂缓缓走近。走进灯圈时，祂从金枝上取下一盏，步下大理石阶。在这里，祂把斗篷铺在地上：把灯放在旁边：俯首祈祷。外面，在城市与世界，人们玩耍，或劳作，或犯罪，或酣睡。里面，在宗徒的墓前，宗徒在祈祷。
午夜，巨门的门闩哐啷落锁。一股凉气渗进来。副堂司务在斑岩似的地面上四处立起铁烛台，高大，古旧。每一支火炬的烈焰，都在无边的幽暗里燃出一小片光的绿洲。

===Original===

From far away, a slim white form which carried a crimson cloak swiftly came, shedding benedictions on the startled beholders; and disappeared in the chapel of the Sacrament.
On returning to His apartment, Hadrian went straight to bed, invoking the souls in purgatory to awaken Him at six o’clock. He slept instantly and well.
At seven o’clock He had paid His debt with the De Profundis; and was dressed and waiting in the throne-room. Entered to Him a dozen cardinals, two by two. Opening their ranks, they disclosed the Cardinal-Prior-Priest solemnly ostending the image of a cock in silver-gilt. Hadrian stood on the steps of the throne, still, erect, vivid. He seemed so brimming over with restrained energy that He resembled a white flame. Not a sound was uttered. In silence they came; and they went away in silence. When the Pontiff was alone again, He strode and stopped in the middle of the floor.
“No, Lord, I never will deny Thee﻿—never!” He exclaimed with tremendous emphasis. “But keep me and teach me and govern me, that I may govern and teach and keep Thy Flock, O Thou Shepherd of the people.”
When the Bishop of Caerleon conveyed the extraordinary news to the Syndic of Rome, Prince Pilastro at once inquired what arrangements were made.
“No arrangements are made.”
“But look here,” said Marcantonio, who affected English brusqueness, “of course we are very happy that the Holy Father should come among us: but, you know, we are bound by our own guarantees to give Him all the honours of a sovereign-regnant. We shall be shamed in the eyes of Europe if we omit those. What I mean by that is this is a state-progress; and we shall have to turn out the troops, and stop the traffic and line the streets﻿—”
“I don’t think His Holiness expects you to do all that, Prince. I’m not speaking officially; and I’m not bringing you an official request for anything of the kind which you name. The Holy Father says He is going quite simply﻿—on foot, in fact.”
“Now I should just like to know what the devil (if Your Splendour will excuse the French) that means.”
“Perhaps His Holiness thinks that the movement of the *sedia gestatoria*, or of a litter, will make Him sick. It did with Leo, you know.”
“What’s the matter with a white mule?”
“I happen to know that He cannot ride.”
“Peuh! No sportsman, then! And yet He’s English?”
“Yes: but not the kind of sportsman you mean, Prince.”

===Chinese===

远远地，一个披着绯红斗篷的细白身影疾步而来，向惊愕的目击者频洒降福；隐入圣体小堂。
回到套房，哈德良径直上床，求炼狱众魂六点钟唤醒祂。祂霎时睡去，睡得极沉。
七点，祂以《自深渊》还清了债；已整装在觐见厅等候。十几位红衣主教鱼贯而入，两人一排。队列分开，露出首席司铎红衣主教，郑重托举着一尊镀金银鸡像。哈德良立在御座台阶上，静，直，神采焕然。祂满蓄着克制的精力，仿佛一团白色的火焰。没有一点声息。他们无声地来；又无声地去。教宗再度独处时，祂大步走到厅堂正中站定。
“不，主，我永不会否认祢——永不！”祂斩钉截铁地呼喊。“只求祢保佑我、教导我、统御我，好叫我统御、教导、保佑祢的羊群，牧养万民者啊。”
卡尔勒昂主教把这惊人的消息传给罗马执政官时，皮拉斯特罗亲王头一句便问：作何安排。
“没有安排。”
“可是听着，”马克安东尼奥说——他喜欢学英国式的直捅捅，“圣父肯莅临我们中间，我们自然高兴：可您知道，我们受自家承诺的约束，须以在位君王的全部仪礼相待。若缺了这些，我们在全欧洲面前就丢了脸。我的意思是：这是一趟国事巡游；我们得出动军队，断绝交通，沿街列队——”
“我想圣下并不指望您做这一切，亲王。我不是以官方身份说话；也不是向您转达任何这一类的官方请求。圣父说祂要走得极简单——实际上是步行。”
“那我倒真想知道，这他妈（阁下恕我用句法语）算什么意思。”
“也许圣下觉得坐肩舆或轿子会晕。利奥就晕过，您知道。”
“牵一匹白骡子不行吗？”
“我碰巧知道祂不会骑马。”
“呸！那算什么运动家！可祂居然是英国人？”
“是英国人：但不是您说的那种运动家，亲王。”

===Original===

“Well: what does He want me to do?”
“Let’s say that I am sent to warn you of His intention, in order that you may arrest Him for disturbing the traffic, if you choose.”
“Of course we shan’t do that.”
“No: of course you won’t. That’s only my way of putting it. I think He really means to advise you beforehand, so that it can never be said that He played you a trick, took you unawares, stole a march on you, so to speak.”
“I see. Well, this is one of the amazing things which you English do as a matter of course. It’s either frantic madness, or﻿—Will His Holiness go in any sort of state?”
“I think not. You see time is short; and (between ourselves) I’m not at all sure that we’re all of one mind over there.”
“By rights, you know, I ought to walk with Orso, just before the ambassadors. Does Orso know about this walking business?”
“No. Only of the incoronation.”
“That means that there will be no formal procession. It is well. You see, as Pilastro, I walk with Orso in the Pope’s progress: while, as Syndic of Rome, I ought to walk at the head of the pontifical pages who precede His Blessedness. I can’t do both, can I? Well, I request Your Splendour to convey my respects to our Holy Father; and to say that Prince Pilastro will assist at the throne during the incoronation, and the Syndic of Rome will go before the Pope to Lateran.”
“You will not take the chance of coming to blows with Prince Orso on the question of precedence then?” joked the bishop.
“But no. During the incoronation I shall secure the right hand; and the Pope will be between us. Afterward, no question of precedence will arise, because Orso may or may not join in this promenade to Lateran; and in each case the Syndic will have the more honourable position. I may not be the rose: but at least I shall be near the Rose﻿—a great deal nearer than Orso,” punned the versatile Marcantonio.
At eight in the morning, Hadrian descended to St. Peter’s. Miscellaneous multitudes paved the spaces with tumultuous eyes. He came down in ruddy vesture, gleaming with rubies and garnets and carbuncles like a fire borne high above the crowd, slowly, deliberately, dropping benedictions. His English phlegm was much admired. They roared at Him, “Long live the Pope-King.” Instantly He stopped His bearers; and the very air of Him struck sudden silence.

===Chinese===

“那么：祂要我做什么？”
“这么说吧，我是奉命预告您祂的意图，好让您把祂当扰乱交通罪逮捕——如果您愿意的话。”
“我们当然不会那么干。”
“是：当然不会。那只是我的说法。我想祂的用意是事先知会您，免得日后有人说祂对您使了花招、打您个措手不及，这么说吧，抢了您的先。”
“明白了。唉，这就是你们英国人当作家常便饭的奇事之一。不是疯狂透顶，就是——圣下会不会带任何仪仗？”
“我想不会。您看时间紧；而且（这话只你我之间）我一点也拿不准我们那边是否全体一条心。”
“照理说，我该同奥尔索一起走，就在大使们前面。奥尔索知道这步行的事吗？”
“不知道。只知道加冕。”
“那就是说不会有正式的巡游队列了。这样倒好。您瞧，作为皮拉斯特罗，我在教宗巡游时同奥尔索并走：而作为罗马执政官，我又该走在教宗陛下的仪仗童子之前。两样我不能兼顾，对不对？好吧，请阁下代我向圣父致敬；并转告：加冕礼上皮拉斯特罗亲王将在御前襄礼，而罗马执政官将为教宗前导，同赴拉特兰。”
“那么您不想抓住机会，同奥尔索亲王为位次打一架了？”主教打趣。
“才不呢。加冕时我占右手；教宗在我们中间。过后，位次问题不会再有，因为奥尔索去不去拉特兰散步未定；而无论哪一种情形，执政官都占更尊的位置。我未必是玫瑰：可至少我离玫瑰近——比奥尔索近得多，”多才多艺的马克安东尼奥一语双关。
早晨八点，哈德良下到圣彼得。杂沓的人群以骚动的眼睛铺满了空场。祂穿着赤红的礼袍下来，红宝石、石榴石、红玉髓烁烁生光，像一簇高擎在人海之上的火，缓缓地、从容地洒着降福。祂那份英国式的冷峻大受赞赏。人们朝祂吼：“教宗王万岁。”祂即刻叫抬舆停住；祂周身的气势霎时压出一片寂静。

===Original===

People stared, and forgot to shout: the wave of acclamation ebbed in the great nave and transepts. He moved onward, sitting erect, godlike, with a frozen mien prohibiting personal homage. Mitred and enthroned, He was the servant of those who would serve Him: that was the import of His demeanour. A child acolyth of the lowest rank held up before him a salver containing flax: set it on fire; and shrilled,
“Behold most Holy Father, how that the glory of this world passeth away.”
His features showed no emotion. He well knew all about that. He was accepting, even insisting on, the observance of all rites to consolidate Him in the Supreme Pontificature: not that He cared for them, but that He might be free to act. It was not the glory of the world which He craved: but the combat, the combat﻿—because one rests so much more sweetly after strife.
Slowly, and with all the unspeakable solemnity accumulated during centuries, the mass was sung. The Apostle elevated the Host to the four quarters of the globe. Cardinals ruffled like huge flamingoes round Him. He always was white and still. At the end, the Cardinal-Archpriest of St. Peter’s brought Him a damask purse containing twenty-five gold coins, honorarium for a mass well-sung. He bestowed it on della Volta and Sega, who had intoned the Gospel in Greek and Latin; and they passed it to their trainbearers. Down the nave, He went again toward the great porch. Out of the crowd a voice cried “*Christus regnat*.” As He sat enthroned amid the surging peoples, Macca crowned Him, saying,
“Receive this tiara adorned with three crowns, and know Thyself to be the Ruler of the World, the Father of Princes and Kings, the earthly Vicar of Jesus Christ our Saviour.”
Hadrian understood the formula in no metaphorical, but in the plain and literal, sense of the words. He neither minimised nor magnified their significance. He had an opportunity which was entirely grateful to Him. He was Ruler, Father, Vicar. And He was altogether unafraid. He stood up, and blessed the City and the World.
In the Xystine Chapel, they relieved Him of the pontifical regalia, and the voluminous far-flowing petticoat of white taffetas, which is so sumptuous to the eye of the beholden and so ridiculously cumbersome to the legs of the wearer; and He ate some apples while Orezzo, on behalf of the Sacred College, recited time-honoured compliments.

===Chinese===

人们瞪着，忘了喊：欢呼的浪潮在中殿和耳堂里退了下去。祂继续前行，端坐如神，一脸凛然，禁止任何私人性质的致敬。戴冠升座，祂是那些愿侍奉祂之人的仆人：这是祂仪态的全部含义。一名品级最低的儿童辅祭在他面前托起一只盛着亚麻的银盘：点火烧了；尖声唱道：
“看哪，至圣的圣父，今世的光荣就是这样逝去的。”
祂面容不为所动。这个祂早就全知道了。祂接受、甚至坚持行全一切仪节，为的是把祂在至高教宗之位上安牢：并非祂看重仪节，而是祂要好放手行事。祂渴求的不是今世的光荣：而是战斗，战斗——因为斗争之后，歇息才格外甘甜。
弥撒缓缓地唱起来，带着几个世纪积攒下的全部无可言说的庄严。宗徒把圣体举向大地的四方。红衣主教们像一群巨大的火烈鸟在祂四周翻羽。祂始终白而静。礼毕，圣彼得首席司铎红衣主教奉上一只锦缎钱袋，内装二十五枚金币——一台唱得圆满的弥撒的谢仪。祂转赐给德拉沃尔塔和塞加——他们分别以希腊文和拉丁文唱了福音；两人又传给了各自的袍摆司。祂沿着中殿，再次走向大门廊。人群中一个声音高呼“*Christus regnat*（基督为王）”。祂端坐于人海的涌动之中，马卡为祂加冠，诵道：
“请接受这顶三重冠冕，并自知你是世界的统治者、诸王与诸侯之父、我等救主耶稣基督在世的代理人。”
哈德良对这段套语的理解，不用比喻义，而用字面而直白的本义。祂既不贬损也不夸大这些字句的分量。祂得到了一个全然称心的机会。祂是统治者、是父、是代理人。而祂全然无惧。祂站起身，降福了城市与世界。
在西斯廷小堂，人们卸下祂的教宗礼服，卸下那条白塔夫绸的、层层叠叠、远远流曳的衬裙——它在观者眼里那样华美，在穿着者的腿上又那样可笑地累赘；祂吃着苹果，听奥雷佐代表枢机团诵那些久经岁月的颂词。

===Original===

“Lord Cardinals,” said Hadrian, “We thank you for your service: and We invite those of you who are able and willing to attend Us, now, when We go to take possession of Our episcopal see.”
He moved towards the door. The short train of His cassock trailed behind Him, and the Bishop of Caerleon stooped to it.
Ragna had something to howl.
“Holiness, this is suicide for You and murder for us. The City is full of Jews and Freemasons; and we shall most assuredly be stabbed, or shot, or shattered to pieces with bombs, or drenched with vitriol﻿—”
“The Church wants a martyr badly. Your Eminency is invited, not commanded.”
Berstein muttered to Vivole, in a scandalized tone, that the Pope was courting popularity. Pepato, with a note of admiration, commented on the mad English. Word of the invitation rushed on ahead. Of the crowd of officials, many began to arrange themselves in a certain order: others had pressing calls elsewhere. Masters-of-ceremonies, wracking their brains for long forgotten details, flew hither and thither with instructions and pushes. Poor old Grani sat down in a recess; and wept to think that there was no time to get out the white gennets annually presented by the King of Spain. Hadrian came on slowly, chatting with Caerleon, giving people a chance of making up their minds. When He emerged from the colonnade in the Square of St. Peter’s, the Syndic of Rome fell into the ranks just before the Pope; and a royal escort of the Praetorian Guard surrounded Him. Hadrian stopped; and beckoned Prince Pilastro.
“Sir Syndic, are We free?” He mewed.
“But free, Holy Father.”
“Let your soldiers precede and not surround Us; and let no one come within ten paces of Us. We go by Via Giulia and Monte Celio.”
The squadron moved to the head of the line. The Pope took His train from the Bishop: threw it over His left arm: and came-on alone. Acting as though the ideal were real, He made it real. If Jews and Freemasons would slay Him, well and good: it was part of the day’s work, no doubt. He was by no means anxious to be martyred; and He sincerely hoped that, if it should come to Him, it would not be very painful or distorting. But, as it was His Own affair, a piece of the part He was fulfilling, He displayed Himself alone. Ten paces before Him went Prince Pilastro, looking back from time to time.

===Chinese===

“主教大人们，”哈德良说，“吾等谢过诸位的辛劳：吾等并邀请诸位中能够且愿意者，即刻随吾等前往，去接管吾等的主教座堂。”
祂向门口走去。身上那件短后摆的教宗袍曳在身后，卡尔勒昂主教俯身替祂提着。
拉尼亚又有得嚎了。
“圣父，这对您是自杀，对我们是谋杀。城里满是犹太人和共济会员；我们准会被捅、被枪、被炸弹炸得粉碎、或被泼一身镪水——”
“教会正缺一位殉道者，急需。主教大人是被邀请，不是被命令。”
伯恩斯坦愤然向维沃莱咕哝，说教宗在讨好民心。佩帕托则带着几分赞叹，评点这英格兰式的疯劲。邀请的话头飞跑在前。随行官员里，许多开始自排次序：另一些则在别处有了要紧的约。司仪们翻捡久已淡忘的细节，拿着指令和推搡满场飞。可怜的老格拉尼坐进一处壁龛，哭着想：来不及牵出西班牙国王每年进献的白骡子了。哈德良慢慢地往前走，同卡尔勒昂聊着，给人留出拿定主意的时间。当祂走出柱廊、踏上圣彼得广场时，罗马执政官插进队列、恰在教宗之前；一支禁卫军的王家卫队围拢上来。哈德良停住；朝皮拉斯特罗亲王一招手。
“执政官先生，我们自由吗？”祂细声细气地问。
“自由的，圣父。”
“让你的士兵走在前面，不要围着吾等；任何人不得靠近吾等十步之内。我们走朱利亚大道和切利奥山。”
骑兵队移到队伍前头。教宗从主教手里接过自己的袍摆：搭上左臂：独自前行。祂装作理想已然成真，于是理想成真。犹太人和共济会员若要杀祂，也好：那想必是这一天活计的一部分。祂绝无求殉道之心；祂诚心希望，倘若真轮到祂，别太疼、别太难看。可既然这是祂自己的事、祂所扮演的角色的一块，祂便独自亮身。皮拉斯特罗亲王在祂前头十步走，不时回头张望。

===Original===

Ten paces behind Him came the bishop, ruddy and strong in white and purple, wondering. The vermilion nine followed in a compact phalanx, very venerable and grand; and, after a great deal of bustle and noise, seventeen other cardinals added their magnificence. A motley of patriarchs, archbishops, bishops, prelates, and pontifical guards closed the rear.
A tremendous shout greeted Hadrian’s first appearance in the square. It was quite incoherent: for the real significance of the pageant was not immediately realized. No Pope had set His foot in Rome since 1870: but here undoubtedly was the Pope, with a gentle inflexible face﻿—a lonely white figure Whose left hand lay on the little cross on His breast, Whose right hand gravely scattered the same sign. This crowd was not the even human parallels which authority is wont to describe on streets when the Great go by. It was a concurrence from sideways coalescing with loafers and ordinary passersby, suddenly dipping its knees, gazing, panting, and emitting howls of delirious onomatopes. Cabs and carts swept to the side of the road; and the drivers kneeled on the boxes. Here and there, some dowdy alien said “What mockery” and patronizingly explained that the Salvation Army did these things much more properly. Here and there, some sour sorry incapable stood spitting in praise of secret societies. Here and there some godless worldling scoffed in an undertone. But Hadrian went-on, walking at that deceptive pace of His, which seemed so leisurely and was so swift. His movements resembled the running of a perfectly-geared machine: they had the smooth and forceful grace of the athlete whose muscles are supple and strong: even the occasional impulse had no jerkiness. It was the manner with which He disguised His natural timidity. He sometimes glanced from side to side. Once He smiled at a bare-legged rascalt of brown boys who kneeled by one of Bernini’s angels on the parapet of the bridge. He adored children, although He was so desperately afraid of them. Going up the hill by the Church of Sts. John and Paul, a little girl dabbed an indescribable rag on her head: rushed into the road, dashing primroses; and remained transfixed by her own audacity. He led her by the hand to her mother; and blessed them both. All His life long He had yearned to be giving.

===Chinese===

祂身后十步，跟着主教，一身白紫，红光满面而健壮，满心纳罕。朱红的那九位结成紧凑的方阵随后，威仪赫赫；闹腾了一番之后，另有十七位红衣主教也来增其辉煌。主教长、总主教、主教、神长与教廷卫队杂沓殿后。
哈德良初现广场，一声惊天动地的欢呼迎来。欢呼全然不成句：因为这一场巡游的真义一时无人领会。自1870年以来，没有教宗把脚踏进过罗马：可这分明是教宗，一张温和而不容置疑的脸——一个孤零零的白色身影，左手按着胸前的小十字，右手庄重地向四方撒着同样的圣号。这群人不是权威们描述大王过街时惯说的那种齐整人墙。这是几股人流从横里合拢，同闲汉与寻常路人搅作一处，骤然屈膝，瞪着眼，喘着气，迸出狂喜的拟声喊叫。马车板车纷纷扫向路边；赶车的跪在车座上。东一处，哪个邋遢的外邦人说“多滑稽”，还屈尊指点：救世军办这些事体面得多。东一处，哪个酸溜溜的废物站在那儿吐唾沫，赞秘密社团。东一处，哪个不敬神的老油子低声嘲骂。可哈德良一路走去，迈着祂那种骗人的步子——看着那样从容，实则那样迅疾。祂的动作像一台啮合完满的机器：有着运动员式的、筋肉柔韧有力才有的流畅遒劲的优雅：连偶发的冲刺也不带一点磕绊。那是祂用来遮掩天生腼腆的做派。祂不时左右张望。有一次，祂朝桥栏边贝尼尼天使旁跪着的一帮光腿棕皮小淘气笑了笑。祂酷爱孩子，尽管祂怕他们怕得要死。上圣若望保禄堂那段坡时，一个小丫头把一块没法形容的破布顶在头上：冲进路中，抛着樱草花：随即被自己的大胆吓僵在原地。祂牵起她的手，送回到她母亲身边；降福了她们两个。祂这一辈子，一直渴望着有所给予。

===Original===

Now, under any circumstances, He always had something to give, ten words and a gesture; and people seemed so thankful for it. He was glad.
In the porch of the Mother and Mistress of All Churches in the City and the World, He sat on the low throne while canons made shift to intone, “He raiseth up the poor out of the dust, and lifteth the needy out of the dunghill; that He may set Him with the Princes, even with the princes of His people.” They gave Him gold and silver keys. They attended Him to the throne of precious marbles in the centre of the apse. They intoned Te Deum. Ascending to the lodge of benediction, He blessed the mobile vulgar in the Square of St. John; and anon returned in the way by which He came, Bishop of Rome in act and deed, and Supreme Pontiff.

===Chinese===

如今，无论何时何地，祂总有东西可给，十个字加一个手势；而人们领受时似乎那样感激。祂高兴。
在城市与世界诸圣堂之母、之师的门廊里，祂坐上低座，众咏礼司祭勉强启唱：“祂从尘埃里提拔贫寒人，从粪堆中抬举穷乏人；使祂与王子同坐，与百姓的王子同坐。”他们呈给祂金银钥匙。他们伴祂到半圆殿中央那座宝理石御座。他们唱起《感恩颂》。祂登上降福阁，降福圣若望广场上熙攘的众生；随即循原路而返——名实相副的罗马主教，至高的教宗。

## IX / 九
===Original===

Hadrian knew that He was becoming confirmed in His pose of director. Not that He was inflated by His exaltation to the apostolature. He was conscious that people, except a few enthusiasts, were become indifferent to religion. He knew the danger of indifference to be so great that it was no time to strain at gnats. He could not trouble about rats in the ship’s hold while the torpedo was approaching. He was thought to share the abominable heresy of Tolstoy, whose works He never would touch with tongs. He saw that most men lived in mist; and preferred it: that most men durst not see clearly, because their business and their social interest would not stand it. He was not absolutely certain that He Himself could see the remedy: but He was certain that blindness was no remedy. So He put forth the evangelic counsels for obedience. “Strip; and obey those” appeared to be sufficient for the present; and He would not fiddle-faddle with human doctrines or empirical experiments. He had the big vision, the seeing eye, the hearing ear, wit, perverseness, daring, and the lonely heart, and the contempt of the world. The effect of His entire freedom of action was to inspire Him physically and mentally with the thrilling vigour of a pentathlete. He had the violent energy of the minute electron in the enormous atom. He felt Himself strong. He knew that His forces were tensely strung; and in their melody He was very glad. Sometimes He caught Himself wondering how long He could maintain the pitch: but from that thought He turned away. It was enough that He was able. He would not spare Himself. The night cometh when no man can work.
“Let it come,” he said to Cardinal Sterling: “but, while day lasts, We work.”
A splendid sentence of Mommsen’s bit into his brain. Caesar ruled as King of Rome for five years and a half﻿ ﻿… ; in the intervals of seven great campaigns, which allowed him to stay not more than fifteen months altogether in the capital of the empire, he regulated the destinies of the world for the present and the future.﻿ ﻿… Precisely because the building was an endless one, the master, as long as he lived, restlessly added stone to stone, with always the same dexterity and always the same elasticity busy at his work, without ever overturning or postponing, just as though there were for him merely today and no tomorrow.

===Chinese===

哈德良知道，自己那副主导者的姿态正在定型。倒不是升上宗座叫祂膨胀。祂心里明白，除寥寥几个热心人外，人们对宗教已经漠然。祂知道漠然之险已大到没功夫计较蚊子大的过了。鱼雷逼近时，祂顾不上船舱里的耗子。人家说祂与托尔斯泰那可憎的异端同流——那人的书祂连火钳都不肯夹。祂看得出，多数人活在雾里：而且宁要雾：多数人不敢看清，因为看清了，他们的营生与交游就撑不住。祂并不绝对确信自己看得见救药：可祂确信，瞎不是救药。于是祂把福音的劝谕当作服从之道端了出来。“剥光自己；服从这些”眼下似乎就够了；祂不愿在人间的教条和经验的试验上磨牙。祂有大视野、能看的眼睛、能听的耳朵、机智、执拗、胆气，还有孤独的心和对世界的轻蔑。行动上的全然自由，使祂身心两头都灌满了五项运动员那种令人战栗的劲道。祂有着巨大原子里那颗微小电子的暴烈能量。祂觉得自己强壮。祂知道自己的力绷得满满的；在它们的旋律里祂满心欢喜。有时祂发觉自己在想：这样的音高还能撑多久：可这念头祂扭头就撵走。能干就够了。祂不会饶过自己。黑夜将至，无人能做工。
“让它来，”祂对斯特林红衣主教说：“可趁白昼还在，我们做工。”
蒙森的一句煌煌之言咬进了祂的脑子。凯撒以罗马之王的名义统治了五年半……；在七大战役的间隙里——这些战役容他在帝国首都统共待不到十五个月——他调度了当世与来世的世界命运。……正因这座营造永无竣工，这位主人只要一息尚存，便不休止地一石一石往上垒，始终同样娴熟、始终同样轻盈地忙于其工，从不推翻、从不延宕，仿佛对他来说只有今天、没有明天。

===Original===

Thus he worked and created as never did any mortal before or after him; and, as a worker and creator, he still, after two thousand years, lives in the memory of the nations﻿—the first, and withal unique, Imperator Caesar.﻿—And Julius, also, had been Pontifex Maximus. Hadrian took a white umbrella for a walk as far as the black-lava fort on the Appian Way.
He considered the horrible condition of France and Russia. It was a menace to the world. Of Russia, He could learn nothing new. Thews and Thought together had abolished authority and gone mad in butchery. The information, which He had obtained from the French Cardinals, was not of a rather useful nature. Elements of emotional sentiment and archaic conventionalism rendered their opinions well nigh worthless. They were tolutiloquent in expressing horror at the impiety of mob-rule which had deprived them of the right to military salutes ordained by the Concordat. They made the blood boil by their heartrending descriptions of holocausts of priests and nuns﻿—earnest heroic enthusiasts absolutely incapable of doing anything really practical in the way of eradicating that demoniality of which they became the victims. Nothing would please Their Eminencies better than to hasten to their distracted native-land, to offer up themselves as martyrs to the devils of their dioceses. They were no cowards﻿—if desire to rush on death be bravery:﻿—but they were picturesque, and dithyrambic﻿—mainly picturesque, with their long hair and their rabats edged with white beads. That would not do as an essential. Out of the mellay of matter laid before Him, the Pontiff extracted certain points. France, qua France, no longer was Christian. The Devil was in power. Christians who were able to cross frontiers, did so. Spain, Italy, Switzerland, Germany, received them. England, America, Japan, blockaded Toulon, Brest, Cherbourg. Their liners tapped the coasts; and carried thousands into freedom. Poverty afflicted the emigrants: those left behind were butchers, or subject to butchery. Dom Jaime de Bourbon having perished, the Pope sent for the Duke of Orleans;﻿—and dismissed him with austere disgust. He subsequently withered away. His Holiness gave audience to a score of the French nobility; and spent some days picking the brains of emigrants fortuitously collected.

===Chinese===

他这样劳作与创造，前无古人、后无来者；而作为劳动者与创造者，两千年后他仍活在万国的记忆里——第一位、也是绝无仅有的那位英白拉多·凯撒。——而尤利乌斯，也当过大祭司。哈德良撑一把白伞散步，一直走到阿庇亚大道那座黑熔岩的堡垒。
祂斟酌法兰西与俄罗斯的惨状。那是对世界的威胁。俄罗斯方面，祂打探不出新东西。膂力与思想联手废了权威、又在屠戮中发了疯。从法国红衣主教们那里得来的情报，用处不大。感伤的情绪与古旧的成见，叫他们的意见几乎一文不值。他们滔滔不绝地控诉暴民夺去了政教协约规定的军礼之权，如何大不敬。他们声泪俱下地描绘神父修女被成批烧献，叫人血压飙升——而自己却全然干不成一件真正切实的事，去根除那害得他们成为祭品的魔鬼之政。叫诸位主教大人最痛快的，莫过于奔回那水深火热的故土，把自己作殉道的祭品献给各自教区的魔鬼。他们绝非懦夫——倘若抢着赴死算勇敢的话：——可他们好做一幅画，好发酒神颂——长发披肩，假领缀着白珠，主要就是好做一幅画。这当不得正事。教宗从摆在面前的这堆乱麻里抽出了几条要点。法兰西，作为法兰西，已不再是基督教的。魔鬼当权。能越境的基督徒都越了境。西班牙、意大利、瑞士、德国收留了他们。英国、美国、日本封锁了土伦、布雷斯特、瑟堡。她们的邮船沿各处海岸接人；成千上万运进了自由。贫困折磨着移民：留下来的，要么是屠夫，要么等着挨屠。堂海梅·德·波旁既死，教宗召奥尔良公爵来见；——带着冷峻的厌恶把他打发走了。此人随后日渐委顿。教宗又接见了二十来位法国贵族；花了几天工夫，向碰巧聚在罗马的移民们掏取见识。

===Original===

Then, He again convened the French cardinals, and declared the pontifical will. They all were deposed from their episcopal sees, and nominated Apostolic Missionaries. Their charge was the cure, first of the bodies, second of the souls, of Frenchmen everywhere. The Cardinal-Missionary of Paris would go to London with the Cardinal-Archbishop of Pimlico, having powers to draw one million sterling from the pontifical treasure in the Bank of England: which sum, in halves, was to be the nucleus of two funds, an English and a German, for French Christians in their need. Each cardinal-missionary also received a breve authorizing him, and persons delegated by him, to collect money in every Christian country for the said funds. It was not to be a clerical charity. The Lord Mayor of London and the German Emperor were willing to administer it, each independently. Further Their Eminencies were to use their own discretion about adventuring themselves in the diabolical dominion. If they best could serve God there, then in God’s Name, and with God’s Vicegerent’s benediction, let them go: but they most straitly were bidden to keep one only object before them, viz. the service of God through the relief and comfort of His servants. Nothing was to prevent them in that.
The world began to concentrate the corner of its eye on Hadrian. Holland and Belgium fell into the arms of anarchical France. The vigorous bold brilliant young Sultan Ismail, having failed to win Morocco to his Pan-Islamic scheme, was intriguing for an alliance with the other great Muhammedan power, England. His Majesty’s murdered predecessor, by the aid of Germany, had formed an army of a million and a half, full of fanatical valour and the wonderful natural adaptability of the Turk, the rawest recruit of which had a greater fighting-value than was possessed by the conscripts of any other nation. This force was available for active service at fifteen minutes’ notice. The Turkish alliance was worth anyone’s while; and was coveted. Germany had trained the Ottoman squadrons: but was not to profit thereby. Teutonic stolidity had been outwitted by the wily Oriental. Islam could only and only would mate with Islam﻿—as might have been foreseen. The rest of the continent of Europe ringed frontiers under arms. Each nation feared the other; and all feared France and Russia.

===Chinese===

随后，祂再度召集法国红衣主教，宣示了宗座的意志。他们全被免去主教教区，改任宗座传教士。其职分是：医治普天下法国人的身体为先，灵魂为次。巴黎的传教红衣主教将偕平利科总主教同赴伦敦，有权从英格兰银行的宗座库中支取一百万镑：此款对半拆开，做两笔基金的母金，一英一德，济法国基督徒之急。每位传教红衣主教还领到一道诏书，授权他本人及其所委之人，在每一基督教国家为上述基金募捐。这不当是教会的慈善。伦敦市长与德皇都愿意各自独立经理其事。此外，诸位置身魔域与否，悉听己便。他们若在那边最能事奉上帝，那就奉上帝的名、领上帝代理人的降福，去：只是严令他们把唯一一个目标摆在眼前，即：借抚慰与救济上帝的仆人来事奉上帝。任何事不得拦阻这一点。
世界开始把眼角聚向哈德良。荷兰与比利时落进了无政府的法兰西怀抱。年轻干练、光彩照人的伊斯梅尔苏丹，拉摩洛哥入泛伊斯兰阵线未成，正谋划与另一大穆斯林强权——英格兰——结盟。陛下的那位前任遇害之后，德国帮着练出了一支一百五十万人的大军，满是狂热的勇武和土耳其人天生的惊人适应力，其中最生的新兵，战斗力也胜过任何别国的征兵。这支力量十五分钟内即可投入现役。土耳其这个盟友谁都值得交；人人眼热。德国练出了奥斯曼的舰队：却不因此得利。条顿的木愣叫狡黠的东方人耍了。伊斯兰只肯、也只会同伊斯兰配偶——这本可预见。欧洲大陆其余各国沿边境布防。各国怕各国；而各国都怕法兰西和俄罗斯。

===Original===

Hadrian watched the diplomatic processes with interest. He knew that England was quite capable of taking care of Herself, with or without the Mussulman. He grasped the theory that Muhammedanism, arising six hundred years after Christ, justified the Wisdom of God in Judaism, proving that the Oriental mind could bear nothing more perfect; and He conceived a sort of sympathy with Islam. His conversations with ambassadors became known in courts, (the King of Prussia’s legate wrote amazing things to the German Emperor:) from courts, descriptions of opinions, tastes, habits, descended until they were discussed in clubs and miscellaneous congeries. Hadrian’s custom of walking about unattended, looking-at the excavations in the Forum, visiting the sick in hospitals, sensuously delighting Himself with the glories of sunset seen from the Pincian Hill, were the themes of common conversation. And when, one evening, He got-in a left hander (from the shoulder) on a socialist, who spat at Him in Borgo Nuovo; and then, (on the filthy beast’s bursting into tears and collapsing with the effects of the blow upon semi-starvation), pressing upon him His pectoral cross and chain, His gold spectacles, and all the coins left in His pocket after a couple of hours in Rome﻿—then the English race began to find the Pope observable; and English newspapers started columns called “Rome Day by Day.” How the special correspondents spread themselves! She of the *Pall Mall Gazette* got the usual exclusive information of the Borgo Nuovo affair; and split nine infinitives in describing the myopic Pontiff narrowing His eyes to slits, groping His way along the colonnades with His fainting assailant; His passionate denunciation of the farce of organized charity, which had let a man become so degraded; His agitation until Cardinal Carvale came running with His spare pair of spectacles; His strangely pathetic thankfulness for the gift of sight which they afforded; His anguish at the defilement of His garment; and His tender invitation to the starving socialist to be His guest in Vatican. All this suited the English temper to a T﻿—being English. But there was created a profound and perdurable impression. The King of Prussia’s legate wrote more amazing things to the German Emperor.

===Chinese===

哈德良饶有兴味地注视着外交的进程。祂知道，英格兰完全照管得了自己，有没有那个穆斯林都一样。祂领会这套理论：穆罕默德之教兴于基督后六百年，恰恰证成了上帝在犹太教中的智慧，证明东方的心智承受不起更完备的东西；于是祂对伊斯兰生出几分同情。祂同各国大使的谈话传进了各宫廷（普鲁士王的公使给德皇写了些骇人的东西：），从宫廷往下，关于祂的见解、趣味、习惯的描绘一路流布，直至俱乐部和各色人堆里议论纷纷。哈德良惯于独自步行，看看古罗马广场的发掘，去医院探望病人，从苹丘上感官地受用落日的辉煌——这些都成了街谈巷议的题目。等到某天晚上，祂在新堡街朝一个啐祂的社会主义者回敬了一记左勾拳（从肩上发力）；随后（那脏货眼泪迸涌、被那记拳打在半饥饿的身子上当场瘫倒），祂把自己的胸前十字和链子、金丝眼镜、以及祂在罗马城里逛了两个钟头后兜里剩下的全部硬币，一股脑塞给了他——英国人种这才开始觉得这位教宗值得注意；英国的报纸开出专栏，名曰“罗马日讯”。特派记者们可撒开了欢！《蓓尔美街报》的那位女将拿到了新堡街事件的独家；描写近视的教宗把眼睛眯成两条缝、扶着快晕的袭击者沿柱廊摸路的情状，一口气拆散了九个不定式；还有祂对有组织的慈善那场闹剧的痛斥——那闹剧竟容一个人堕落到这步田地；还有祂的焦灼，直到卡维尔红衣主教一路小跑送来祂的备用眼镜；还有祂戴上眼镜重见光明时那份古怪得叫人鼻酸的感激；还有祂为自己被玷污的衣袍痛心；还有祂请那饿瘪的社会主义者到梵蒂冈作客的殷殷之意。这一切对英国脾气正是一拍即合——因为够英国。可一种深远持久的印象就此铸成。普鲁士王的公使又给德皇写了些更骇人的东西。

===Original===

Hadrian became regarded in cabinets and chancelleries as one who cared or strove neither for loss nor gain, neither for life nor death﻿—as the one Potentate who rightly or wrongly knew his own mind﻿—as a Power with whom a reckoning might have to be made. After all, it merely was the effect of simplicity upon complexity, of felinity upon caninity.
He was sitting alone, thinking, and carefully unravelling a woollen antimacassar. It had been crocheted in five bossy strips, three of orange hue and two of grey, alternately arranged. He had unravelled two orange and two grey strips; and had the wool neatly rolled in four balls beside Him. The next time He should go into the City, some little girl would be made happy with two nice balls of grey wool and a lira to buy knitting needles; and, the time after that, another little girl would have three balls of orange wool and a lira also; and pontifical eyes would not be scorched by ghastly antimacassars any more, nor would the kind heart of anyone be wounded. He finished the job; and went to talk to his socialist. That one turned out to be a goldsmith, with the ideals and the brains and the fingers of Cellini, but not the acquisitiveness. Hence straits, socialism, sophistries, starvation. They walked about the sculpture-galleries for coolness; and spoke of Beautiful Things. Hadrian revelled. His guest was a man of taste; and talked-on-a-trot with wonderful gestures, making and moulding ideal images which the mind’s eye could see. They came to the *Apoxyomenos*: stood: raved; and became dumb, feasting on the lithe majesty of perfect proportion. The artificer first spoke.
“Holiness,” he said, “can You see that body and those limbs crucified?”
Hadrian’s mind caught the idea. The splendid forms of the marble seemed to rearrange themselves in the new pose. His eyes came slowly round to His questioner.
“Yes,” He answered: “but soaring and triumphing, ‘reigning from the tree,’ not drooping and dying﻿—and not the head and bust.” He took the goldsmith’s arm and hurried him to the *Antinous of the Belvedere*; and began to speak very quickly.
“Sir,” He said, “you will be pleased to stay here; and, with the materials which will be provided, you will make a new cross for Us.

===Chinese===

各国内阁与总理府从此把哈德良看作一个不计得失、不争生死的人——看作唯一一位不管对错、自有主见的君主——看作一个说不定迟早要同他算账的势力。说到底，这不过是简单对复杂、猫对狗的功效罢了。
祂正独坐着想事，细心地拆一条毛线椅披。那椅披钩成五条鼓凸的带子，三条橙色、两条灰色，相间排列。祂已拆下两条橙的、两条灰的；毛线绕得整整齐齐，四个线团搁在手边。下一次进城，哪个小女孩会得了两团上好的灰毛线外加一里拉买针，乐不可支；再下一次，另一个小女孩会有三团橙毛线，也有一里拉；而教宗的眼睛再不会被可怕的椅披灼伤，任何人的一片好心也不会再受伤。祂做完这件事；去看祂那位社会主义者。原来那人是位金匠，有切利尼的理想、头脑和手指，却没有切利尼的贪心。于是困顿、社会主义、诡辩、饥饿。他们在雕塑馆里溜达乘凉；谈美的事物。哈德良如鱼得水。他的客人是位有品味的人；连珠带比划地讲，用双手捏塑理想的形象，叫心灵的眼看得见。他们走到《拭垢者》像前：站住：激赏；随即哑然，饱餐那完美比例的柔韧威仪。匠人先开的口。
“圣下，”他说，“您看得见那身体、那四肢被钉上十字架的样子吗？”
哈德良的心抓住了这个意念。大理石那些辉煌的形体仿佛自行重排成新的姿势。祂的眼睛缓缓转回发问的人。
“看得见，”祂答：“可是上腾、凯旋，‘从木头上为王’，不是垂首、死去——也不要那颗头和胸像。”祂挽起金匠的胳膊，急急领他到观景楼的《安提诺乌斯》前；开始说得飞快。
“先生，”祂说，“请您留在这里；用即将备齐的材料，为我们造一座新的十字架。

===Original===

The cross will be of the kind called Potent, elongate: the Figure will combine the body and limbs of the *Apoxyomenos* with the head and bust of the *Antinous*, but posed as We have described. On the completion of this masterpiece, you will be offered an appointment as goldsmith in the pontifical household﻿—”
“Ah, Padrone.”
Hadrian returned to the secret chamber, happy in anticipation of an emblem which would not offend His taste. True, He was glad (in a way) that a tangled life so easily could be made straight: but it was the visionary ideal of Beauty which really inspired joy.

===Chinese===

十字架采用所谓“杖型”，加长：圣像以《拭垢者》的身体四肢，配《安提诺乌斯》的头与胸，姿势照我们方才所说的那样。这件杰作完工之日，将聘您为教廷御用金匠——”
“啊，帕德罗内（师父）。”
哈德良回到密室，满心欢喜地期待着一枚不再冒犯祂趣味的徽记。不错，一段乱麻般的人生竟这么容易就捋直了，祂（在某一方面）为此高兴：可真正点燃欢喜的，是那个幻视的理想——美。

===Original===

In obedience to the decree of Urban P.M. VIII, I declare that I have no intention of attributing any other than a purely human authority to the miracles, revelations, favours, & particular cases, recorded in this book; & the same as regards the titles of saints & blessed applied to servants of God not yet canonized: except in those cases which have been confirmed by the Holy Catholic Apostolic Roman See, of which I declare myself to be an obedient son; & therefore I submit myself & all which I have written to her judgment.
Fr. Rolfe.
XXII Jul., 1904.

===Chinese===
