主教回来后，哈德良邀他绕着各屋巡视一圈。这些屋子格格不入得刺眼：太红，太铜鎏金，太浮艳的文艺复兴，太乱心的洛可可。祂在这里做不成事。是的，做正事——什么也别想来撹。天哪，在这些彩绘的天花板底下，在这些暴烈的、一事无成的曲线中间，谁做得成事？如今祂做得成了，祂要什么就得有什么。祂要搬到顶层去，叫人踩不着祂的头，窗外的景致也好些。祂要有干净的光墙和素净，不要花哨。那样，祂的心思才动得开。走到衣橱旁边，祂把红天鹅绒骂了个狗血喷头。那东西得走。摸着它，祂浑身发麻；看见它穿在自己身上，祂就想起臭烘烘的狗吠和咋咋呼呼的驴叫。

===Original===

White was all right, if it fitted properly. He would stick to white, soft flannelly white, not this shiny cloth: with a decent surplice (which did not resemble the garments of David’s servants after the attentions of the children of Ammon)﻿—a surplice and the pallium, and the pontifical red stole in public: but no lace﻿—that should be left to ladies. How delicious to have plenty of white clothes to wear! How delicious to wear white in the sun! Well, He was going to work to earn all these amenities. And now, talking of work, something would have to be done to the rooms upstairs: and certain things would have to be settled regarding the domestic arrangements. To what official ought directions to be given?
“The Majordomo is the head of the household; and the Master of the Chamber has immediate charge of Your Holiness’s person.”
“That set man? Look now, he shall continue to be Master of the Chamber. We will not repeat the mistake of Pius IX, or interfere with any of their offices. But he must not come near Us. We should feel bound to assist his decrepitude; and Our idea is to be so free from secular cares that We can concentrate undivided attention upon Our Apostolature. There is the root of the matter. That man is a stranger: his age makes it certain that he has got into a groove: he is full of prior experiences and opinions which he cannot, and ought not to be expected to, change for a newcomer. But, if he remains here, it will be Ourself Who will have to obey him. That would distract Us. Therefore, We must interpose someone whom We know﻿—someone who is young enough to suit himself to Us. There are two young ruffians of about twenty-five years old, who, like most of his other acquaintances, formerly loved and hated George Arthur Rose. Their circumstances are disagreeable: they never had a chance: they are hotheaded passionate people, always in love with some woman or other, because they have no means of amusing themselves innocently, being tied and bound with the chains of respectable poverty. They really have no opportunity of leading godly righteous and sober lives. They’re insane, unhealthy, because civilization gives them no opportunity to live sane healthy lives unless they crush all the most salient and most admirable characteristics of their individuality.

===Chinese===

白色不坏，只要合身。祂要守着白，软法兰绒的白，不是这种亮闪闪的料子：再配一件像样的白袈裟（总不能像亚扪人子弟修理过大卫仆人衣裳的那个样子）——白袈裟加披带，公众场合再加一条教宗的红圣带：可不要花边——花边留给太太们。有满满一柜白衣可穿，多美！在太阳底下穿一身白，多美！好，祂这就去做工，挣来这一切舒服。说到做工，楼上的屋子得收拾出来；家务的章程也得定几条。指示该下给哪位官员？
“总管大臣是全家的首脑；御前大臣则直接侍奉圣下圣躬。”
“那个定了型的男人？您听好：御前大臣他照做。我们不肯重蹈庇护九世的覆辙，不去动他们任何人的职分。可他不许靠近我们。否则我们免不得要扶助他的老迈；而我们的主意是：摆脱尘世的操劳，好把全副精神专注于我们的宗座事业。根子在这里。那人是个陌生人：他那年纪，准已进了轨道；他满肚子从前的阅历和成见，不能、也不该指望他为一个新来的人改。可他若留在这里，就得我们服从他。那要分我们的心。所以，我们必须安插一个我们自己的人——一个年轻到能迁就我们的人。有两个二十五岁上下的小浑蛋，同他其余的大多数相识一样，从前又爱又恨乔治·亚瑟·罗斯。他们的境况糟：从没得过机会：是火爆性子、热肠人，总要爱上个把女人，因为他们没有清白取乐的法子，被体面的贫穷捆得死死的。他们实在没有过敬虔、公义、节制生活的机会。他们疯癫、病态，因为文明不给机会——除非碾碎他们个性里最挺拔、最可敬的东西，否则他们过不上健全健康的日子。

===Original===

Please send for them﻿—John Devine, 107, Arkwright Street, Preston﻿—Iulo Carrino, 95, Bloomsbury Square, London﻿—and let Us give them some service and much freedom, and a little wholesome neglect to strengthen and develop their characters and to give play to their individual natures, as good old Jowett says. We believe in making it, not difficult but, easy to be good﻿—Look, Frank, tell Iulo Carrino to bring with him that yellow cat which you may remember. By the by, both these men cannot move without money. Take this cheque for George Arthur Rose’s balance at Coutts’s: use what is generous﻿—generous, mind you﻿—and account to Us later. And now, about the other things, We had better see Centrina and the Majordomo upstairs.”
The Pope and the bishop inspected a series of empty rooms on the top-floor. They occupied the N. E. and the S. E. sides of the palace. Hadrian chose the large room in the angle with windows on two sides, for the secret chamber. It was approached from the N. E. corridor by way of fifteen antechambers and a large room suitable for private receptions. Beyond the antechambers there was another series of apartments which He also took. The private room in the angle, sitting-room, or workshop (as He called it), led into some smaller rooms on the S. E. face of the palace. Here he fixed upon a bedroom, bathroom, dressing-room, oratory, and sundry storerooms, accessible by a single door in the last room which led into the corridor overlooking the court of St. Damasus.
The Majordomo and the Master-of-the-Chamber attended. The latter was quaking about his situation. Hadrian rapidly reassured him and came to the point. “You are confirmed in your benefice until such time as you choose to retire. The emoluments and the pension are at your disposal. In a few days, two gentlemen will arrive from England. You will prepare a parlour and a bedroom for each, adjoining the first antechamber. Fix a bell in each parlour communicating with this room. (They were standing in the room which had been selected as a workshop.) You will provide two servants for them. They will take their meals in their parlours. After their arrival, Our commandments will come to you through them.” (He turned and addressed Himself to the Majordomo.) “These two gentlemen must be given some official status.”

===Chinese===

请派人去找他们——约翰·德文（John Devine），普雷斯顿阿克赖特街一〇七号——尤洛·卡里诺（Iulo Carrino），伦敦布鲁姆斯伯里广场九十五号——让我们给他们一点差使、许多自由，再加一点有益的疏忽，好让他们的品格坚强发达，让他们各人的天性得所施展——老乔伊特说得那样。我们信：为善，不该难，该容易——听着，弗兰克，告诉尤洛·卡里诺，把那只黄猫也带来，您也许还记得。对了，这两个人没有钱寸步难行。这张支票是乔治·亚瑟·罗斯在库茨银行的存款余额：花多少要大方——记住，要大方——回头再向我们报账。好了，其余的事，我们最好上楼去见森特里纳和总管大臣。”
教宗同主教察看了顶层一溜空屋。它们占着宫殿的东北角和东南角。哈德良挑了角上那间两面临窗的大屋做密室。从东北走廊进去，要穿过十五间前厅和一间宜于私人接见的大屋。前厅再往里，另有一串屋子，祂也一并要了。角上那间私室——起居室，或者按祂的叫法，工房——通到宫殿东南面的几间小屋。祂在那里定了卧室、浴室、更衣室、祈祷室和几间杂用储藏室，出入口是末尾一屋的一道门，门外就是俯瞰圣达马苏斯庭院的走廊。
总管大臣和御前大臣来了。后一位正为自己的位置发抖。哈德良三言两语安了他的心，随即入题。“您的圣职确认在身，直到您自愿荣休为止。薪俸与年金悉听尊便。几日后，有两位先生将从英国来。您在头一间前厅旁边，为他们各备一间客厅、一间卧室。每间客厅装一只铃，通到这间屋。（他们正站在选作工房的那间屋里。）您为他们各派两名仆人。他们在自己客厅里用饭。他们到后，我们的命令经由他们传达给您。”（祂转过身，对总管大臣说。）“这两位先生得有个官家的名分。”

===Original===

“If I understand aright, Your Holiness is appointing two Gentlemen-in-Waiting-in-the-Apostolic-Chamber.”
“That will do. When they arrive, see that they have diplomas of appointment as Gentlemen of the Apostolic Chamber. The Bishop of Caerleon will arrange with you about their emoluments. Now, let Us furnish these rooms.”
They went out into the corridor; and reentered the apartment by the first antechamber.
“Cover all the walls and ceilings with brown-packing paper﻿—yes, brown-packing paper﻿—*carta straccia*,” the Pope repeated. “Stain all the woodwork with a darker shade of brown. The gilding of the cornices can remain as it is. No carpets. These small greenish-blue tiles are clean; and they soothe the eye. Curtains? You may hang very voluminous linen curtains on the doors and windows, greenish-blue linen to match the tiles, and without borders. Furnish all those antechambers with rush chairs and oaken tables. Remember that everything is to be plain, without ornament.﻿—In this room you may place the usual throne and canopy: and that crucifix from downstairs﻿—(how exquisite the mother-of-pearl Figure is!)﻿—and the stools, and twelve large candlesticks﻿—iron or brass.﻿—Now this room is to be a workshop. Let Us have a couch and three armchairs, all large and low and well-cushioned, covered with undyed leather. Get some of those large plain wooden tables which are used in kitchens, about three yards long and one-and-a-half wide. Put writing-materials on one of them, there, on the right of the window. Leave the middle of the room empty. Put three small bookcases against that wall and a cupboard here.﻿—Make a bedroom of this room. Let the bed be narrow and long, with a husk mattress; and let the back of the head be toward the window. Put one of the large wooden tables here and a dozen rush-chairs.﻿—(He spoke to the bishop.) Do you know that there is no water here at all, except in little jugs? (He continued to the Majordomo.) Line the walls of this room with greenish-blue tiles, like those on the floor. Put several pegs on both doors. In this corner put a drainpipe covered with a grating; and, six feet above it, let a waterpipe and tap project rectangularly two feet from the wall. Yes. Six feet from the floor, two feet from the wall; and let there be a constant and copious supply of water﻿—rainwater, if possible. Do you understand?”
The Majordomo understood.

===Chinese===

“若臣下理会得不错，圣下这是任命两位宗座宫廷侍从。”
“可以这么说。他们到时，请给他们备好宗座宫廷侍从的委任状。俸禄一节，卡尔勒昂主教会同您商量。现在，让我们来布置这些屋子。”
他们走进走廊，再从第一间前厅折回那套屋子。
“所有的墙面和天花板，全用棕色包装纸裱糊——对，棕色包装纸——意大利话叫‘纸筋’（carta straccia），”教宗重复了一遍。“所有的木活，染成深一档的棕色。檐口的贴金可以照旧不动。不要地毯。这些青绿色的小方砖干净，也养眼。帘子呢？门窗上尽可以挂宽大的亚麻布帘，青绿色亚麻布，配地砖的颜色，不带滚边。那些前厅，一律摆灯心草椅和橡木桌。记住，样样要朴素，不加点饰。——这间屋里，可以照例设宝座和华盖：再把楼下那个十字架请来——（那螺钿的圣像做得多精！）——再加上凳子，十二支大烛台——铁的或黄铜的。——这间做工房。给我们一张睡榻、三把扶手椅，都要宽大、低矮、坐垫厚实，蒙本色的皮革。弄几张厨房用的那种大木桌来，约三码长、一码半宽。一张摆上笔墨，就搁那儿，窗子右手。屋子当中空着。那面墙根靠三只小书柜，这边放一口柜橱。——这间做卧室。床要窄而长，铺壳褥；床头朝窗。这里摆一张大木桌，十几把灯心草椅。——（祂对主教说。）您可知道，这里除了一壶一壶的水，竟没有一点水？（祂接着对总管大臣说。）这间屋的墙上，也镶青绿色瓷砖，同地上的一样。两道门上都钉几个挂钩。这个角落装一根下水管，上加格栅；离地六英尺，再叫一根水管加龙头从墙里直角伸出两英尺。对。离地六英尺，出墙两英尺；水要长流、要丰沛——雨水最好。您听明白了？”
总管大臣听明白了。

===Original===

The Master-of-the-Chamber shivered.
“And lamps. Get two plain oil-lamps for each room, with copper shades: large lamps, to give a very strong light. Paint over both doors of the bedroom, on the outside of each, *Intrantes excommunicantur ipso facto*. When We have finished here,” (He addressed the Master-of-the-Chamber again), “you will parade your staff; and We will select one person and provide him with a dispensation from that rule as long as he behaves himself well. He will have charge of the bedroom and the sole right to enter it.” (The Pope passed into the next room: paused, and whispered explicit directions to the Majordomo; and moved on to the farther room.) “The clothespresses from downstairs can be moved into this room. They will serve. And you had better make a door here, so that it can be entered from the corridor.” (He went on again.) “This room is to be the vestry;﻿—and this the oratory. Let Us have a plain stone altar and the stations, and the bare necessaries for mass, all of the simplest. Let everything, walls, floor, ceiling, everything, be white﻿—natural white, not painted; and make a door here, also leading into the corridor, a large double-door convenient for the faithful who assist at the pontifical mass. The rooms beyond﻿—you will take order about them at a convenient occasion.”
Hadrian and the bishop returned to the pontifical apartments downstairs.
“Your Holiness will excuse me﻿—”
“Yes?”
“﻿—but have You ever contemplated the present situation?”
“No. Why?”
“Well, Your Holiness seems to have everything cut and dried.”
The Pope laughed. “You shall know that George Arthur Rose has had plenty of time for thinking and scheming. His schemes never came to anything, except once; and he certainly never schemed for this. But you understand perhaps that the last twenty years have rendered Hadrian conscious both of His abilities and His limitations, as well as of His requirements; and hence He is able at a glance to describe in detail what He wants. When He wants something, without knowing what He wants, He asks questions. For example, what is that hinged arrangement under Cardinal Courtleigh’s ring?”
“A master-key, Holiness; I have just got one too.” The bishop showed his own ring.
“What is that?”

===Chinese===

御前大臣打了个寒噤。
“还有灯。每间屋两盏素油灯，配铜罩：大灯，要极亮的。卧室两道门的外面，各漆上一行字：‘擅入者以此为准，即遭绝罚’。我们在这里的事一了，（祂又转向御前大臣，）您把您手下全体列队；我们挑出一个人，给他一纸特免，只要他行为检点，此例对他长免。卧室归他管，也只有他有进那屋的权利。”（教宗踱入邻室：停下，对总管大臣低声交代了几句明明白白的话；又往更里间去。）“楼下的衣橱可以搬进这间。尽够用了。这里最好再开一道门，从走廊也进得来。”（祂又往前走。）“这间做祭衣房；——这间做祈祷室。给我们一座素石祭台，设十四处苦路，再备弥撒的必需品，一切从简。一切东西——墙、地、顶，一切——都要白：天然的白，不是漆出来的；这里也开一道门，同样通走廊，要宽大的双扇门，好方便辅礼教宗弥撒的信友。再过去那些屋子——找个方便的日子，您再作安排。”
哈德良同主教回到楼下教宗的那套屋子。
“圣下恕我多嘴——”
“嗯？”
“——圣下可曾细想过眼下的局面？”
“没有。怎么？”
“这个，圣下看去事事都有成算了。”
教宗笑了。“您该知道，乔治·亚瑟·罗斯有的是工夫去想、去盘算。他的盘算从没成过一件事，只有一件例外；这一桩，他确实从没盘算过。可您大概明白：过去这二十年，叫哈德良认清了自己的能与不能，也认清了自己的所需；所以他一眼之间，就能把要的东西一样样说到底。他若想要什么、却不知道要的是什么，他就发问。比方说，考特利枢机那枚戒指底下装着的绞链玩意儿，是什么？”
“一把万能钥匙，圣下；我新近也得了一把。”主教亮出自己的戒指。
“那是什么？”

===Original===

“I have several places which I have to keep locked, safes, cupboards, and that sort of thing; and the keys, which are all different, have to be entrusted to my various chaplains, and so on. Well, each of these can only open the lock of the thing which concerns him: but, with that master-key, I can unlock everything and no one else in the world can do that.”
“Capital! Where do you get these things made?”
“At a place in Band Street﻿—Brahma I think the name is.”
“Tell them to﻿—” The voice sank, for some scarlet gentleman began to bring in tables with the sealed dishes of the pontifical supper. Hadrian’s eyes lingered on the intruders for a moment. They were so slim, so robust, so deft, so grave, so Roman. He drew the bishop into the embrasure of a window.
“Aren’t they lovely?” He said. “Isn’t the world full of lovely things, lovely live things? It’s the dead and the stagnant that are ugly.”
This was so rapid a change of mood that Talacryn could not follow it. As soon as the servants were gone, Hadrian continued, returning the episcopal ring,
“Tell your Brahma people to fit all the doors upstairs with locks which have separate keys, and to send another score of locks also with separate keys; and also to send a man here who is capable of making an episcopal ring for Us which shall contain a master-key to all those locks.”
“Very well, Holy Father.”
“Don’t go. Supper can wait a minute. Look here: We desire to be in direct communication with the Sacred College. We chiefly are curious to know the nine Compromissaries: but distinctions sometimes are invidious. At all events, We must have a long and secret conference with Cardinal Courtleigh. So will you please make it known to Their Eminencies that We will receive them after supper. Ask Pimlico to remain after the others. And﻿—who manages the finances here?”
“The Cardinal-Deacon of Santa Maria Nuova is Apostolic Treasurer; and the Majordomo is responsible for the household expenses.”
“Ask the Treasurer particularly to come? Don’t come yourself. Good night: God bless you.”
Caerleon firmly had believed that he knew George Arthur Rose to be charming﻿—perhaps somewhat incomprehensible, and therefore perhaps somewhat dangerous. But as for Hadrian﻿—Caerleon felt about him as M. and Mme.

===Chinese===

“我有好几处地方须得上锁，保险柜呀，柜子呀，这一类；那些钥匙各不相同，只好分交给我的各位专职司铎等等。这样，每一把只开同它相干的那把锁：可凭这把万能钥匙，我什么都打得开，世上再没第二个人打得开。”
“妙极！这些东西哪儿定做的？”
“班德街一家铺子——字号好像叫布拉马。”
“叫他们——”话音低了下去，因为一位绯衣先生开始端着封盖的菜肴进来，摆教宗的晚餐。哈德良的眼睛在那几位闯入者身上逗留了一刻。他们那样苗条，那样健壮，那样利落，那样庄重，那样罗马。祂把主教拉到窗龢里。
“他们不可爱吗？”祂说。“这世界不遍地都是可爱的东西、可爱的活物吗？丑的，只有死的和滞的。”
情绪转得这样快，塔拉克林跟不上了。仆人一走，哈德良把主教戒指还他，接着说：
“叫您那家布拉马，把楼上的门全装上各有专钥的锁；再送二十把也各有专钥的锁来；再派个匠人来，给我们制一枚主教戒指，戒指里藏一把万能钥匙，开得着上述所有的锁。”
“遵命，圣父。”
“别走。晚饭等一等。您听好：我们要同神圣枢机团直接通气。我们顶想知道那九位折衝选举人是谁：可分别是招人嫌的。无论如何，我们须同考特利枢机作一次长而机密的会谈。所以就请您向诸位阁下传话：我们今晚接见他们。请平利科在众人之后留下。还有——这里的财计归谁管？”
“新圣玛利亚枢机执事是宗座总务；家用开销归总管大臣负责。”
“请总务大人务必也来？您自己不要来。晚安：上帝祝福您。”
卡尔勒昂素来信得过：乔治·亚瑟·罗斯这个人迷人——也许有点费解，因此也许有点危险。至于哈德良——卡尔勒昂对他的感觉，就像居里先生和夫人头一回把一枚便士擨在镭上、望着那既具体又无穷尽的源头源源放出的穿透力时一样。

===Original===

Curie felt when they first put a penny on a piece of radium and observed the penetrative energy incessantly thrown off from a source which was both concrete and inexhaustible.
The Pope’s evening party was well attended. Some of the older members of the Sacred College, who really had suffered from the discomforts of the Conclave, had left the Vatican. Most of the French absented themselves, as they had every right to do in view of the informality of the invitation. The Secretary of State stayed away on a plea of business. But a mixed motive, in which inquisitiveness was the dominant ingredient, impelled thirty-two vermilion princes into the Pontiff’s throne-room. The Cardinal-Dean, notwithstanding his age and infirmity, came with glee. Next to succeeding to the paparchy himself, nothing suited him better than to have a perfect stranger for a Pope, Who evidently was about to subvert every single act of Leo’s. He said almost as much to Hadrian, bustling up to the throne and using a stool.
“We take it very kindly that Your Eminency should come to Us; and We let you know that We summon Our first consistory to meet on the thirtieth day of April,” said the Pope, in a tone which was a skilful blend of the World’s Ruler’s with that of youth to age, of a newcomer to an old stager.
Orezzo was pleased. He took the ball of conversation and set it rolling. “It is a fortunate event, Holiness,” he said, “that the Divine Leo﻿—may His soul rest in a cool place﻿—never carried out His intention of nominating His successors.”
“Ah!” the Pope responded. “We remember reading about that in an English newspaper, the *Pall Mall Gazette*, a few years back. Perhaps Your Eminency can tell Us what truth there was in the report?”
“The facts, Holy Father, were these. Leo so firmly believed that the policy, which He had seen fit to pursue during His long reign, was essential to the welfare of the Church, that He wished to be assured of its continuance; and He would have had each of us to promise Him that, upon election, we would not depart from His example. Some of us﻿—I name no names﻿—were unwilling to bind ourselves; and, being unable to secure unanimous assurance, Leo declared that He would use the plenitude of the apostolic power and nominate His successors.”
The other cardinals, attracted by these words, drew nearer to the throne.

===Chinese===

教宗的晚会到场很盛。枢机团里几位年长的，实在叫枢机会议的苦处伤着了，已经出了梵蒂冈。法兰西一派大半缺席——请柬既这样随便，他们大可以不来。国务卿借口公务也躲了。可是一股掺着好奇心大半头的杂拌动机，把三十二位朱红亲王推进了教宗的觐见厅。枢机团团长不顾年迈体弱，兴冲冲地来了。除了自家爬上教宗宝座，再没有比这更合他心意的了：来一个十足的生人做教宗，且眼看要把良的一切行事一桩桩掀翻。他差不多就这样对哈德良说了，一路挤到宝座跟前，还占了一只凳子。
“阁下肯屈驾来见我们，我们领情；并谨告知：我们召集的首届枢机会议，定于四月三十日举行。”教宗说，那声调把尘世主宰的口气同少对老、新客对老手的口气，巧妙地揉作一处。
奥雷佐高兴了。他把话头这只球接过来，让它滚起来。“真是一桩幸事，圣下，”他说，“神圣的良——愿他的灵魂安息在凉快地方——到底没有实行他指定继承人的主意。”
“啊！”教宗应道。“我们记得几年前在一家英国报纸《帕尔莫尔公报》上读到过。阁下或许能告诉我们，那则消息里几分是真？”
“实情是这样，圣父。良坚信，他长任期间所行的那套方略，于教会的福祉必不可少，所以他要担保它延续下去；他要我们每个人向他应许：一经当选，决不离开他的先例。我们中间几位——恕我不点名——不肯把自己绑上；良得不到一致的应许，便宜称：他要动用宗座权力的丰满，指定他的继承人。”
其余的枢机被这番话引了过来，朝宝座围拢。

===Original===

Some sat on stools: others remained standing: all intently listened to Orezzo: all intently gazed at Hadrian. The aspect of the Pontiff did not give satisfaction. It was not listless: it was not inattentive, for, as a matter of fact, it indicated very vivid ardent studiose concern, a perfect perception of being “among the Doctors”: but Hadrian seemed to be treating the matter too impersonally, too much from the viewpoint of the outsider. He gave no sign whatever that He was conscious how very nearly this thing touched Himself.
“He reminds one of a surgeon probing for a bullet in a body which is not his,” said Mundo to Fiamma.
“And He will find that bullet,” the Archbishop of Bologna replied.
Hadrian (Who could see as far through a brick wall as most men, and a great deal further than some), was not by any means unconscious of the situation, and was avidly curious after information. He pursued the inquiry. Many thought it would have been more delicate to drop it.
“Yes. That was the gist of the statement in the paper,” He continued to Orezzo. “We remember it well: because We wondered whether or not such a privilege was included in that ‘plenitude of apostolic power.’ We could not find a precedent; and none of the authorities whom We consulted could provide one. Advise Us, Lord Cardinal.”
If Orezzo had not been Cardinal-Bishop of Ostia and Velletri, Dean of the Sacred College, and Chamberlain of the Holy Roman Church, he would have grinned. He found the moment unmitigatedly delectable.
“Holiness, there is a pious opinion, represented (I believe) by the Cardinal-Penitentiary”﻿—(Serafino-Vagellaio violently flushed)﻿—“to the effect that the Divine Leo was not in error. Also, there is another pious opinion, represented (I happen to know) by the rest of the College, that on this point the said Divine Leo erred as infallibly as possible.”
This was thin ice indeed.
“Your Eminency’s exposition hath been most sound. The matter is one for the theologians,” said Hadrian, ceasing to lean forward. “But why, Lord Cardinal, do you call it fortunate that the nomination was not effected?”
“Because if it had been effected, we might not have experienced the pleasure of saluting a Pontiff Who, according to the Cardinal of Pimlico, is an academic anarchist.”

===Chinese===

有的坐上凳子：有的站着：个个凝神听奥雷佐：个个凝神盯哈德良。教宗的神情叫人不满意。不是无精打采：也不是心不在焉——实情是，那神情透着一股鲜活热切的用功劲儿，透着一种十足的自觉：身在“博士们”中间：可哈德良似乎把事情办得太置身事外，太从旁观者的角度看。他一点没有露出意思：他明白这桩事同他自己贴得多近。
“他叫人想起外科医生，在别人的身体里探一颗子弹。”芒多对菲亚马说。
“而他会找到那颗子弹的。”博洛尼亚总主教答。
哈德良（隔着砖墙，他看得同多数人一样远，比某些人还远得多）对局面绝非无所觉，正急切地想探消息。他追问下去。许多人觉得，撒手才更得体。
“对。报上那篇话的要旨就是这样，”他接着对奥雷佐说。“我们记得清楚：因为我们当时就疑惑，这项特权算不算在那‘宗座权力丰满’之内。我们找不到先例；我们请教过的权威，也拿不出一个。请阁下为我们决疑。”
奥雷佐若不是奥斯蒂亚及韦勒特里枢机主教、神圣枢机团团长、圣罗马教会总司库，他就要嘨嘴笑了。他只觉得这一刻痛快到头。
“圣下，有一种敬度的见解，由（据我所信）忏悔部枢机代表——（塞拉菲诺-瓦格拉约腾地涨红了脸）——其大意是：神圣的良并不为错。另有一种敬度的见解，由（我恰好知道）枢机团其余诸位代表：在这点上，那位神圣的良，错得再绝无错不过了。”
这话真如薄冰。
“阁下的剖析极为中肯。此事归神学家们。”哈德良说完，不再前倾。“可是阁下，您为什么说，提名未曾实行是件幸事？”
“因为倘若实行了，我们就享受不到向一位教宗致敬的乐趣了——照平利科枢机的说法，这位教宗是个学院派无政府主义者。”

===Original===

Hadrian candidly and simply laughed, with a friendly look at Courtleigh, who did not at all like being the second victim of Orezzo’s caustic tongue.
“His Eminency has taken that bad habit of labelling people from Us,” He said. “But, although We give due weight to the epithet ‘academic,’ We abhor from and cannot away with the term ‘anarchist.’ Aristocrat We are not: the mere word Democrat fills Us with repugnance. Such as it is, Our philosophy is individualistic altruism. But, Eminencies, is not the labelling of matter which is in a state of flux, humanity for example, somewhat futile? Even supposing the labelled matter to be static, do not the very words on the label change their meaning with the course of time? But deeds remain; and the motive of a deed is that by which it must, and will, be judged. Give Us then the benefit of your holy prayers, Lord Cardinals, that Our motives may be pure, and Our acts acceptable to Him Who has deigned to Our unworthy hands the awful office of His Vicegerent here on earth.”
He leaned back in His chair for the moment after this little outburst. The sense of His enormous responsibility was upon Him. In an indefinite shadowy sort of way, it had been in His mind to utter some such allocution to the cardinals by way of explaining to them His Own conception of His task: but He had intended to make it more of a deliberate formal pronouncement. The instant when the words had passed His lips, however, He perceived that in one sentence He had said all. He also perceived that the gaiety of the beginning, and the solemnity of the conclusion, sufficed to give His utterance distinction. He said no more. There was no doubt but that He had created an impression: an impression which differed, it is true, according to the temper of the impressed﻿—but still He had created an impression. Those Eminencies, who were more formal than vital, assumed that professional abstraction of demeanour which marks a conference of clergy while one of their number is “talking shop.” Those two or three, who were devout enthusiasts, blessed themselves and exhibited the white cornea beneath the iris of their eyes. The majority, (who combined the qualities of the dignified fine-gentleman-of-the-old-school, with those of the scholar, the teacher, and the practical Christian) beamed instant approbation.

===Chinese===

哈德良爽朗而单纯地笑了，友好地望了考特利一眼——考特利做了奥雷佐毒舌的第二个牺牲品，心里老大不痛快。
“阁下这个给人贴标签的坏习惯，是从我们这儿学的，”祂说。“不过，‘学院派’这个字眼，我们给它应有的分量；‘无政府主义者’这个词，我们厌而难忍。贵族主义者，我们不是：‘民主党人’这个词，单单听着就叫我们生憎。我们的哲学，要说有，是个性主义的利他主义。可是，诸位阁下，给流动不居的东西——比如人性——贴标签，岂非徒劳？就算被贴的东西是静止的，标签上的字眼，难道不也随岁月改换意义？唯有行为长存；而行为的动机，才是它必受、也将受审判的凭据。那么，枢机大人们，请把你们圣洁的祈祷赐给我们，愿我们的动机纯净，愿我们的行为，蒙祂悦纳——祂竟肯把这世上祂代理人的可怕职位，交到我们不配的手里。”
这一小阵倾吐之后，祂往椅背上一靠。那千钧的责任感压在祂身上。原本，祂心里影影绰绰存着意思：要向枢机们发一篇这样的训谕，把自己对本身职分的见解讲给他们听：只是祂原打算把它弄成一篇从容的正式文告。可话一出口，祂便发觉：一句话里，祂已说尽了一切。祂又发觉：开头的明快同结尾的庄重，已足以给这番话添了身份。祂不再多说。毫无疑问，祂造成了印象：这印象，自然随受者性情各有不同——可印象到底是造成了。那几位重形式过于重血性的阁下，摆出职业性的出神面孔——教士开会、同僚“谈行话”时惯摆的那种。那两三位虔诚的热心人，画了十字，把眼睛下方的眼白都露了出来。多数派（他们兼有旧派体面绅士与学者、教师、实践的基督徒诸般品性）霎时喜形于色。

===Original===

Their verdict was that the utterance was very correct and proper. Nothing could be more true.
The assemblage split-up into groups; and separate conversations were begun. The Pope sat, still and grave. Orezzo gracefully pleaded his age and the hour of night: kissed the Apostle’s knee; and retired.
Hadrian beckoned the Cardinal-Deacon of Santa Maria Nuova; and addressed him in a confidential manner.
“We understand that the expenses of Our household pass through the hands of the Majordomo. Are they paid from some fund particularly allotted to the purpose?”
“Yes, Most Holy Lord; from﻿—”
“The details are unimportant. And the expenses of the paparchy in general?”
“There are numerous funds, Most Holy Lord, which are administered by numerous departments under my supervision.”
“And those funds﻿—Some suffice; and some do not suffice. They vary, no doubt?”
“Most Holy Lord, they vary.”
“Is there any particular fund over which We have exclusive control?”
“The whole revenue, Most Holy Lord, is subject to Your pleasure: but Peter’s Pence belong to the pontiff-regnant personally. They are His private property﻿—salary﻿—honorarium, I should say.”
“In eight days, Your Eminency will be good enough to let Us know the annual average of that income, say for the last twenty years.”
“It shall be done, Most Holy Lord.”
“Meanwhile, what money is at Our disposal at this moment?”
“There has been accumulated a large reserve, the exact amount of which is known only to the bankers. It is Yours, Most Holy Lord.”
“What approximately is the sum?”
“In round numbers, Most Holy Lord, it cannot be less than five millions.”
“Lire?”
“Pounds sterling, Most Holy Lord.”
Hadrian’s eyes sparkled. “Where is it?”
“The bulk is in the Bank of England, Most Holy Lord: but there is much gold in the safe.”
“Which safe?”
“The safe in the bedroom wall, Most Holy Lord.”
“Where is the key?”
“The Cardinal-Chamberlain holds all keys, Most Holy Lord.”
“Tomorrow Your Eminency will be good enough to cause the safe in the bedroom-wall to be removed to a similar position in the bedroom which We have instructed the Majordomo to prepare on the upper storey. And now please follow the Cardinal-Chamberlain: obtain the key of the safe; and bring it to Us.”
The Apostolic Treasurer rose; and went out. Hadrian also stood up. The company, understanding that the reception was ended, made obeisance and began to move away.

===Chinese===

他们的评语是：这番话十分正、十分得体。再真不过。
人群散成一小簇一小簇；各谈各的起来了。教宗端坐，静而庄重。奥雷佐优雅地诉了年高夜深：亲了宗徒的膝：告退了。
哈德良召新圣玛利亚枢机执事近前，用密谈的口气同他说话。
“我们晓得，我们这一家的开销经总管大臣之手。开销出自一笔专拨的款项么？”
“是，至圣者；出自——”
“细节不要紧。那教宗职位一总的开销呢？”
“款项繁多，至圣者，由我监督下许多部门分掌。”
“那些款项——有的够，有的不够。想必参差不一？”
“参差不一，至圣者。”
“可有哪一笔款项，归我们一人独断？”
“全部进项，至圣者，都凭圣意裁夺：惟圣彼得献金归在位教宗本人。那是他的私产——薪俸——该说酬金。”
“八天之内，请阁下把这笔进项的年均数告诉我们，就算近二十年的吧。”
“遵命，至圣者。”
“眼下的当口，我们手边可支配多少钱？”
“积有一大宗储备，确数只有银行家知道。那都是圣下的，至圣者。”
“大约几何？”
“取整数，至圣者，不会少于五百万。”
“里拉？”
“英镑，至圣者。”
哈德良目光一亮。“在哪里？”
“大头在英格兰银行，至圣者：不过保险柜里也有许多金子。”
“哪只保险柜？”
“卧室墙里那只，至圣者。”
“钥匙在谁手里？”
“钥匙全在枢机总司库手里，至圣者。”
“明日烦阁下把卧室墙里的保险柜，移到楼上那间卧室的同样位置去——那间屋我们已吩咐总管大臣收拾。现在请随枢机总司库去：取保险柜的钥匙；给我们拿来。”
宗座总务起身出去了。哈德良也站起。众人明白接见到此为止，躬身行礼，开始散去。

===Original===

The Pope detained Courtleigh.
“Eminency,” He said, “We have many things to say to you: but We will not detain you now. Tomorrow We go to Castel Gandolfo. Come with Us. A few tired priests are sure of a hospitable welcome there. Yes, come with Us. Who is that young cardinal by the door?”
“That is Monsignor Nefski, Holiness﻿—the Archbishop of Prague.”
“He is marked by some fearful sorrow?”
“A most fearful sorrow indeed.”
“Once, in a man’s rooms at Oxford, a young undergraduate happened to enter. He had just that deadly pallor, that dense black hair, that rigidity of feature, that bleached bleak fixity of gaze. When he was gone, We remarked on his appearance. Our host said that he had been seeing his best friend drowned. They were on a cliff, somewhere in Your Eminency’s native-land, taking photographs of breakers in the height of a storm. The friend was on the very verge. Suddenly the cliff gave way; and he fell into the raging sea. He was a magnificent swimmer. He struggled with the billows for more than half an hour. There was no help within five miles; and, finally, the breath was battered out of him. The other perforce had to stand by, and watch it all. It indelibly marked him. Cardinal Nefski, you say, is marked by a fearful experience. Lately? Was it as fearful as that?”
“Ten weeks ago, Holiness; and a much more fearful experience.”
“Eminency, bring him also to Castel Gandolfo. Some of you must attend the Pope. Let Us have those to whom We can be useful.”
When he was alone, Hadrian examined the safe in the bedroom wall. It added to His consciousness of His immense potentiality. What a number of long-planned things He could do now! With its contents, He would open a current account at the Bank of Italy. With that, and another at the Bank of England﻿—He acquainted Himself with the tools of His new trade. Truly, Caerleon did not altogether err in calling Him an incomprehensible creature. On the one hand, with His principle of giving He could not even grasp a problem which involved taking: while, on the other hand, He utterly failed to realize that most people are averse from giving. As for Himself, He took freely; and, as freely, He was going to give.

===Chinese===

教宗把考特利留了下来。
“阁下，”祂说，“我们有许多话要同您说：可现在不放您了。明日我们去冈道尔夫堡。请与我们同行。几个疲乏的司铎，在那里准有一场宾至如归的接待。对，同我们去。门口那位年轻枢机是谁？”
“那是涅夫斯基蒙席，圣下——布拉格总主教。”
“他带着某种可怕的忧愁的印记？”
“实在可怕得很。”
“有一回，在牛津一个人屋里，一个年轻的本科生恰巧进来。他正有着那种要命的苍白，那种浓密的黑发，那种面容的僵冷，那种惨白荒寒的定定的目光。他走后，我们说起他的样子。主人说：他刚眼睁睁看着自己最好的朋友淹死。他们在崖上，就在阁下的故乡什么地方，顶着风暴的顶点拍海浪的照片。朋友正站在最边上。崖忽然塌了；他跌进狂怒的海里。他是一把游泳的好手。他同巨浪搏斗了半个多钟头。方圆五英里没有救应；到末了，气息被生生砸没了。另一个只得站在旁边，眼看这一切。这在他身上留了洗不掉的印。涅夫斯基枢机，您说，也带着可怕经历的印。新近么？有那样可怕么？”
“十个星期前，圣下；那经历可怕得多。”
“阁下，把他也带来冈道尔夫堡。你们当中总得有人随侍教宗。让我们把那些我们用得着的人带在身边。”
只剩一人时，哈德良察看了卧室墙里的保险柜。它更叫祂意识到自己那浩大的潜能。如今多少蓄谋已久的事，祂可以动手了！他要拿它的内容，在意大利银行开一个往来户。有这一笔，再加英格兰银行的另一笔——祂把新行当的家什一件件摸熟了。真的，卡尔勒昂称祂为费解的人物，不能算全错。一头，祂本着给与的原则，连“取”字当头的题目都把握不住；另一头，祂又全然不明白：大多数人是厌恶给与的。至于祂自己，祂取起来大方；给起来，也要一样大方。

===Original===

As for the Bishop of Caerleon’s opinion﻿—it is so easy and so satisfactory to call a man “an incomprehensible creature,” when one is mentally incapable of comprehending, or unwilling to try to comprehend, the “creature.”

===Chinese===

至于卡尔勒昂主教的见解——当一个人在心智上无力领会那“人物”、或不屑费神去领会时，把人家叫一声“费解的人物”，岂不又省事、又舒服。

## VI / 六
===Original===

He spent the first day at Castel Gandolfo in the garden, writing, enjoying the loveliness of late spring. He produced a score of sheets of swiftly-scribbled manuscript bristling with emendations. The second day He summoned Cardinal Courtleigh directly after breakfast; and addressed him with some formality.
“We desire to establish relations with Your Eminency, chiefly because You hold so responsible a position in England, a country dear above all countries to Us which We design to treat with singular favour. In pursuance of Our intention, and of Our desire, certain matters must be defined. If Our words are unpleasing, Your Eminency must take them in the light of Our said intention and desire.”
The cardinal put on his cardinalitial mask. He was to hear and to note this rash young man. If anything needed to be said, he was there to say it.
“It is Our wish to make England ‘a people prepared for The Lord.’ We will attempt it of the whole world; and for this reason We begin with the race which dominates the world. We find Ourself impeded at the outset by the present habitude and conduct of English Catholics, especially of the aboriginal English Catholics.”
At this unexpected fulguration, this feline scratch, the cardinalitial eyebrows shot upward with a jerk and horizontally came down again. His Eminency slightly bowed, and attended. The Pope fingered a volume of cuts from English newspapers: selected a cut; and continued,
“Kindly let Us have your opinion of this statement:﻿—‘A remarkable petition has been prepared for presentation to Parliament. The petitioners are the Roman Catholic laity resident in England; and they pray Parliament to set up some control over Roman Catholic moneys and interests. It is pointed out that the total capital invested in the Roman Catholic clergy in the United Kingdom must amount to nearly £50,000,000. It is alleged that no account is afforded by the Roman Catholic bishops of the management or disbursements of such property and moneys. And the petitioners also call attention to gross injustices which are of daily occurrence.’ ”
“That emanated from a priest of my archdiocese, Holiness. It was a terrible scandal: but we were successful in preventing it from spreading.”
“Then there was such a petition? At first, We were prepared to ascribe it to the imagination of one of Sir Notyet Apeer’s young men.

===Chinese===

在冈道尔夫堡的头一天，祂在园子里写作，享受暮春的明媚。祂写满了二十来张匆匆挥就的手稿，满纸涂改，锋芒四起。第二天，早饭后祂径直召见考特利枢机，郑重其事地开了口。
“我们愿与阁下缔结一份情谊，主要因为阁下在英格兰身居要职；那个国家，于我们亲过世上一切国家，我们有意待以殊恩。为奉行我们的这番意向与心愿，有几件事须先界定清楚。我们的话若有不入耳处，请阁下凭我们上述的意向与心愿来听。”
枢机戴上了他那副枢机的面具。他要听，要记下这个莽撞年轻人的话。若有什么须答的，他正坐在那里答。
“我们的心愿，是叫英格兰成为‘预备好了迎接主的百姓’。这件事我们要在全世界试；而为这缘故，我们从这个主宰世界的种族下手。可一动手，我们就受了拦阻：拦在我们面前的，是英格兰天主教徒——尤其是土生的英格兰天主教徒——如今的习气与行径。”
这一记出其不意的闪电、这一记猫爪，惊得那两道枢机的眉毛猛地向上一挑，又横着落下来。阁下微微一躬，恭听。教宗翻着一册英国报纸的剪贴；拣出一条；接着说：
“请对这个说法赐我们一点高见：——‘一份为提交议会而拟的请愿书，殊堪瞩目。请愿人是旅居英格兰的罗马天主教平信徒；他们吁请议会设立某种监督，监管罗马天主教的款项与利益。文中指出，投在英国境内罗马天主教神职界名下的资本总额，必已近五千万镑。文中声称，罗马天主教的主教们对这笔产业与款项的管理与支出，从不提供账目。请愿人并提请注意种种每日都在发生的重大不公。’”
“那东西出自圣下教省的一位司铎。那是一桩骇人的丑闻：可我们总算把它压住，没让它散开。”
“那么真有这样一份请愿书？起初，我们还当它是诺耶特·阿皮尔爵士手下哪个年轻人编出来的呢。

===Original===

And really were there many supporters of the petition?”
“Unfortunately, yes.”
“Then you have rebellion within the camp. And was there any ground for these statements?”
“There was no ground whatever for the insinuation that we habitually misuse our trusteeship. The man had a grievance. His agitation was merely a means to compel us to solace him. He trusted, by making himself unpleasant to us, to make us pleasant to him. So he attacked our financial arrangements. It was a wicked stroke: for, you know, Holy Father, that we cannot be expected to account to any Tom-Dick-and-Harry for bequests and endowments which we administer.”
“Your accounts are properly audited, no doubt?”
“To a great extent, yes.”
“But not invariably? You trust much to the honesty and the financial ability of individual clerks? We do not presume for a moment that there is any systematic malversation of trust. You have had a lesson on that subject.”
“Lesson?”
“Yes: in 1886: after the notorious Carvale Case, when the infatuated imbecility of the Gaelic and Pictish bishops was shown to render them undesirable as trustees, the clergy simply dare not stray into illegal paths. Oh no. But are the clergy actually capable of financial administration?”
“As capable, I suppose, as other men.”
“Priests are not ‘as other men.’ However, We take it that you all believe yourselves to have acted conscientiously. We also take it that, in view of the power and influence which the position of trustee affords, your clergy eagerly become trustees and are unwilling to submit to supervision or to criticism. That is quite human. We entirely disapprove of it.”
“But what would your Holiness have?”
“We cannot say it in one sentence. You must collect Our mind from Our conduct as well as from our words. We entirely disapprove of the clergy competing for or using any secular power or dominance whatever, especially such power as inheres in the command of money. The clergy are ministers﻿—ministers﻿—not masters. And as to the other charge﻿—‘the gross injustices which are of daily occurrence’?”
“That, of course, is simply the scream of an opponent. It is spite.”
“Does Your Eminency mean that there are no injustices? Don’t you know of gross injustices?”
“ ‘It needs must that offences come.’ ”
“ ‘But woe to him by whom the offence cometh.’ Eminency, why not frankly face the predicament?

===Chinese===

当真有许多人附议这份请愿？”
“不幸，有。”
“那么你们营垒之内有了叛乱。这些说法可有凭据？”
“说我们惯常滥用托管之权，那影射毫无凭据。那人有他的委屈。他的鼓噪，无非是逼我们抚慰他的手段。他指望把自己弄得叫我们难受，好叫我们待他好。所以他攻击我们的财务安排。那是一记恶手：因为，圣父，您知道，我们受托管理的遗赠和捐助，总不能指望向张三李四一一报账。”
“你们的账目，无疑都正经审计过？”
“大体上，是。”
“可并非每笔都审？你们多多仰仗个别账房的诚实与理财的本事？我们一刻也不想假定那里有系统性的侵吞。你们在这一门上，是上过课的。”
“上课？”
“是：一八八六年：卡尔维尔案举世皆知之后——盖尔与皮克特那几位主教痴愚得叫人看出他们不堪信托——神职界如今简直不敢往非法的路上溜。哦不。可是，神职人员当真有理财的本事么？”
“同旁人一样吧，我想。”
“司铎不是‘旁人’。不过，我们且当你们个个自认行事凭了良心。我们也且当：既然受托人的位置自带权势与影响，你们的神职人员趋之若鹜地做受托人，又不肯受监督、受批评。这很合人情。我们完全不以为然。”
“可圣下要怎样呢？”
“一句话说不尽。你得从我们的言语里、也从我们的行事里，体察我们的心。我们完全不以为然的是：神职人员去竞争、去行使任何尘世的权力或支配，尤其是驾驭金钱的那种权力。神职者是仆役——仆役——不是主人。至于另一桩指控——‘每日都在发生的重大不公’？”
“那个么，自然只是对手的尖叫。是恶意。”
“阁下的意思是：没有不公？重大不公，您不知道么？”
“‘绊倒人的事是免不了的。’”
“‘但绊倒人的有祸了。’阁下，何不痛快正视这个窘境？

===Original===

The clergy are more than less human; and they certainly are not even the pick of humanity. Now, don’t they attempt too much in the first instance; and, in the second, don’t they invariably refuse to admit or amend their blunders? Listen to this. The *Pall Mall Gazette* states, on the authority of the *Missiones Catholicae* that, in Australia, during the last five years, we have increased our numbers from 3,008,399 to 4,507,980. But the government census taken last year gives the total population of Australia at 4,555,803. That leaves only 47,823 for the other religious and irreligious bodies. As a matter of fact, the latest Roman Catholic record is 916,880. Therefore an overstatement of 3,591,100 has been made. Which is absurd. And perpetuated. Which is damnable.”
“I do not precisely see Your Holiness’s point.”
“No? Well, let us go to another.” The Pope produced a small green ticket on which was printed,

> “Church of the Sacred Heart﻿—Quest Road﻿—Admit Bearer to﻿—Midnight Service﻿—New Year’s Eve 1900﻿—Middle Seat 6d.”

===Chinese===

神职人员不是超人，是人，甚至算不得人类里的精选。他们起先不是揽事太多么？接着，又何尝不是死不认错、死不改错？请听这一条。《帕尔莫尔公报》据《天主教传教纪事》称：在澳大利亚，近五年间，我们从三百零八万三千三百九十九人增长到四百五十万零七千九百八十。可去年政府的户口普查，给澳大利亚的总人口是四百五十五万五千八百零三。这么着，留给其余各宗教与无宗教团体的，只剩四万七千八百二十三。而实情是，罗马天主教最近在册的数是九十一万六千八百八十。于是多报了三百五十九万一千一百。这可笑。而且年年照报。这就可诅咒了。”
“圣下的要点，臣下不很明白。”
“不明白？好，换一条。”教宗取出一张绿色小票，上面印着：

> “圣心堂——奎斯特路——准持票人入场——除夕午夜礼拜——一九〇〇年——中座六便士”

===Original===

“This comes from Your Eminency’s archdiocese,” he said.
The cardinal looked at the thing, as one looks at the grass of the field. There it is. One has seen it all before.
“We disapprove of that,” said the Pope.
“What would Your Holiness suggest then to prevent improper persons from attending these services?”
“Improper persons should be encouraged to attend. No obstacle should be placed in their way.”
The cardinal was irritated. “Then we should have scenes of disorder, to say nothing of profanation.”
“That is where Your Eminency and all the aboriginals err. Your opinion is formed upon the apprehensive sentimentality of pious old-ladies-of-both-sexes whose ideal of Right is the Not-obviously Wrong. When a thing is unpleasant, they go up a turning: wipe their mouths; and mistake evasion for annihilation. They don’t annihilate the evil: they avoid it. Now, we are here to seek and to save that which was lost: and our churches must be more free to the lost than to the saved﻿—if any be saved. Experience proves that your pious fears have no sure warranty. Wesleyan schismatics have performed Watch-night services for more than a century. Anglican schismatics have done the same: and, in later years, they have celebrated their mysteries at midnight on Christmas Eve. We Ourself have assisted at these functions. The temples were open and free: and We never saw or heard a sign of the profanation of which you speak. Sots and harlots undoubtedly were present: but they were not disorderly: they were cowed, they were sleepy, they were curious, but they made no noise. Even though they had shouted, it only would have been in protest against some human ordinance; and a human ordinance must give way the moment it becomes a barrier between one soul and that soul’s Creator. Supposing means of grace to be obtainable in a church, who durst deny them to those who chiefly need them? The position which you clergy take up is an essentially false one. We are not here to establish conventions, or to enforce conformity. We are here to serve﻿—only to serve. We especially disapprove of any system which bars access to the church, or which makes it difficult;﻿—this admission-fee, for example.”
“Holy Father, the clergy must live.”
“You lead Us to infer that they cannot live without these sixpences?”

===Chinese===

“这出自阁下的教省。”祂说。
枢机看那玩意儿，像看野地里的草。它就在那儿。见惯了。
“我们不以为然。”教宗说。
“那圣下有什么法子，拦住不三不四的人来赴这些礼拜呢？”
“不三不四的人，正该劝他们来。什么障碍也不该挡他们的路。”
枢机恼了。“那我们就要有乱子了，更别说亵渎。”
“毛病正在这里：阁下同所有的土生子都错了。阁下的见解，是从那种虔诚的、雌雄皆有的老太太的忧惧感伤里养出来的；她们的‘对’的理想，就是‘看不出错’。一样东西叫她们不痛快，她们就拐进岔道：抹一抹嘴；把躲避当作消灭。她们没有消灭恶：她们绕开了恶。而我们在这里，是要寻找拯救失丧的人：我们的教堂，对失丧者的门，该比对得救者开得更大——倘若有得救的。经验证明，你们那种虔诚的忧虑，并没有稳当的保单。卫斯理宗的分裂分子行守夜礼拜，行了一个多世纪。圣公会的分裂分子也行：近年，他们还在圣诞夜半行他们的奥秘。我们本人就辅礼过这些场次。殿门大开，分文不取：我们从没见过、也没听过你们说的那种亵渎的一丝影子。醉汉和娼妇无疑在场：可他们不闹：他们怯着，他们困着，他们好奇着，可他们不出声。就算他们喊了，那也只会是抗议某条人定的规矩；而一条人定的规矩，一旦成了灵魂同灵魂造物主之间的障碍，就必须立刻让路。倘若恩典的手段在教堂里求得到，谁敢不给最需要的人？你们神职人员站的立场，根本是假的。我们在这里不是立规矩、强划一。我们在这里是服役——只服役。我们尤其不以为然任何把人挡在教堂外、或叫人难进教堂的制度；——比如这个门票钱。”
“圣父，神职人员总要活命。”
“您这是引我们推断：没有这一个个六便士，他们就活不成？”

===Original===

“We are so poor: we have no endowments: the fee is no more than a pew-rent for a single service﻿—”
“Lord Cardinal, be accurate. You have endowments: not equal to those of which you are thinking, the ‘stolen property’ enjoyed by the Church-of-England-as-by-Law-Established: but you have endowments. You mean that they are meagre. But pew-rents are abominable: so are pews, for that matter. Abolish them both.”
“I am bound to obey Your Holiness: but I must say that this quixotic impossible idealism will be the ruin of the Church﻿—”
“That is impossible: because Her Founder promised to be with Her always even unto the end of the world.”
“God helps those who help themselves﻿—”
“But not those who help themselves out of other people’s pockets.”
“The workman is worthy of his hire﻿—”
“Perfectly. But he accepts the wage: he does not dictate it. The builder of London’s new concert-hall in Denambrose Avenue did not let his masons domineer. He offered work at a certain wage. They took it, or left it. You confuse the functions of the buyer with those of the seller, as the clergy always do. Besides, as you seem fond of Scripture, ‘provide neither gold nor silver nor brass in your purses,’ and ‘take no thought for the morrow﻿—’ ”
“This is simply Tolstoy!”
“No. We never have read a line of Tolstoy. We studiously avoid doing so. We give you the commands of Christ Himself as reported by St. Matthew. Lord Cardinal, you are all wrong﻿—”
“Your Holiness speaks as though You were not one of us.”
“Oh no! The head looks down at the hands; and says ‘Your knuckles and your nails are dirty.’ ”
The cardinal really was angry. Hadrian paused: fixed him with a taming look: and continued “Is it right or even desirable that the clergy should engage in trade﻿—actually engage in trade? Look at the *Catholic Hour*; and see the advertisement of a priest who actually trades as a tobacconist.

“枢机大人，说话要准。你们有捐产：比不上您心里想的那种——国教作为‘法定教会’所享的那份‘赃产’：可你们有捐产。您的意思是：微薄。可座位租是可憎的：为那件事，座位本身也可憎。两样都废除。”
“我理当服从圣下：可我必须说，这种堂吉诃德式的、办不到的理想主义，要断送教会——”
“那不可能：因为她的创建者许过，要同她常在，直到世界的末了。”
“自助者天助——”
“可从别人口袋里自助的，天不助。”
“工人得工价是应当的——”
“十分应当。可工人是接受工价：不是规定工价。伦敦德南布罗斯大道新音乐厅的营造人，没有叫他的石匠们发号施令。他出一笔工钱。要便要，不要便散。您把买主同卖主的职分搅混了——神职人员向来如此。况且，您既然爱引圣经：‘腰袋里不要带金银铜钱’，还有‘不要为明天忧虑——’”
“这简直是托尔斯泰！”
“不是。托尔斯泰的书我们一行也没读过。我们有意躲着不读。我们给您的，是基督亲口的诫命，照圣玛窦所记。枢机大人，你们全错了——”
“圣下说话的口气，倒像不是我们中人。”
“哦不！头颅低头看手；说‘你们的指节和指甲脏了。’”
枢机真恼了。哈德良顿住：拿一副驯服的眼光盯牢他：接着说：“神职人员经商——当真下海经商，这正当么？连可取都说不上吧？看看你们的《天主教名录》：看那位司铎的广告——得了总主教的核准——情愿对投资照付银行利息，明明白白说：出息借钱，直接违背圣路加所记吾主在这题上的话。看看《天主教时报》：看那位司铎的广告——当真做起了烟草生意。

===Original===

Look in the precincts of your churches; and see the tables of the Fenian-literature-sellers and the seats of them that sell tickets for stage-plays and bazaars where palmistry is practiced﻿—”
“I merely interrupt to remind Your Holiness that Your august predecessor traded as a fisherman.”
“Very neat,” the Pope applauded, enjoying the retort: “but not neat enough. A fisherman’s trade is an open-air trade, and a healthy trade, by the way: but﻿—did Our predecessor St. Peter trade as a fisherman after He had entered upon the work of the apostolature? We think not. No, Lord Cardinal, the clergy attempt too much. They might be excellent priests. As tradesmen, variety-entertainers, entrepreneurs, they are failures. As a combination, they are catastrophes. These two things must be kept apart, the clerical and the secular, God and Mammon. The difference must be emphasized. By attempts at compromise, the clergy fail in both. As priests, they are mocked: and as for their penny-farthing peddling﻿—”
“But Holy Father, do think for one minute. What are the clergy to live on?”
“The freewill offerings of the faithful; and one must keep the other.”
“But suppose the faithful do not give freewill offerings?”
“Then starve and go to Heaven, as Ruskin says. That is what We are going to do, if possible.”
“How are we to build our churches?”
“Don’t build them, unless you have the means freely given. Avoid beggary. That way you sicken the faithful﻿—you prevent generosity﻿—”
“How shall we keep up those we have? For example, the cathedral﻿—”
“Yes, the cathedral﻿—a futile monument of one vain man’s desire for notoriety. How many lives has it ruined? One, at least, We know. How many evil passions has it inspired?﻿—the passion for advertisement by means of the farthing journalist, the critical passion which is destroying our creative faculty, the passions of envy and covetousness, the passion of competition, the passion of derision﻿—for you know that the world is mocking the ugly veneered pretentious monstrosity now. Better that it never had been. As it is, and in regard to the churches which exist, you must do what you can. If the faithful freely give you enough, then let them stand. If not, you must let them go. England never will lack altars. In any case, encumber yourselves with no more unpaid-for buildings. Accept what is given: but ask for nothing and suggest nothing.

===Chinese===

再到你们教堂的院里看看：看卖芬尼亚会书报的桌子，看卖戏票、卖义卖会入场券的座位——那种义卖会上还设着手相摊——”
“臣下插一句，只是想提醒圣下：圣下那位尊贵的前任，就做过打鱼的生意。”
“好利落，”教宗击节称赏，很受用这一反击：“可还不够利落。打鱼是露天的营生，顺便说一句，还是健全的营生：可是——我们那位前辈圣彼得，既已着手宗座的事业，还照旧打鱼么？我们想没有。不，枢机大人，神职人员揽事太多。做司铎，他们本可以出色。做买卖人、杂耍艺人、实业家，他们是废品。两样合做，他们就是灾祸。这两样必须分开：圣职的与尘世的，上帝与玛门。这分别必须挑明。想两头讨好，神职人员两头皆输。做司铎，他们被人嗤笑：至于他们那点鸡毛蒜皮的叫卖——”
“可圣父，请想一分钟。神职人员拿什么活呢？”
“信友自愿的奉献；而且这一个须帮衬那一个。”
“可倘若信友不奉献呢？”
“那就挨饿，进天国——罗斯金说的。我们自己就打算这样，若做得到。”
“教堂拿什么盖？”
“没有白给的款项，就不要盖。躲开乞讨。那条路叫信友作呕——叫慷慨绝了根——”
“已有的怎么维持？比方说，座堂——”
“对，座堂——一个虚荣人求出名的虚荣碑。它毁了多少条命？至少一条，我们知道。它挑起了多少恶情？——借小钱记者自我标榜的情，正在毁掉我们创造机能的批评情，妒情与贪情，竞胜情，嘲弄情——因为您知道，世人如今正嘲弄那座贴金粉、摆架子的丑八怪。不如从来不曾盖起。如今既已如此，既有的教堂，你们只能尽力。信友白给够了，就让它们立着。不够，就只好由它们去。英格兰从来不缺祭台。无论如何，再别背上一身没付清账的房产。给的收下：一点也不讨，一点也不示意。”

===Original===

Lord Cardinal, the clergy do not act as though they trusted the Divine Disposer of Events. They mean well: but their whole aim and object seems to be to serve God by conciliating Mammon. There is nothing more criminally futile. Instead of winning England’s admiration, you secure Her scornful toleration. Instead of consolidating the faithful, multitudes have become disaffected, and multitudes leave you day by day. Instead of improving the clerical character, (and, by consequence, the character of all who look to the clergy for example,) the clergy ever more and more assimilate themselves to the laity. The clergy should cultivate the virtues, not the vices, of humanity. Not one of us can tell which of our actions is important or unimportant. By a thoughtless word or deed, we may lead-astray a brother for whom Christ died. That is what is to be feared from your worldly clergy. Teach them that *magna ars* which St. Thomas of Aquino says *est conversari Jesu*. Teach them to rise above the world.”
“Surely, Holy Father, they do.”
“Some members of the clergy do, no doubt. We never met them. The tone of the clergy is distinctly worldly. Here is an illustration from your own newspaper. The very first thing which *The Slab* thinks worthy of note is ‘How Monsignor Cateran signally vindicated his honour and suitably punished his traducer, the proprietor of “The Fatherland.” The terms of the apology which Sir Frederick Smithers has had to publish in his own journal are set forth as a warning to evildoers.’ It is on p. 397. You know the particulars?”
“I have read them.”
“You cannot approve of the savage triumph of the letter on p. 416, in which Monsignor Cateran describes his victory: you cannot approve of the sneer at his enemy who ‘could not be punished by damages﻿—he has no means to pay,’ or the gibe at the freemasonry of the libeller, or the vicious malignant spite of the whole disgraceful document﻿—”
“But, Holiness, the libel was a dreadful one and grossly unjust.”
“But, Eminency, the accused was bound by his Christianity to suffer revilings and persecutions and the saying of all manner of evil falsely. He forgot that. In vindicating himself, he behaved, not as a minister of God but, as a common human animal.

===Chinese===

枢机大人，神职人员的行事，不像信得过那位统辖万事的上帝。他们用意不坏：可他们全部的目标，仿佛就是靠讨好玛门来事奉上帝。再没有比这更罪过的徒劳了。你们没有赢得英格兰的敬仰，只落得她轻蔑的容忍。你们没有巩固信友，大批人离心，大批人天天离你们而去。神职品格没有改善——连带一切奉神职为楷模者的品格也没有——神职人员反倒一天天同平信徒混成一路。神职人员该栽培人性的诸德，不是诸恶。我们中谁也说不准自己哪一件事要紧、哪一件不要紧。一句无心的话、一件无心的事，就可能带偏一位基督为他死了的兄弟。你们这些世俗神职的可怕，正在这里。教给他们圣多玛斯·阿奎那说的‘大艺’——‘与耶稣同在’。教他们高出世界。”
“圣父，他们自然是高出的。”
“神职里有些人无疑高出。我们没有遇到过。神职的调门分明是世俗的。这里有一条，出自您自家的报纸。《石板报》认为值得一记的头一件事，是‘卡泰兰蒙席如何堂堂捍卫了他的名誉，又如何得体地惩罚了他的诽谤者——《祖国报》的东家。弗雷德里克·斯密瑟斯爵士被迫在自己报上登出的道歉条款，全文照录，以儆效尤。’在第三九七页。详情您知道？”
“我读过。”
“第四一六页上那封信的野蛮得胜，您总不能赞成：卡泰兰蒙席在信里自述战绩；‘赔偿惩罚不了他——他没有钱可赔’这句对仇人的讥刺，您总不能赞成；对诽谤者那番共济会勾当的挖苦，您总不能赞成；那份丢尽脸的文件通篇恶毒的怨毒，您总不能赞成——”
“可是圣下，那诽谤骂得凶，又大大冤枉。”
“可是阁下，受诽谤的人既奉基督，就有本分忍受辱骂、逼迫、以及人假意捏造的种种恶言。他把这忘了。他为自己辩白时的行径，不是上帝的仆役，是一只寻常的人畜。”

===Original===

However, besides the so-called triumphant vindication of Monsignor Cateran, which *The Slab* glorifies in three separate columns, this same number bristles with improprieties. On p. 415, you have Dominican and Jesuit controversialists calling each other liars, and otherwise politely hating and abusing one another﻿—”
“Oh, Jesuits and Dominicans!”
The Pope put down the paper, and looked. The cardinal collected himself for a sally in force.
“Your Holiness will permit me to say that all this is extremely unusual. I myself was consecrated bishop in 1872, fourteen years before You were a Christian; and it seems to me that You should give Your seniors credit for having consciences at least﻿—”
“Dear Lord Cardinal, if We had seen a sign of the said consciences﻿—”
The cardinal tottered: but made one more thrust.
“I am not the only member of the Sacred College who thinks that Your Holiness’s attitude partakes of﻿—shall I say singularity﻿—and﻿—ha﻿—arrogance.”
“Singularity? Oh, We sincerely hope so. But arrogance﻿—We cannot call it arrogance to assume that We know more of a particular subject, which We eagerly have studied from Our childhood, than those do who never have studied it at all. Eminency, We began by saying that We desired to establish relations with you. Now, have We shown you something of Our frame of mind?”
“Certainly, Holy Father: You wish me to﻿—”
“We wish you to act upon the sum of Our words and conduct, in order that England may have a good and not a bad example from English Catholics. No more than that. We may call Ourselves Christendom till We are black in the face: but the true character of a Christendom is wanting to Us because the great promises of prophecy still lack fulfilment. The Barque of Peter has been trying to reach harbour. Muting within, storms without, have driven Her hither and thither. Is She as far-off from port today as ever? Who knows? But the new captain is trying to set the course again from the old chart. His look is no longer backward but onward. Lord Cardinal, can the captain count on the loyal support of his lieutenant?”
“Holy Father, I assure You that You may count on me.” It was an immense effort: but, when it came to so fine a point, the nature and the pride of the man gave way to the grace of his Divine Vocation.
“Well now, only one more blow from the flail, and then We will take up the crook.

===Chinese===

然而，除了《石板报》用整整三栏颂扬的所谓卡泰兰蒙席的大胜，同一期还满纸失检。第四一五页上，多明我会与耶稣会的论战家彼此骂‘撒谎’，又客气地互相仇恨、互相詈骂——”
“哦，耶稣会和多明我会！”
教宗放下报纸，看定他。枢机攒足了力气，预备全线出击。
“圣下容臣一言：这一切都极不寻常。臣下一八七二年受祝圣为主教，比圣下做基督徒还早十四年；依臣下之见，圣下总该信年长者至少也有良心——”
“亲爱的枢机大人，我们若见过那良心的一丝迹象——”
枢机晃了一晃：可又挺了一枪。
“枢机团里以为圣下这副姿态未免——该说是奇特——还有——呃——傲慢的，不止臣下一人。”
“奇特？哦，我们诚心巴望如此。至于傲慢——某一门学问，我们自幼热心研习，那些从没研习过的人不懂，我们假定自己懂得多一点，这算不得傲慢。阁下，我们开头说过，愿与阁下缔结情谊。现在，我们的心迹，可让阁下看见几分了？”
“看见了，圣父：您是要我——”
“我们要您照我们言语与行事的总和行事，好叫英格兰从英格兰天主教徒那里得一个好榜样，不是坏榜样。仅此而已。我们可以把自己叫作基督教世界，叫到脸黑；可基督教世界的真质，于我们尚缺，因为预言的大应许还悬着未应验。彼得的船一直在试着进港。内里的哗变，外面的风浪，把她刮来刮去。她今日离港比往日更远么？谁知道？可新船长正试着照旧海图重定航线。他的目光不再向后，而是向前。枢机大人，船长能指望副手的忠心支持么？”
“圣父，臣下向您担保：您指望得着我。”这是天大的努力：可事到这般细处，这个人的天性与骄傲，终于让位给他神圣圣召的圣宠。
“那么好，连枷再打最后一记，我们就拿起牧杖。”

===Original===

Do stop your Catholics from toadying the German Emperor. Read that. It’s perfectly absurd for them to tell him that ‘the whole Catholic world would be delighted if the protection of Catholics in the Orient were confided to him.’ He’s an admirable person: but We are not going to confide the protection of Catholics in the Orient to him. England is the only power which can manage Orientals. And what right have these Erse and Gaelic Catholics to speak for ‘the whole Catholic world’? Do neither England nor Italy count? Do make these pious fat-wits mind their own business﻿—make them understand that when they tell the Kaiser that ‘they will exert themselves to remove all misunderstandings between Germany and England’﻿—England last, you note﻿—they would be comical if they were not impertinent and entirely stupid﻿—and of course disloyal as usual.”
Hadrian collected His documents and the book of newspaper-cuts: swept them all into a portfolio; and abruptly changed the subject.
“Will Your Eminency be good enough to tell Us the circumstances which led to Our extraordinary election?”
Barely recovered from his commotion of mind, and posed point-blank like this, Cardinal Courtfield hesitated and said something about the Acts of the Conclave. His aboriginally tardy temperament was incapable of keeping pace with the feline agility of the Pontiff. Hadrian perceived his difficulty, and intently pursued the inquiry from another footing.
“We know all about the Acts of the Conclave, which We shall read at Our leisure. But We want the more human light which Your Eminency can throw upon the subject. Perhaps it will be simpler if We use the Sokratic method. By what means did Our name, did the mere fact of Our existence become known to the Sacred College?”
“By my means, Holiness.”
“We understand that Your Eminency actually proposed us to the Conclave?”
“That is so.”
“And We infer that you also recommended Us: or at least described Us in such a way that the cardinals knew whom they were electing?”
“Yes, Holy Father.”
“Why did Your Eminency propose Us?” the Pope purred.
The cardinal seemed to be at a loss again. He appeared to have a difficulty in expression, not a lack of material for expression. Hadrian made a dash for the rudiments.
“There were other names before the College? Why were none of their owners chosen?”
“It was impossible to agree about their merits, Holiness.”

===Chinese===

叫您的天主教徒别再巴结德皇。念念这个。他们告诉他‘若把保护东方天主教徒之权托付陛下，整个天主教世界将欢欣鼓舞’——荒唐透顶。他是个可敬的人：可我们并不打算把保护东方天主教徒之权托付给他。英格兰是唯一治得了东方人的强权。这几个爱尔兰盖尔天主教徒，又有什么权代‘整个天主教世界’发言？英格兰就不算数？意大利就不算数？务必叫这些虔诚的肥脑袋各人管各人的事——叫他们明白：他们告诉德皇‘愿尽力消除德英之间一切误会’时——您注意，英格兰搁在末尾——他们若不算放肆、不算十足的愚蠢，倒真滑稽——当然，照例还是不忠。”
哈德良把文件和那本剪报收拢：一古脑扫进一只文件夹；猛地换了话题。
“烦阁下告诉我们：我们那场奇绝的当选，究竟是怎样的经过？”
心里的震荡刚刚平息，又被这样迎面一问，考特利枢机迟疑了，支吾了几句枢机会议实录。他那土生的慢性子，跟不上教宗猫一般的敏捷。哈德良看出了他的难处，换个立足点，盯住不放。
“枢机会议的实录我们全知道，得暇自会细读。我们要的是阁下能给的、更近人情的光。或许我们用苏格拉底的法子更省事。我们的名字、我们这个人，凭什么进了神圣枢机团的耳朵？”
“凭我，圣下。”
“我们理会得：向枢机会议提名我们的，正是阁下？”
“是。”
“我们又推断：您还荐举了我们；或者至少把我们都描述了一番，叫枢机们知道自己选的是谁？”
“是，圣父。”
“阁下为什么提名我们？”教宗咕噜噜地问。
枢机又像没了主意。他的难处像在表达，不在材料。哈德良直奔根子。
“我们之前，枢机团里另有别的名字？他们的主人怎么一个也没选上？”
“他们的优劣，议不拢，圣下。”

===Original===

“Several attempts, no doubt, were made?”
“The Ways of Scrutiny and Access were tried seven times.”
“And then?”
“And then came a deadlock. None of the candidates obtained a sufficiency of suffrages: and none of the electors were willing to change their opinion.”
“And then?”
“The Way of Compromise was tried.”
“And, through Your Eminency’s means, the Compromissaries were induced to impose Us on the Sacred College?”
“Yes, Holiness.”
“Eminency, at the time when the Conclave first was immured, We hardly can have been in Your mind. It is improbable that you could have thought of Us then in this connection. At what point did We come into your calculations?”
“I ought perhaps to say that Your name had been brought before me some weeks before the demise of Holiness’s predecessor.”
“That would be in connection with the matter of which we treated in London.”
“Yes.”
“Precisely in what way was Our name brought before Your Eminency?”
“It was brought before me in a letter from Edward Lancaster﻿—a perfectly frantic letter accusing himself of all sorts of crimes. Your Holiness perhaps is aware what a queer person he is, rather inclined to be scrupulous, and most impulsive.”
“Yes, We know him. We Ourself would have said ‘unscrupulous’: Your Eminency uses the word ‘scrupulous’ in the Catholic sense, whereas We prefer frank English.”
“I mean that he is given to tormenting himself about fancied sins﻿—”
“And We mean that as a rule, he does nothing of the kind: but, like a good many others, is singularly successful in lulling his conscience. At least, for fifteen years he contrived to do so in this case. However, he now has made amends; and there is nothing more to be said. Let us continue. You received a self-accusing letter from Edward Lancaster. And then?”
“Not one letter, Holiness: a dozen at least. The injustice, of which You had been the victim, was on his nerves. He wrote me several letters; and came to see me several times. He is, as you know, a person of some importance and a great benefactor to the Church; and so I was obliged to take the matter up. I promised to investigate the case myself.”
“Yes. And you did.”
“I instituted an inquisitorial process among some of the persons who had had to do with Your Holiness; and I am bound to say that their replies gave me grounds for thought.”
“Why?”

===Chinese===

“想必试过好几回？”
“投票道与附议道，试了七回。”
“然后呢？”
“然后就僵住了。哪位候选人都凑不够票：哪位选举人也不肯改主意。”
“然后呢？”
“就试了折衝道。”
“于是，经阁下的手，折衝选举人们被说动，把我们强安在神圣枢机团头上？”
“是，圣下。”
“阁下，枢机会议闭门之初，我们多半还不在您心里。那时您不可能往这上头想到我们。我们是从哪一点进您的算计的？”
“或许该这样说：圣下的名声，早在先圣下崩逝前几个星期，就传到我这里了。”
“那该是我们在伦敦谈的那桩事。”
“是。”
“我们的名声，究竟以什么方式传到阁下耳中？”
“是爱德华·兰开斯特一封信带给我的——一封全然发狂的信，把他自己骂成各式罪人。圣下或许知道，那个人有多古怪，天容易起小节，又最冲动。”
“是，我们认识他。我们自己要说的词是‘无顾忌’；阁下用‘顾忌’，用的是天主教里的意思；我们偏爱直率的英国话。”
“我是说，他惯于为想象出来的罪折磨自己——”
“我们的意思是：照例他全不那样；倒是同许多人一样，哄自己的良心哄得异乎寻常地成功。至少这一桩，他哄了十五年。不过他如今已补了过；也就不必再说。往下讲。您收到爱德华·兰开斯特一封自责的信。然后呢？”
“不止一封，圣下：至少一打。圣下所受的那桩冤屈，压在他神经上。他写给我好几封信；还来看过我好几趟。他是——您知道——一个有身分的人，教会的大施主；我不能不把这事接下来。我应承亲自查一查。”
“是。您也查了。”
“我在同圣下有过往来的人里，启动了一套查问程序；我得说，他们的回话给了我想头。”
“为什么？”

===Original===

“They differed materially as to the details of Your history; and yet their opinion of You seemed to be fairly unanimous.”
“It was not a desirable opinion.”
“No, Holiness.”
“It would not be. We never were able to arrange to be loved. To be disagreeable was a sort of habit of Ours. But is Your Eminency able, from memory, to give Us an idea of these differences in regard to facts? Opinions do not matter.”
The cardinal pondered for a minute. “Yes, Holiness, I can give you three examples from Oxford. Fr. Benedict Bart said that he had met You twice personally: but that he had heard much of You from his friends, priests as well as laymen. He stated that all that could be done for You had been done; and that You were﻿—ha﻿—Your Holiness will pardon me﻿—a very incapable and ungrateful person.”
The Pope gave the little leaden weight of His pallium a swing: and beamed with delight. The cardinal went on.
“Fr. Perkins who received You into the Church said ‘I’m afraid he’s a genius, poor fellow!’ ”
“What rank blasphemy!”
“Blasphemy, Holiness?”
“Yes: blasphemy. Almighty God happens to make something a little out of the common; and, instead of praising Him for the privilege of tending a singular work of His, Fr. Perkins actually bewails the fact! But continue.”
“I confess I never thought of it in that light before﻿—”
“No: nor did Fr. Perkins. Continue.”
“I also took the opinion of a certain Dr. Strong who appears to be one of the superiors of the university.”
“He was senior Public Examiner in Honour Greats, if you know what that means.”
“Quite so. Well: he said that You had been his intimate and valued friend for more than twenty years, that You had had no influential friends to encourage You, and that Your abilities were no less distinguished than Your moral character.”
The Pope laughed again. “Dr. Strong is an experienced writer of testimonials.”
“But I should hardly think that a man in his position﻿—”
“Certainly not. Dr. Strong is one of the two honest men known to Us. Well: and how did the discrepancy between his statement and Fr. Benedict’s strike you?”
“It struck me in this way. How did so many worthy priests arrive at practically the same opinion, (for what Fr. Benedict said, others said also,) when their knowledge of facts seemed to be so superficial and so doubtful. “We do not call these things ‘accidents.’ ”

“圣父，那才叫离奇。我们这些折衝选举人，同神圣枢机团先前一样，议不拢。后来，一天会议散了，我忽然看出德拉沃尔塔枢机活像一个我见过、却忘了名字的人。那纯是偶然：可我一路绞着脑汁回去。当夜又出了第二桩怪事。我有些文件要签，走到公文箱跟前；纯属偶然，又翻着了爱德华·兰开斯特写来的、关于圣下的那几封信——”

“一八九四年。”

“那个我说不上：虽然我心里其实没多少疑问——不过，实情是这样。我心里装满了这案子，就在早晨的会上把它讲出来，当作一个例证，证明那架‘机器’会出错——这名字，我想正是圣下起的——对，正是——证明机器会出错。我永远忘不了我那番话在芒多枢机身上的效力。那才叫非凡。

He said﻿—I shall remember what he said as long as I live﻿—he said ‘My Lord Cardinal, you owe it to that man to propose him for the paparchy; yes you owe it!’ He rather upset me. I replied that Your Holiness was not even in sacred orders. He answered ‘Whose fault is that?’ I may say that the point was a very keen one. No one could fail to perceive its relevancy. To use a vulgar expression, it touched the thing with a needle. The others did not help me at all; and I considered the matter for a few minutes. Mundo went on, ‘If that man had a real Vocation, he will have persevered: if he has persevered, the twenty years or more of waiting will have purified﻿—’ ”
“Pray do not quote Cardinal Mundo.”
“Well, in short, I was irresistibly moved to propose Your Holiness﻿—”
“And then, because no other candidate was forthcoming: because﻿—We understand. You came to Us, found Us persistent﻿—”
“Yes, Holiness.”
“Well: shall we take a little stroll in the garden, and say some Office?”
Cardinal Courtleigh jumped. “I’m sure﻿—if Your Holiness doesn’t mind walking by the side of my bath-chair﻿—”
“Oh, but We do. It is Our invariable custom to walk behind bath-chairs and push them.”
“Indeed I could not for one moment permit﻿—”
“No: but for an hour you will submit. Nonsense man, do you suppose that one never has pushed a bath-chair before! Now sit-down quietly and open your breviary and read the Office; and We will look over your shoulder and make the responses. It’s awfully good exercise, you know.”

他说——他那些话我到死都记得——他说：‘枢机大人，您欠那人的，该提名他做教宗；对，您欠他！’他把我闹了个措手不及。我答说：圣下连圣品都还没受。他答：‘那是谁的过错？’可以说，这一点扎得极深。谁看不出它的分量。用句俗话，一针见血。旁人全不帮腔；我盘算了几分钟。芒多接着说：‘那人若有真圣召，他必坚持了：他既坚持了，这二十多年的等候必已炼净——’”
“请不必再引芒多枢机的话。”
“总之，我身不由己地被推动，提名了圣下——”
“随后呢，因为再没有别的候选人露头；因为——我们明白。您来寻我们，见我们不改口——”
“是，圣下。”
“好了：我们去园子里散散步，念念日课，好么？”
考特利枢机吓了一跳。“我确信——倘若圣下不嫌弃走在我轮椅旁边——”
“哦，我们正嫌弃。走空轮椅后头、推着走，是我们的老规矩。”
“那我实在一刻也不能容——”
“不：可这一个钟头，您就屈尊吧。胡说，伙计，您当人家从前没推过轮椅！好了，安安静静坐下，翻开日课经，念日课；我们在您肩后看，给您对答。这可是顶好的运动，您知道。”

## VII / 七
===Original===

After his morning’s exertions in the way of taming and domesticating a prince of the church, Hadrian was conscious that He required a change of emotions. His thoughts went to the next thing on His list﻿—the matter of Cardinal Nefski. That would be an exceedingly interesting experience. He did not want to intrude upon grief: but He was attracted by all singular phenomena; and the pathos of the pale young prelate seemed to be quite exemplary. Once in His secular life, George Arthur Rose had been taken by a doctor to see a man who had severed his throat in an unusual manner, using a broken penknife and cutting a jagged triangle, of which the apex missed the larynx, and the base the sterno-kleido-mastoid, avoiding by a hair’s breadth carotid and jugular. The doctor wanted a diagram of the wound made for the enlightenment of the jury which was to pronounce upon attempted suicide; and George had made the sketch from the staring speechless life, noted the furniture of the room and the aspect of his model, quite untouched by the man’s sensations or the horror of the event. Hadrian approached Cardinal Nefski with similar feelings. He was curious, He was psychically apart: but, at the same time, something of subconscious sympathy in His manner elicited the desired revelation. It was a ghastly one. Nefski, Cardinal Archbishop, had rushed to a little city in Russian Poland, occupied by anarchists, for the purpose of pleading with them. He arrived at sunset. There was a college there where a hundred and twenty lads of noble birth were being educated: among them, his own youngest brother, just seventeen years old. The cardinal was seized and crucified with ropes to the fountain in the market-square. Anarchists burst into the college: stripped its inmates naked; and flung them into the street before his eyes. He absolved each one dashed from the lofty windows. Some instantly were smashed and killed: others, who fell on others, were broken and shattered, but not killed outright. All night long, Nefski remained crucified. The anarchists must have forgotten him: for they left him; and at dawn someone, whom he did not know, came and cut him down. He remembered nothing more, until he found himself paralyzed, in a wagon with two priests, en route for Prague.

===Chinese===

清晨驯服、收服了一位教会亲王之后，哈德良自觉需要换一换情绪。祂的心思转到单子上的下一件——涅夫斯基枢机的事。那将是一段极其有趣的经历。祂不想闯进人家的悲恸：可一切奇异的现象都引祂；这位苍白青年高级神长的哀婉，又似乎堪作典范。还在世俗生活中时，乔治·亚瑟·罗斯有一回被一位医生带去看一个人：那人用一种不寻常的方式割了自己的喉咙——用一柄折断的铅笔刀，割出一个锯齿的三角，三角的尖避开了喉头，底避开了胸锁乳突肌，颈动脉和颈静脉都只差一根头发丝。医生要一张伤口的图，好开导那将要裁断自杀未遂的陪审团；乔治对着那具瞪目无言的活人写生，记下屋里的家具和模特的神态，对那人的感觉、对那事件的恐怖，全然无动于衷。哈德良就是怀着类似的心境走近涅夫斯基枢机的。祂好奇，祂在灵里置身事外：可同时，祂举止里某种潜意识的怜恤，引出了所要的自陈。那自陈惨极了。涅夫斯基枢机总主教，当时扑到俄属波兰一座被无政府党人占据的小城，为的是劝他们。他日落时赶到。那里有一所学校，一百二十名贵族子弟在其中受教：里面有他自己的幼弟，刚十七岁。枢机被抓住，用绳子钉上市场广场的喷泉，钉成十字。无政府党人冲进学校：把里面的人剥光；掷到他眼前的街上。他从高窗里每掷下一个，便赦免一个。有的当场摔烂摔死：有的落在别人身上，摔断摔碎，却没死透。整整一夜，涅夫斯基钉在那里。无政府党人想必把他忘了：因为他们丢下他走了；破晓时分，有个他不认得的人来把他放了下来。再往后他什么都不记得，直到发现自己瘫痪了，同一辆货车上的两位司铎在一起，正运往布拉格。

===Original===

Then he came on to Rome, hoping to lose the phantasm which continually occupied his sight and hearing﻿—the heap in the dark night, the growing groaning heap on red stones of white young bodies and writhing limbs like maggots in cheese, the pale forms strained and curved, the flying hair, the fixed eyes, continually falling, the cut-off shrieks, the thudding bounding ooze of that falling, the interminable white writhing. It was a ghastly tale, quite unimpassionately told. The young man still was in that stupor which benignant Nature sends by the side of extreme pain. His paralysis was passing away. He could walk easily now﻿—only he saw and heard. He spoke affectionately of his murdered brother: but he did not mourn for him.
Hadrian was moved. He put all the human kindness which he had, and it was not much, into His voice and manner. He really tried to comfort the cardinal. He quoted the splendid verses of the herald in the *Seven Against Thebes*,

> being pure in respect to the sacred rites of his country,

blameless hath he fallen, where ’tis glorious for the young to fall.

随后他来到罗马，指望甩掉那个盘踞在他视听里的幻影——黑夜里那一堆，红石头上越长越高的呻吟的一堆，雪白年轻的身体和蛆虫钻奶酪般扭动的肢体，苍白的人形绷紧、弯折，飞散的头发，定住的眼睛，不住地坠落，斩断的尖叫，坠落时沉闷弹跳的浆声，没完没了的白的扭动。这故事讲得惨极，却讲得毫无激情。那年轻人仍处在仁慈的自然安放在剧痛旁边的那种麻木里。他的瘫痪正在退去。他如今行走自如——只是看见了，听见了。他讲起被害的弟弟，口气恩爱：却不为他举哀。

Nefski seemed grateful. The Pontiff offered to remove him from Prague; and to attach him to the Court of Rome: but he preferred to return to his archbishopric for the present, at least, he said, until this tyranny be overpast. And, anon, he asked permission to retire. The sunlight dazzled him.
During the rest of the time at Castel Gandolfo, the Pope seldom was seen. A boatman rowed Him out on Lake Albano for an hour or two in the afternoon, while He occupied Himself in pencilling corrections on manuscript. But the white figure, set in the blaze of the sunny blue water, did not escape the notice of passersby on the high road near the Riformati; and, finding Himself under observation, He returned to the seclusion of the garden. His memory flew back to the time when people used to jeer at Him for His habit of writing letters, letters which explained a great deal too much, to blind men who could not see, to deaf adders who would not hear. He chuckled at the thought that those same people would read, mark, learn, and inwardly digest, every word and every dotted *i* of His letters now﻿—letters which were not going to be painfully voluminously conscientiously persuasive any more: but dictatorial. He wrote sheet after sheet; and emended them: He returned to His room and burned all the rejected preliminaries; and He took a fair copy with Him to Rome on the night of the twenty-eighth of April.
Early on the morning of the thirtieth, at a secret audience in the new throne-room, Caerleon introduced five rather startled very dishevelled and travel-stained priests, five priests who had undergone a mental shock. Mr. Semphill, with a white close-cropped head and the face of a clean pink schoolboy, contrived to remind himself that he was in the presence of the most amusing man he ever had met. He bucked-up; and made his obeisance with an aplomb which was a combination of the Service, Teddy Hall, an Anglican curacy and a Pictish rectory. Mr. Sterling, a stalwart brown schoolmaster, very handsome except for a mole on his nose, hid his feelings in calm inscrutability. Mr. Whitehead, a levelheaded commonsense Saxon, golden-hearted, who never had had any wild oats for sowing, observed reticence in a matter which was beyond his comprehension. Mr. Leighton, plump, clean, curly-haired, blinked genially and waited. Mr.

涅夫斯基像是感念。教宗提议把他调离布拉格；附设到罗马教廷：他却宁愿暂且回自己的总主教区——他说，至少等这场暴虐过去。过了一会，他请求告退。日光晃他的眼。
在冈道尔夫堡余下的日子里，教宗很少露面。午后，一个船夫载祂到阿尔巴诺湖上荡一两个钟头，祂便用铅笔改手稿。可那一袭白影，嵌在阳光蓝水的明晃里，逃不过里福尔马蒂近旁大路上过路人的眼睛；祂发觉自己受了观瞻，便回园中的幽静里去了。祂的记忆飞回当年——那时人们惯于嘲祂写信的习惯，写那些解释得太多的信，写给睁眼看不见的盲人，写给塞耳不听的聋虺。祂想到同一批人如今要读、要圈、要学、要“内心消化”祂信里的每一个字、每一个带点的 i——如今这些信，再不会写得那样苦口婆心、那样卷帙浩繁、那样苦苦劝化了：而是命令式的。祂一张一张写；改；回屋把废弃的草稿全烧了；四月二十八日夜里，带一份誊清的进罗马。
三十日清晨，新觐见厅里一次密见中，卡尔勒昂引见了五位相当惊惶、十分狼狈、风尘满身的司铎，五位受过一场精神震荡的司铎。森普希尔先生一头白茬短发，一张粉净的学童脸，还能提醒自己：此刻面对的，是他平生遇过最好玩的人。他打起精神，行礼的从容里掺着军官团、泰迪学堂、圣公会副牧师区和皮克特牧区的诸般气派。斯特林先生，一位棕色的健硕校长，除了鼻上一颗痣外十分英俊，把心思藏在镇静莫测里。怀特黑德先生，一位头脑平稳、常识充足的撒克逊人，一副金子心肠，从没有野燕麦可播，遇着超出他理解的事便守口如瓶。莱顿先生，胖胖的、干干净净、一头鬈发，和气地眨着眼等着。卡尔

===Original===

Carvale, a lithe intense little Gael, with the black hair and rose-white skin and the delicate lips and self-contained mien of a dreamer, looked upon his old college-acquaintance with clear eyes of burning blue. Some of the five had the remembrance of sins of omission at the back of their minds. None remembered sins of commission. All were wondering what was required of them﻿—what the devil it all meant, as Semphill secularly put it. If any of them expected allusion to the past, they must have been disappointed. Hadrian gave them no sign of recognition. It was the Supreme Pontiff Who very apostolically received them and addressed them.
“Reverend Sirs, Our will is to have such assistance in the work of Our Apostolature as the organs of sense can render to the mind, or as the experimentalist can render to the theorist. For reasons known unto Ourself, We have selected you. Believing you to be single-hearted in this one thing, namely the service of God, We call upon you to devote yourselves actually to the service of His Vicegerent. To this end, We would attach you to Our Person in a singular and intimate connection, by raising you to the cardinal-diaconate. Those of you who believe yourselves unable to do God-service better in this than in your present capacity, can depart without forfeiting Our goodwill. The conscience of each man is his own sole true light. Far be it from Us to interfere with any man’s prerogative as his own director in so grave a matter.”
The five remained standing, saying nothing. Semphill was sincerely delighted: the literary quality, the tops-i’-th’-turfy straightforwardness of the allocution gave him the keenest joy. The others felt obedience to be their plain duty: for George Arthur Rose never had been wantonly fantastic, there always had been a fundamental element of reason about his eccentricities, he never had revolved at random but always round some deliberately fixed point. And, to plain priests, the voice of the Successor of St. Peter was a call, to be answered, and obeyed.
The Pope addressed Semphill. “Your Reverency quite legitimately hoped to end your days at St. Gowff’s?”
“True﻿—(hum!)﻿—Holiness: but I may be translated elsewhere by a telegraph’s notice from my diocesan.”
“You are not yet a missionary-rector?”
“Merely a poor master-of-arts of Oxford.”
“But you have been at St. Gowff’s as long as We can remember.”
Mr.

维尔先生，一个柔韧、热切的小个子盖尔人，黑头发、玫瑰白的皮肤、梦游者般纤细的唇和自持的神态，用燃烧的澄蓝眼睛望着他大学时代的旧识。五人中，有的心底还压着疏忽之罪的记忆。谁也不记得有过错行之罪。人人都在纳罕要他们做什么——照森普希尔的俗话，这一切到底他妈的是什么意思。他们中若有谁指望提到往事，那准要失望。哈德良不露一丝相识的表示。接见他们、对他们讲话的，是至高教宗，一副宗徒气派。
“可敬的诸位司铎，我们的意愿是：为我们的宗座事业求得这样的臂助——如感官之于心灵，如实验家之于理论家。出于我们自己知道的缘由，我们选中了你们。既信你们在这一点上心地专一，即事奉上帝，我们召请你们，实际献身于事奉祂的代理人。为此，我们愿把你们系于我们本人，结一种独特而亲密的连带：擢升你们入枢机执事品。你们中间，凡信自己在如今的岗位上事奉上帝胜过在这个岗位上的，可以退去，不失我们的善意。各人的良心，是他自己唯一真光。我们万不敢干涉任何人在如此重大之事上自为指导者的特权。”
五人站着，不作声。森普希尔是真心欢喜：那篇训谕的文学素质、那“草皮尖上”的直爽，给了他莫大的乐子。其余几位觉得服从是明摆着的本分：因为乔治·亚瑟·罗斯从不无故出奇，他的古怪里总有一个理性的底子，他旋转从不乱转，总绕着一个刻意定下的轴。而对朴素的司铎们，圣彼得继任者的声音就是一声呼召：须应，须从。
教宗对森普希尔说话。“可敬阁下极合情理地指望，在圣高夫终老？”
“正是——（嗯！）——圣下：可我的主教一纸电报，就能把我调往别处。”
“您还不是传道区的主任司铎？”
“不过是牛津一个可怜的文学硕士。”
“可您在圣高夫待的年头，我们记事以来就没断过。”
森

Semphill choked a chuckle. “Having a little patrimony, Holiness, I made my will in favour of the archdiocese of St. Gowff’s and Agneda; and I did not omit to mention the fact to my archbishop. I happened also to say that, in the event of my being moved from St. Gowff’s, I should be compelled to make another will: but of course I did not contemplate being moved as far as Rome.”
Hadrian turned to Mr. Sterling. “The last words, which We said to Your Reverency, were that you had cause to be ashamed of yourself.”
“One had cause, Holy Father.”
“To you, Our invitation is a means of repairing a single small defect in a praiseworthy career.”
“It shall be repaired, Holy Father.”
To the others the Pope said nothing: for He saw their clean souls.
In the Sacred Consistory, the Supreme Pontiff dictated to consistorial advocates a pontifical act, denouncing the Lord Francis Talacryn, Bishop of Caerleon, as Cardinal-presbyter of the Title of the Four Holy Crowned Ones:﻿—the Lord George Semphill as Cardinal-deacon of St. Mary-in-Broad Street:﻿—the Lord James Sterling as Cardinal-deacon of St. Nicholas-in-the-Jail-of-Tully:﻿—the Lord George Leighton as Cardinal-deacon of The Holy Angel-in-the-Fish-Market:﻿—the Lord Gerald Whitehead as Cardinal-deacon of St. George-of-the-Golden-Sail:﻿—the Lord Robert Carvale as Cardinal-deacon of St. Cosmas and St. Damian. Then the six were brought in, and sworn of the College: their heads were hatted, their fingers ringed with sapphires, their mouths were closed and opened by the Pope; and they retired in ermine and vermilion.
What their emotions were, need not be inquired. Indeed, they had little time for emotion, seeing that during the rest of the day they sat in the secret chamber, writing writing writing from Hadrian’s dictation. In the evening, Whitehead and Carvale put on their old cassocks and posted a carriage-full of letters at San Silvestro. These all were sealed with the Fisherman’s Ring; and, as they were addressed to kings, emperors, prime-ministers, editors of newspapers, and heads of various religious denominations, it was considered undesirable to trouble Prince Minimo, the pontifical postmaster, with material for gossip.

对其余三位，教宗一字没说：因为祂看得见他们干净的灵魂。
在神圣枢机会议上，至高教宗口授宗座公文，由会议辩护师录写，册封：卡尔勒昂主教法兰西斯·塔拉克林阁下为枢机司铎，领衔四位圣加冠者堂；——乔治·森普希尔阁下为枢机执事，领衔宽街圣玛利亚堂；——詹姆斯·斯特林阁下为枢机执事，领衔塔利狱中圣尼古拉堂；——乔治·莱顿阁下为枢机执事，领衔鱼市圣天使堂；——杰拉尔德·怀特黑德阁下为枢机执事，领衔金帆圣乔治堂；——罗伯特·卡尔维尔阁下为枢机执事，领衔圣葛斯默暨圣达弥盎堂。随后六人被引进来，宣誓入团：他们的头戴上了帽，手指戴上了蓝宝石戒指，他们的口由教宗封了、又开了：他们穿着貂皮和朱红退了出去。
他们的感想如何，不必细问。其实，他们也几乎没有工夫感想：当天的其余时间，他们都坐在密室里，照哈德良的口授写呀写呀写呀。晚间，怀特黑德和卡尔维尔穿上旧日的黑袍，把满满一马车信件投进了圣西尔维斯特罗。信全用渔人权戒钤封；收信的既是国王、皇帝、首相、报纸主笔和各宗派的领袖，就不宜再拿闲话材料去麻烦宗座的邮政总管米尼莫亲王了。

Meanwhile Hadrian and Cardinal Semphill sat in the Vatican marconigraph office alone with the operators; and the Pope dictated, while the experts’ fingers expressed His words in dots and dashes in London and New York. By consequence, what His Holiness called “the five decent newspapers” came out on the first of May with an apostolic epistle, a pontifical bull, and editorial leaders thereupon.
The world found the “Epistle to All Christians” very piquant, not on account of novelty, but because of the nude vivid candour with which old and trite truths were enunciated dogmatically. Christianity, the Pope proclaimed, was a great deal more than a mere ritual service. It extended to every part of human life; and its rules must regulate Christians in all matters of principle and practice. He laid great stress on the assertion of the principle of the Personal Responsibility of the Individual. It was quite unavoidable, quite incapable of being shifted on to societies or servants. Each soul would have to render its own account to its Creator. In connection with the last doctrine, He denounced as damnable nonsense the fashionable heresy which is crystallized in the Quatrains of Edward Fitzgerald,

> O Thou, Who didst with pitfall and with gin

Beset the road I was to wander in,

Thou wilt not, with predestined evil, round

Enmesh; and then impute my fall to sin.

O Thou, Who man of baser earth didst make;

And, e’en with paradise, devise the snake;﻿—

For all the sin, wherewith the face of man

Is blackened, man’s forgiveness give﻿—and take!

这其间，哈德良同森普希尔枢机坐在梵蒂冈的马可尼电报房里，只同报务员在一起；教宗口授，专家们的手指把祂的话化成点和划，发往伦敦和纽约。于是，五月一日，教宗圣下所谓的“五家像样的报纸”，同时登出宗徒书信、宗座通谕，以及随之的社论。
世人觉得这篇《致一切基督徒书》很够味：不在新，而在那些陈旧的老生常谈，竟以信条的口吻、赤裸鲜活地直陈出来。基督教，教宗宣告，远不止一套仪轨事奉。它伸进人生的每一部分；它的规则，必须在一切原则与实行上统辖基督徒。祂重重压在一条原则上：个人的个人责任。它无可逃避，无可推给团体或仆从。每个灵魂，都须亲自向造物主交账。连带末一条道理，祂把时新的异端斥为可诅咒的胡话——那异端结晶在爱德华·菲茨杰拉德的四行诗里，

> 祢啊，祢用陷阱和罗网

> 围住了我将徘徊的路，

> 祢以命定的恶环绕了我，

> 却把我的堕落归罪于罪。

> 祢啊，祢用贱土造了人，

> 又连乐园一并设下蛇；——

> 人脸上熏黑的一切罪，

> 求祢给人的宽恕——给，也领受！
