单为剥夺你们那个借口——你们推卸对近人之责的借口——单为剥夺你们一个机会，把我归进你们疏忽所造成的那堆烂货——我自讨半饥半饱与全饥全饱，我一丝不苟地戒酒，我对女人几乎连客气话都难得说一句；我像被驱的奴隶那样劳作。不：我从不曾闲散。但我是个顶顶可怜的傻子。我从前以为，这种勤勉的苦修生活终了最合算。我错在没给格言“德行即是其本身之报”里那个“本身”二字应有的分量。除了一身勉强修成、却无可否认的德行，我没有别的报偿。有过一头魔鬼般的畜生对我说：‘我要有你那副脑筋，一年就挣一千镑。’我答：‘拿去：告诉我做什么：给我命令，我无不信从。然后，你拿那一千镑一年，分我二百；我一辈子给你祝福。’他一言不发；一事不做。他不过一个自大的骗子。我嘲弄他：当场拿住他偷我的信件——他的亲笔供状在这里；——于是他怀恨多年，写了这些匿名诽谤。”那可怕的、闪动的声音颤了一颤；——又更迅疾地亮下去。“我再说一遍，我从不曾闲散。我做了一件又一件工作。我设计家具、设计火具。我描绘圣徒与炽天使，也描绘罪人——主要是前者：在一段铤而走险的反叛期里，画过一组相当有趣而名号纷繁的魔鬼。我奴仆般做职业照相师，照着法国印片，为圣地幻灯片制一套底片，广告上号称‘据原底片’——《天主教时刻报》优雅地把我那段炼狱称作‘瞎混’。好，我承认是混、而且脏，在本条法律意义上也不卫生——可你们瞧，我是在为一个天主教徒干活。我干新闻，验尸采访一篇十八便士。我给杂志写稿。我写书。我发明了二十来样东西。专家们常对我说：这些发明里有一笔家财等着我：随便哪个资本家都会帮我开发。

===Original===

They were small people themselves, these experts﻿—small, in that they were not obliged to pay income tax: they had no capital to invest: but they recommended me, and advised me, to apply to lots of people who had:﻿—gave me their names and addresses, dictated the letters of application which I wrote. I trusted them, for they were ‘business men’ and I knew that I was not of that species. I quieted my repugnance; and I laid invention after invention, scheme after scheme, work after work, before capitalist after capitalist. I was assured that it was correct to do so. I despised and detested myself for doing it. I scoured the round world for a ‘patron.’ These were my ‘begging letters.’﻿—At that time I was totally ignorant of the fact that there are thousands of people who live by inviting patronage; and that most of them really have nothing to be patronized: while the rest are cranks. I knew that I had done such and such a new thing: that I had exhausted myself and my resources in doing it: that my deed was approved by specialists who thoroughly knew the subject. I was very ashamed to ask for help to make my invention profitable: but I was quite honest﻿—generous: I always offered a share in the profits﻿—always. I did not ask for, and I did not expect, something for nothing. I had done so much; and I wanted so little: but I did want that little﻿—for my creditors﻿—for giving ease to some slaves of my acquaintance. I was a fool, a sanguine ignorant abject fool! I never learned by experience. I still kept on. A haggard shabby shy priestly-visaged individual, such as I was, could not hope to win the confidence of men who daily were approached by splendid plausible cadgers. My requests were too diffident, too modest. I made the mistake of appealing to brains rather than to bowels, to reason rather than to sentiment. I wanted hundreds, or thousands﻿—say two: others wanted and got tens and hundreds of thousands. A cotton-waste merchant could not risk fifteen-hundred on my work, although he liked me personally and said that he believed in the value of my inventions: but, at the same time, he cheerfully lost twelve-thousand in a scheme for ‘ventilated boots.’ I myself was wearing ventilated boots, then: but the ventilated-boot man wore resplendent patent leather.

===Chinese===

这些专家自己都是小人——小到不必缴所得税：他们没有资本可投：却把我推荐来推荐去，劝我去找许许多多有资本的人：——给我姓名住址，口授我写的申请信。我信他们，因为他们是‘生意人’，我自知非其族类。我压下厌恶；把一样又一样发明、一套又一套方案、一件又一件作品，捧到一个又一个资本家面前。人人都担保这样做是对的。我一面做，一面鄙夷、憎恶自己。我跑遍全球找‘恩主’。这些便是我的‘乞讨信’。——那时我全然不知：世上靠邀人恩养过活的有几千人；他们大半实在没有一点配恩养的东西；其余是些怪物。我知道自己做成了某件新事：为做成它耗尽了自己和自己的资财：我的成绩得了深知此道的专家的认可。求人帮我使发明生利，我羞愧得很：但我全然诚实——慷慨：我总是提出利润分润——总是。我不白要、也不指望白得。我做了那么多；只要那么少：可那一点少我确是要的——为我的债主——为让我相识的几个奴隶松一口气。我是傻子，一个血热无知、可怜巴巴的傻子！我从不吸取教训。我照旧往前撞。一个像我这样形容枯槁、衣衫褴褛、面带怯色的教士相的人，斗不过天天缠着大人物的花腔大王。我的请求太讷、太谦。我错在向头脑呼吁而不向肺腑、向理性而不向感情。我要的是几百、几千——譬如说两千：人家要的是几万、几十万，也拿得到几万、几十万。一个废棉纱商人，不肯在我作品上冒一千五百镑的险——他个人喜欢我，也说相信我发明的价值：可是同一个人，痛痛快快在一项‘通气靴’方案上蚀掉一万二千。我自己当时就穿着通气靴：而通气靴那人穿的是锃亮的漆皮鞋。

===Original===

Cardinals’ secretaries could live at the rate of two-thousand-two-hundred-and-ninety pounds a year and borrow three-thousand-and-sixty pounds, on a salary of two-hundred pounds a year; and they could become bankrupt for four-thousand-one-hundred-and-twenty pounds with one-hundred-and-eighty pounds worth of assets. But I﻿—I could not get my due from that man, one of whose secretaries wrote his business to me on the franked notepaper of the late Queen of England’s Treasury: while the other, the bankrupt, gave me a winter of starvation, because his lord had altered his mind, quoth he, about the job on which I was working, and had determined to put his money into a cathedral. No. I never accomplished the whole art and mystery of mendicity. I perfectly could see what was required of him who would be a successful swindler. I was not that one. I was playing another kind of game﻿—unfortunately an honest one. Take that ‘unfortunately’ for irony, please. I mean﻿—but you perfectly know what I mean.﻿—I made nothing of my inventions. By degrees, I had the mortification of seeing others arrive at the discovery which I had made years before. They contrived to turn it into gold and fame. That way, one after another of my inventions became nulled to me. I think I am right in saying that there are only four remaining at the present moment. Finance them now? Engage in trade like a monk or a nun? No. No. I shall give them to﻿—that doesn’t matter. It shall be done today.﻿—Idle? Idle? When I think of all the violently fatuous frantic excellent things I’ve done in the course of my struggles for an honest living﻿—ouf! It makes me sick! Oh yes, I have been helped. God forgive me for bedaubing myself with that indelible blur. I had not the courage to sit-down and fold my hands and die. A brute once said that he supposed that I looked upon the world as mine oyster. I did not. I worked; and I wanted my wages. When they were withheld, people encouraged me to hope on; and offered me a guinea for the present. I took the filthy guinea. God forgive me for becoming so degraded. Not because I wanted to take it: but because they said that they would be so pained at my refusal. But one can’t pay all one’s debts, and lead a godly righteous sober life forever after on a guinea.

===Chinese===

枢机的秘书们能按一年二千二百九十镑的排场过活、以年薪二百镑之身借债三千零六十镑；还能以资产一百八十镑之数，宣告破产四千一百二十镑。可我——我连应得之款都拿不到，从那个人手里；他的秘书之一，用已故英格兰女王财政部的免资官笺给我写他的生意；另一位，那位破产的，给了我一个冬天的饥饿，因为，他说，他主上改了主意，不办我手头那件活了，决定把钱投进一座主教座堂。不。乞食这门全套的艺与秘，我始终没有学全。一个成功的骗子该具备什么，我看得清清楚楚。我不是那人。我玩的是另一种牌——不幸是诚实的那种。请把那个‘不幸’听作反话。我是说——你们全懂我的意思。——我的发明一无所成。渐渐的，我眼看别人到达我多年前已作出的发现，忍着羞辱。他们设法把它变成金子和名声。就这样，我的发明一样接一样对我作废。我想我没说错：此刻只剩四件了。如今再筹款开发？像修士修女那样去做买卖？不。不。我要把它们送给——那无关紧要。今天就办。——闲散？闲散？想起我为诚实营生苦斗的年月里干过的那一桩桩狂热而出色的蠢事——哦！叫人恶心！哦是，我受过帮助。天主饶我把这洗不掉的污点糊在自己身上。我没有勇气坐下、抱臂、死掉。有过一头畜生猜我：大约我把世界当作自己的一只牡蛎。我没有。我做工；我要我的工钱。工钱被扣住时，人们鼓励我继续希望；当场给我一几尼。我收下了那脏几尼。天主饶我堕落到这步。并非我要收：是因为他们说，我若拒绝，他们要难过的。可谁也不能凭一几尼还清全部债务，从此永远过敬虔、正直、清醒的日子。

===Original===

I was offered help: but help in teaspoonfuls: just enough to keep me alive and chained in the mire: never enough to enable me to raise myself out of it. I asked for work, and they gave me a guinea﻿—and a tacit request to go and agonize elsewhere. My weakness, my fault was that I did not die murdered at Maryvale, at St. Andrew’s College. The normal man, treated as I was ill-treated, would have made no bones whatever about doing so. But I was abnormal. I took help, when it was offered gently. I’m thankful to say that I flung it back when it was offered charitably, as the Bishop of Claughton offered it, and Monsignor﻿—you know whom I mean, Talacryn﻿—and John Newcastle of the *Weekly Tabule*. I’ll tell you about the last. He said that, being anxious to do me a good turn, he had deposited ten pounds with a printer-man, who would be a kind friend to me, and would consult me as to how that sum could be expended in procuring permanent employment for me. I took seven specimens of my handicraft to that printer-man. He admired them: offered me a loan of five pounds on their security. With that, I fulfilled a temporary engagement. Then I consulted the printer-man, the ‘kind friend.’ He proposed to give me a new suit of clothes, (I was to do without shirts or socks), to accept my services at no salary, and to teach me the business of a printer’s reader for three months; and, then, to recommend me for a situation as reader to some other printer. But, I said, why waste three months in learning a new trade when I already had four trades at my fingers’ ends? But, I said, what was I to live on during those three months? But, I said, what certainty was there at the end of those three months? But, he said, that he would ‘have none of’ my ‘lip, for’ he ‘knew all’ my ‘capers’; and he bade me begone and take away my drawings. Those were ruined: he had let them lie on his dirty office floor for months. Oh I admit that I have been helped﻿—quite brutally and quite uselessly. Helped? Yes. Once, when they told me at the hospital that I was on the verge of a nervous collapse, a Jesuit offered to help me. He would procure my admission to a certain House of Rest, if I would consent to go there. By the Mercy of God I remembered that it was a licensed madhouse, where they imprisoned you by force and tortured you. Fact! There had been a fearful disclosure of their methods in the *P.M.G.

===Chinese===

人家也给过我帮助：但那是茶匙量的：刚够吊住命、拴在泥里：从不够叫我自拔于泥。我讨的是工作，他们给我一几尼——外加一句无言的请便：到别处去受罪罢。我的软弱、我的过错，在于我没有死在玛丽维尔、死在圣安德鲁学院——被人谋杀而死。一个正常人，受了我受的那种虐待，早没二话地死了。可我不正常。帮助温温和和递来时，我接了。感谢天主：帮助以慈善的面目递来时，我扔回去了——克劳顿主教递的那种，还有那位蒙席——你知道我指谁，塔拉克林——还有《每周一览》的约翰·纽卡斯尔。最后这位我讲给你们听。他说，他一心想帮我一个忙，已把十镑存在一位印刷商那里；那人会做我的好朋友，会跟我商量这笔钱怎么花，好给我谋个长久营生。我带了七件手艺样品去见那印刷商。他赞赏它们：提出以此作抵、借我五镑。我拿这钱履了一桩短约。随后我去请教那印刷商、那位‘好朋友’。他提议：给我一套新衣（衬衫袜子免谈），接受我无薪的服务，用三个月教我校对这一行；然后把我推荐给别的印坊当校对。可是，我说，我指尖上已有四门手艺，何苦再花三个月学新行？可是，我说，这三个月我吃什么？可是，我说，三个月后有何保障？可是，他说，他‘不吃我这一套嘴，他清楚我那些把戏’；并叫我带着我的画滚蛋。画全毁了：他任它们在他龌龊的办公室地板上躺了几个月。哦我承认我受过帮助——受得残暴而全无用处。帮助？是。有一回，医院里告诉我，我濒临神经崩溃，一个耶稣会士要帮我。他肯保我进一所‘休养院’，只要我肯去。托天主的仁慈，我想起那是一家领照的疯人院，进去便被强禁、被折磨。事实！他们的手法曾有一篇骇人的揭发，登在《帕尔美尔新闻

===Original===

* Well: I refused to go. Rather than add that brand to what I had incurred through being Catholic, I made an effort of will; and contrived to escape that danger: contrived to recover my nerves; and I continued my battle.﻿—Regarding my pseudonyms﻿—my numerous pseudonyms﻿—think of this: I was a tonsured clerk, intending to persist in my Divine Vocation, but forced for a time, to engage in secular pursuits both to earn my living and to pay my debts. I had a shuddering repugnance from associating my name, the name by which I certainly some day should be known in the priesthood, with these secular pursuits. I think that was rather absurd: but I am quite sure that it was not dishonourable. However, for that reason I adopted pseudonyms. I took advice about adopting them: for, in those days, I used to take advice about everything, not being man enough to act upon my own responsibility. Also, the idea of using pseudonyms was suggested to me; and the first one was selected for me. As time went on, and Catholic malfeasance drove me from one trade to another﻿—for you know﻿—Talacryn﻿—Carvale﻿—Semphill﻿—Sterling﻿—that two excellent priests declared in so many words that they would prevent me from ever earning a living﻿—legal assassination, you see definitely was contemplated﻿—I say as Catholic malfeasance drove me from one trade, I invented another, and another; and I carried on each of these under a separate pseudonym. In fact I split up my personality. As Rose I was a tonsured clerk: as King Clement, I wrote and painted and photographed: as Austin White, I designed decorations: as Francis Engle, I did journalism. There were four of me at least. I always have thought it so inexplicable that none of the authorities﻿—you, Talacryn, with your pretended confidence in me and your majestic immobility towards me﻿—that none of you ever realized the tremendous amount of energy which was being expended, misdirected, if you like. Certainly no one of you ever made a practical attempt to direct that energy. I was a like a wild colt careering round and round a large meadow. You all looked on and sneered ‘Erratic!’ Of course I was erratic, for you all did your very best, by stolidity, hints, insinuations, commands, to create obstacles over which I had to jump, through which I had to tear a way; and there was no one to bit and bridle me, to ride me, and to share his couch with me.

===Chinese===

报》上。好：我拒绝了去。与其给因皈依天主教而受的烙印再添一枚，我发一次意志之力；躲过了那危险：救回了我的神经；继续我的战斗。——至于我的化名——我那一大把化名——请想想这个：我是个剪过发的前驱，立志坚守我的神圣圣召，却被迫一度从事世俗营生，既为糊口也为还债。我一想到要把自己的名字——那个我确信终有一日要在司铎职位上为人所知的名字——同这些世俗营生连在一起，就战栗作呕。我想这相当荒唐：但我确信它并非不名誉。总之为此我用了化名。用化名这件事我请教过人：因为那时我凡事请教，还没种到自己担责任的境地。而且用化名的念头也是旁人提醒我的；头一个还是别人替我选的。后来，天主教的失德把我从一行赶到另一行——你们知道的——塔拉克林——卡瓦尔——森普尔——斯特林——两位出色的司铎明白宣布，要断我一切生路——你看，合法的暗杀确确实实酝酿过——我说，天主教的失德把我从一行赶出来，我就另发明一行，又一行；每行各用一个化名。实际上我把人格拆开了。作为罗斯，我是剪过发的教职人员：作为克莱门王，我写作、作画、照相：作为奥斯汀·怀特，我设计装饰：作为弗朗西斯·恩格尔，我跑新闻。至少有四个我。我一直觉得不可解：当局中人——你，塔拉克林，还有你对我那副假装的信任、那副雄伟的不动——竟无一人意识到那股正在倾泻的精力有多大——你们可以说它用错了地方。你们中当然也无一人做过什么实际的尝试来引导那股精力。我像一匹野驹，绕着大草场一圈圈狂奔。你们全体旁观、嗤笑：‘没常性！’我当然没常性：因为你们全力以赴，用麻木、暗示、讥刺、命令，造出一道道障碍，叫我跳、叫我撞开路；而没有一个人来给我上嚼口缰辔，骑我，与我同衾。

===Original===

And of course my pseudonymity has been misunderstood by the stupid, as well as mispresented by the invidious. Most people have only half developed their single personalities. That a man should split his into four and more; and should develop each separately and perfectly, was so abnormal that many normals failed to understand it. So when ‘false pretences’ and similar shibboleths were shrieked, they also took alarm and howled. But, there were no false pretences. I told my name to everyone whom it concerned. I am not the only person who has traded under pseudonyms or technikryms. Take, for example, the man whose shop I am said to have offered to buy. He himself used a trade-name. He begged for my acquaintance when I was openly living as a tonsured clerk, about a couple of years before my first pseudonym even was thought of. Take, for another example, those priests, Fr. Aleck of Beal, and the Order of Divine Love, who are alleged to have ‘charitably maintained’ me. By the way, they never did that. They always were paid for my entertainment, in hard coin, and their own price﻿—always. And the Fathers of Divine Love refused me shelter for one night in 1892 at the very time when they are said to have ‘charitably maintained’ me. They did suggest a common lodging-house at fourpence, though; and I flung back the suggestion in their faces and walked the streets all night. But all these people knew all about me and my pseudonyms. In fact, the very priest who suggested the common lodging-house, was the man on whose advice I adopted my first pseudonym. It was invented by an old lady who chose to call herself my grandmother: she was that priest’s patron and penitent. It was approved by him and adopted by me. And there you have the blind and naked truth on that point. It now is pretended that ‘King Clement’ was a Jesuitical machiavellian device of mine, implying royalty, dominions, wealth, and interminable nonsense. I think that the pretension is due to malice and imbecility. It is malignant now: but I firmly believe that it began by being imbecile. I confess that the name, taken together with my domineering manner, my pedantic diction, my austere and (shall I say) exclusive habit, was liable to misconstruction by the low coarse half-educated uncultured boors among whom I lived. It’s an example of the ‘Eidola Fori,’ the strange power of words and phrases over the mind.

===Chinese===

我的化名身份，自然既被愚人误解，又被尖刻者歪曲。多数人连单一的人格也只开发了一半。一个人把自己的拆成四份、更多份，各自分别开发到完满，这太反常，许多正常人便不能懂。于是一喊起‘虚饰’之类的口令，他们也跟着惊叫。可是，没有任何虚饰。凡与我有关的人，我个个告知了真名。用化名、用行号做买卖的，也不是我一人。譬如，据说我曾想盘下的那家铺子的主人。他自己用的就是营业字号。他在我公开以剪发教职身份生活时就攀交我——那还在我头一个化名被想起之前两年。再譬如那几位神父，比尔的亚力克神父和圣爱会，据说曾‘慈善供养’过我。顺便说，从无此事。我的食宿，他们回回照价收取现钱——他们自己的定价——回回如此。而圣爱会的神父们在一八九二年拒绝我宿一夜，恰在据称他们‘慈善供养’我的那阵子。倒是提议过一家四便士的平民寄宿舍；我把这提议摔回他们脸上，走了整夜街头。可这班人对我、对我的化名全知道底细。其实，提议平民寄宿舍的那位神父，正是劝我采用头一个化名的人。那名字是一位自愿认我作孙儿的老太太发明的：她是那神父的恩主和忏悔者。经他核准，经我采用。这便是那一点上赤裸裸的盲目真相。如今又有人宣称，‘克莱门王’是我一条耶稣会式的马基雅维利诡计，暗指王位、领土、财富，以及无休无止的胡说。我认为这宣称出于恶意加低能。它如今是恶毒的：但我坚信它起头只是低能。我承认，这个名字，连同我的专断作风、我的掉书袋谈吐、我那严谨（要不要说？）孤高的习惯，容易被我混居其中的粗鄙半吊子莽汉误解。这是‘市场偶像’的一例：字词短语支配人心的奇异力量。

===Original===

I think it really was believed, in some vague way, that I was an exiled sovereign or some rot of that sort. I believe that I perceived it; and laughed to myself about it. But I did my best to disabuse the fools of their foolery. That made things worse. Liars themselves, they could not conceive of a man speaking truth to his own detriment. My disclaimer was taken for a lie; and they honoured me the more for it; and chuckled at the thought of their own perspicacity:﻿—that is to say, when what I said was intelligible to them. You see I used to be a great talker. I have had many experiences; and I used freely to talk of them. It amused and instructed; and I like to amuse and to instruct. You will understand that my voice and my manner of speech did not resemble the voice and the manner of speech of the ruffians with whom I worked and lived. Live as poorly as I would, dress as shabbily as I would, the moment I opened my mouth I was discovered to be different to those people. They perceived it; and I never could disguise my speech. Also, I’m quite sure that they could not understand my speech﻿—follow my argument. I used words which were strange to them to express ideas unimagined by them, while their half-developed minds were more than half occupied, not in listening to me but, in contemplating me, and in trying to form their particular idea of me by the aid of the *Vulgi sensus imperiti*, the imperfection of undisciplined senses, at their disposal. I called that Imbecility. Perhaps Ignorance is the apter term. The Malice is to be found among people who ought to know better: people to whom I have told the exact truth about myself, exact at the time of telling: people, who being possessed by a desire to think evil, think evil: people who read between, instead of on, the lines: people, prone to folly, whom I have not helped to avoid their predilection. I tried to be simple and plain, to sulk (if you like) in my own corner by myself. It was no good. Anyhow, I told no tales of realms or wealth as mine. I made no false pretences. I myself was grossly deceived: barbarously man-woman-and-priest-handled. I was foolish to try to explain myself. I was foolish to try to work with, to live with, to equal myself in every respect with, verminous persons within the meaning of the act. I ought to have died. But I did not die. That is all. It is not half. Now you know.

我想，真有人隐隐相信我是流亡的君主，或那一类的什么货色。我相信我觉察出来了；暗自好笑。但我尽力叫傻子们丢开他们的愚。那反而更糟。他们自己是骗子，便想不出一个肯对自己不利还说真话的人。我的自澄清被当作谎；他们因此愈发敬我；为自己的眼力得意：——就是说，当我所说的他们听得懂时。你们知道，我从前是个很健谈的人。我的阅历多；惯于放谈。谈着有趣有教益；我就喜欢有趣有教益。你们会理解：我的嗓音和谈吐，同我一起干活、一起生活的那些粗人的嗓音谈吐不类。任我住得再穷、穿得再破，一开口就被听出异样。他们觉出来了；我从来遮不住口音。还有，我确信他们听不懂我的话——跟不上我的论证。我用他们陌生的字眼，表达他们无从想象的思想；而他们那半开的头脑，大半不在听我，而在打量我，想凭*Vulgi sensus imperiti*（庸众粗疏之感官）、凭他们那未经训练的感官之不足，拼出他们对我的成见。我把那叫作低能。或许‘无知’更贴切。恶意倒出在本该明白些的人中间：出在我把自身实情原原本本告诉过的人中间——当时句句是实：出在被思恶之欲盘踞、故而思恶的人中间：出在读字缝而不读字面的人中间：出在那班性喜愚妄、我又不曾相助避开的人中间。我尽力简单平白，独自（随你们怎么说）蔫在自己的角落里。没有用。无论如何，我没吹过什么国土财富为我所有。我没有虚饰。被狠狠骗了的正是我自己：被野蛮地——被男人的手、女人的手、神父的手——摆布过。我试着解释自己，是蠢。我试着同虫豸共事、同住、处处与之拉平，是蠢——本条法律意义上的虫豸。我本该死。可我没死。这就是全部。还不止一半。如今你们知道了。

===Original===

Make what you please of it.”
“Tell me,” Gentilotto instantly said: “Why did you never go to the Trappists?”
“Because I went to something worse, to something infinitely terribly more ghastly. Trappists live in beautiful silent solitude; they have clean water, beds, regular meals, and peace. I went to live in intellectual silence and solitude in an ugly obscene mob, where clean water was a difficulty, food and a bed an uncertainty, and where I had the inevitable certainty of ceaseless and furious conflict.”
He hurled the words like javelins, and drew back in his chair. The old bitter feeling of disgust with himself inspired him. He feared lest perhaps he might have seemed to be pleading for sympathy. So he angrily watched to detect any signs of a wish to insult him with sympathy. But he really had gone far, far beyond the realm of human sympathy. *There was not a man on the earth who would have dared to risk rebuff, to persist against rebuff, to soar to him with that blessed salve of human sympathy﻿—for which﻿—underneath his armour﻿—and behind his warlike mien﻿—he yearned.* Pity perhaps, horror perhaps, dislike perhaps, might have met him. But he only had emphasized his own fastidious aloofness. He had cleared-off the mire: but he had disclosed the cold of marble, not the warmth of human flesh.
The cardinals remained silent for a minute. Then Ragna said “ ‘An enemy hath done this!’ Who is it?”
George blazed with vigorous candid delight. “That is the first genuine word which I have had from the heart of Your Eminency!”﻿—He returned to his repellent manner. “I gave the names of my calumniators to Cardinal Leighton.”
“Jerry Sant the Liblab, aided by the woman and a clot of worms who had turned;” Leighton said to Ragna.
“Let them be smothered in the dunghill. *Anathema sint.*” Ragna growled.
Again there was an exposition of silence in the throne-room. George was frozen hard and white. Ragna and Leighton continued to look at each other. Carvale’s eyes had the blue brilliance of wet stars. Saviolli, Semphill, Talacryn, Whitehead, were as though they had seen the saxificous head of the Medoysa. Stirling gazed straight before him, in the manner of the sphinx carven of black basalt. George was watching them with half-shut eyes from the illimitable distance of his psychic altitude.

你们随意处置罢。”
“告诉我，”真蒂洛托立刻说：“您为什么从没去入特拉普派？”
“因为我去过更坏的、坏到无限可怕的地方。特拉普派住在美丽安静的孤独里；有干净的水、床、按时开饭，还有平安。我住的却是智识的死寂与孤独——在一群丑恶淫秽的暴民中间，那里干净水是难题、饭和床无着落，而且有一桩躲不掉的确定：无休无止的狂暴冲突。”
他掷出这些话，如掷标枪，随即往椅背一缩。对自己旧有的苦恶之感又支配了他。他怕自己方才像是在乞求同情。于是他怒气冲冲地监视，捕捉任何一丝以同情侮辱他的意思。可他实在早已远远超出了人间同情的疆域。*世上没有一个男人敢冒碰壁之险、一挫再挫地坚持、带着人类同情那贴福膏飞升到他身上——而在他的甲胄底下、在他好战的仪表背后，他渴望的正是它。*也许怜悯、也许恐怖、也许厌恶，还迎得到他。可他只把自己那副挑剔的孤高愈发钉实了。他擦净了泥污：露出来的却是大理石的冷，不是人肉的暖。
枢机们静了一分钟。随后拉尼亚说：“‘这是仇人干的！’是谁？”
乔治焕发出雄健坦然的光。“这是我从阁下心里得到的第一句真话！”——他随即回到那拒人的样子。“诽谤我者的名单，我给了莱顿枢机。”
“自由工党的杰里·桑特，加上那女人，加一群倒戈的虫子；”莱顿对拉尼亚说。
“把他们闷死在粪堆里。*Anathema sint.*（当受诅咒。）”拉尼亚低吼。
觐见殿里又是一阵死寂。乔治冻得又硬又白。拉尼亚同莱顿继续对望。卡瓦尔的眼睛带着湿星星的蓝光。萨维奥利、森普尔、塔拉克林、怀特海，仿佛看见了那颗石化的美杜莎头。斯特林直望着前方，像黑玄武岩雕的斯芬克斯。乔治从他那精神高度的无限远处，半阖着眼，看他们。

Presently, the pure pale old face of Gentilotto and the pure pale young face of Van Kristen simultaneously were lifted; and their eyes met His. He blushed: slowly drew out the pontifical ring: and put it on His finger.
“Lord Cardinals, it is Our will to be alone:” the Supreme Pontiff said.
They came one by one and kissed His ring; and retired in silence.

“枢机大人们，我们愿独处：”至尊的教宗说。

## XXIII / 二十三
===Original===

When the door was shut, Hadrian remained quite motionless on the throne; and set Himself to review what He had said. He wondered whether He for once had got-down to and laid-bare the root of the matter: whether He for once had made His argument clear and convincing.﻿—Good God! Who even could hope to be convincing?﻿—He flung the thing away from Him; and forever closed that volume of the book of His life.
He rose; and went straight into the bedroom. Here He stripped, and stood erect, knees and feet close: gripped a pair of ten-pound dumbbells; and swung them with the alternating gesture of a right and left overhand bowler, rhythmically swaying from the hips. He counted up to a hundred; and went to another movement: a full round overhead sweep of both arms together, expanding the long-breathing lungs, quickening the pulses, brightening the eyes. His skin became moist and warm. He washed His face and hands in oatmeal-water with no soap; and went into the bathroom, turning on the high tap and letting the cold soft water rain-down upon Him until He was numbed. He quickly dried Himself; and put on completely clean clothes, rolling up those which He had discarded and thrusting them into a linen bag. Then, He emerged all flushed and white and fresh; and summoned Sir Iulo to the secret chamber.
“And so you are thinking of marriage, carino;” Hadrian said, putting the young man into a chair and bestowing fumificables.
Sir Iulo went almost as scarlet as his uniform: his eyes and teeth gleamed. Hadrian handed to him a sheet of paper containing six stanzas of passionate expression in rhyme, under the heading “*Vorrei che tu ascoltassi la mia voce*.”
“Don’t leave your sonnets about. And don’t be so terrified, you silly boy. Well: is it true?”
The lover’s face twitched rather. “I l‑o‑v‑e her,” he said with an enormous vocal expansion of the middle word. “But I will not to abandon You, Santità:” he added with fixed eyes.
“Who is she? Is she good? Has she any money?”
“She is the little daughter of the dentist. But good? But, yes. But no money:” was the categorical reply.
“Does she love you?”
“Oh, but how she loves me!”
“How long have you known her?”
“Since Christmas, Santità, when the father of that has scaled the my tooths.”
“Have you spoken to ‘the father of that’ about ‘that’?”
“Oh, but not yet, Santità. Nothing of less, he knows. I gave him to know without the word.”

===Chinese===

门关上以后，哈德良在宝座上纹丝不动；开始回想自己说过的话。祂纳闷：这一回，自己可曾下到根、把根刨露出来？这一回，可曾把论辩说得清楚、说得服人？——天主啊！谁敢指望服人？——祂把那东西整个抛开；把祂生命之书的这一卷，永远合上了。
祂起身；径直进了卧房。在那里祂脱光衣服，笔直站定，两膝两脚并拢：抓起一对十磅重的哑铃；照右臂左臂轮流上手投球的姿势抡动，胯部有节奏地摆。数到一百；换一个动作：双臂齐举，头顶绕一整圈，叫长呼吸的肺张开，脉博加快，眼睛发亮。皮肤变得温热潮润。祂用燕麦水不用肥皂洗净脸和手；进浴室，拧开高处的水龙头，让冷软的水下雨般浇在身上，直到麻木。祂迅速擦干；换上全然干净的衣服，把换下的卷起塞进一只布袋。随后祂走出来，浑身通红、雪白、新鲜；召尤洛爵士进密室。
“这么说，你在想结婚的事了，小亲亲；”哈德良说，把年轻人按进椅子里，递上烟具。
尤洛爵士红得几乎同他的制服一个颜色：眼睛和牙齿闪着光。哈德良递给他一页纸，上头是六节押韵的炽烈表白，题目是“*Vorrei che tu ascoltassi la mia voce*（愿你得听我的声音）”。
“别把你的十四行诗乱放。也别吓成这样，傻孩子。嗯：是真的么？”
恋人的脸抽动了一下。“我——爱——她，”他说，把当中那个字拖得极响亮。“但我不抛弃您，圣下：”他直勾勾补上一句。
“她是谁？人品好么？有钱么？”
“她是那位牙医的小女儿。人品？呃，好。钱：没有：”是斩钉截铁的回答。
“她爱你么？”
“哦，她多么爱我！”
“你认识她多久了？”
“打圣诞节起，圣下，从她爸爸给我洗了牙那时起。”
“你同‘她爸爸’提过‘那件事’么？”
“哦，还没有，圣下。反正他知道。我没开口就叫他知道了。”

===Original===

“And he didn’t drive you out of the house?”
“But no: for behold me not the assassin of that dentist.”
Hadrian laughed. “Can you describe her?”
“Oh that I might to describe her to one who is so dear, so wise﻿—”
“Describe her.”
“Is named Evnica. Is example of goodness, of intellectuality. For example: yesterday with the favour of the Most Holy I make a visit. I am entering the saloon in the manner of cat, softly, softly. Behold in a book reads the Signorina Evnica﻿—not book of novels, not journal of *Don Chisciotte*. No. I look over her shoulder, reading titles. Behold, book of piety entitled *Office to the Proximate*﻿—”
“*Office to the Proximate*? What book of piety is that?”
Sir Iulo repeated the title in Italian.
“Ah yes, *The Duty Towards Our Neighbour*. Yes: a very good sign in a girl. Go on.”
Sir Iulo fixed his bright green eyes upon a mental image; and described each point as he observed it, using his gorgeously florid Tuscan idiom. “Has a face to make burn Jove, and to return to ram, eagle or bull; and to make scorn to medals old and new. Blond she has the hair like thread of gold. The cheeks appear like a rose damasked. The mouth and the eyes are worth a treasure. Has looks angelic, divine: but in the effects and all the motions, human; and the her excellencies not have end. She has what they call a good and fine hand: is white like snow of mountains. Is literate; and makes to talk Tuscan; and in life not a flaw can be found. There is not who better to a swan understands me. Does great things, enough facts, little eats: not drinks never in the middle of eating and not at afternoon-tea (merenda). More, I say. She is in her proper acts so learned, that all I have in the world, or small or great, I should have given to her pleasure at a stroke. The more beautiful to my day I never saw: none more servitial: none more prudent: nor acts in a girl more courteous and gay. Has Petrarch and Dante in her hand; and, at time and place if I command, she vomits a little sonnet lightly. Girl of all perfect qualities; and holds me in pledge there if mine﻿—”
“Well now: suppose that you marry her, will you be good to her?”

“《近人之务》？那是什么敬虔书？”
尤洛爵士用意大利语把书名重说了一遍。
“啊，是了，《对近人的本分》。是：姑娘家读这个，是好兆头。往下讲。”
尤洛爵士把亮晶晶的绿眼睛凝在一个心像上；照他所观察的逐点描述，用的是他那套华丽繁花的托斯卡纳土话。“她有一张脸，能叫朱庇特烧起来，重新变公羊、鹰或公牛；能叫新旧的一切勋章害臊。她一头金发，像金线。两颊好似织锦玫瑰。嘴和眼睛抵得上一宗财宝。她有天使的、神圣的眉目：但一举一动、一颦一笑，全是人间的；她的好处没有穷尽。她有一双人人称道的好手：白得像山上的雪。她识字；说一口托斯卡纳话；一生找不出一个疤瘕。世上没有一个天鹅比我更懂得她。她做大事、顶多事、吃得少：吃饭中间从不喝水，下午茶（点心）也不喝。还有呢，我说。她行事实在有心窍，弄得我把世上所有的一切——或小或大——恨不得一挥手全给了她取乐。我生平再没见过更美的：再没有更肯伺候人的：再没有更稳重的：姑娘家的行事也没有更客气、更快活的。她一手彼特拉克、一手但丁；时候地点若由我定，她能轻轻呕吐出一首小十四行。一位全德全美的姑娘；她把我扣在那里作人质，若她肯——”
“那么：假设你娶了她，你会好好待她么？”

“Oh, that she shall be the my life and the my delight, dressed in velvet, guarded as a queen, for fear that if she goes about too much should not be robbed by some little hypocrite: that she shall live on collops and bread of baker﻿—”
“How amusing you are! Well: marry that paragon, and be good and happy. You must have an apartment in the City for her, you know;﻿—and, about your duties here:﻿—you can come when you like. You are not dismissed: but John and James will suffice. Understand, boy, you are wanted, wanted here, always.”
“I am here always, Santità.”
“No. Go-away and marry. ‘The most certain softeners of a man’s moral skin, and sweeteners of his blood, are domestic intercourse and a happy marriage and brotherly intercourse with the poor.’ Always remember that. By the by, what are you going to live on?”
“If I am always a Gentleman of Hadrian, I am having a plenty of money.”
“Ah, but you always will not be a Gentleman of Hadrian, because Hadrian will not be always; and, when He is not, His successor will say ‘Via! Via!’ to you.”
“And then I shall do some things?”
“Ah, but what things?”
“Who knows? But I shall do things.”
Hadrian went to the safe in the bedroom: then to the writing-table, and wrote. He came back with some papers in His hand.
“Attend! Take this note to Plowden by the Post-office. He will give you a thousand sterling. That is a marriage-gift to you, so that you may get an apartment in the City and marry that little daughter of the dentist. Don’t be silly. Listen. What do you know about photography?”
“About photography? But I know to use that kodak, the gift *della Sua osservantissima e venerabilissima Santità*.”
“And you do it very well. You are one of the few men now alive who perceive the right moment for pressing the button. Understand?”
“I see with eyes.”
“But there is something beside seeing with eyes. There is a mind which ponders and selects.”
“Too much of honour.”
“No. No honour at all: a stated fact. Well now: think of negatives. They are dense in places: clear in places; and, in other places, more or less dense. Understand? Under the negative you put a certain paper; and expose it to light. Light goes through the clear places and stains the paper black: it partly goes through the more or less dense places; and stains the paper grey in various gradations of tint. It fails to go through the dense places and leaves the paper white.

===Chinese===

“哦，她将是我的性命、我的欢喜，穿天鹅绒，当王后一样护着，生怕她出门太多，被哪个小伪君子抢了去：她将吃肉排、吃面包房的面包——”
“你这个人真有趣！好：娶了那位完人罢，好好地、快快活活地。你须在城里给她备一套住房，知道么；——至于你在这里的差事：——你爱来就来。你不是被辞退：不过约翰和詹姆斯两个尽够了。明白么，孩子，这里用得着你，用得着你，永远。”
“我永远在这里，圣下。”
“不。走罢，去结婚。‘软化一个男人道德之皮、加甜他的血，最稳当的，是家常来往、美满婚姻、同穷人的兄弟来往。’永远记着这句话。对了，你们往后靠什么过活？”
“只要我一直是哈德良的内廷侍从，我就有的是钱。”
“啊，可你不会一直是哈德良的内廷侍从，因为哈德良不会一直在；等祂不在了，祂的继任人会对你说：‘去！去！’”
“那时我就做事？”
“啊，可做什么事呢？”
“谁知道？可我会做出事来。”
哈德良走到卧房的保险柜前；再到写字台前写了几行。祂手里拿着几页纸回来。
“听着！这张条子你从邮局寄给普劳登。他会给你一千镑。那是给你的婚仪之赠，好叫你在城里备起住房、娶上牙医家的小女儿。别犯傻。听着。摄影你懂多少？”
“摄影？我会用那架柯达，那是*至敬至尊的圣下*所赐。”
“而且你用得很好。当今活着的人里，懂得按快门的正确时刻的，没有几个，你是其中之一。明白么？”
“我亲眼看得见。”
“可还有比亲眼更多的。有一颗会掂量、会拣选的心智。”
“太抬举了。”
“不。全无抬举：一句实情。好，现在想想底片。底片有的地方厚：有的地方透；还有的地方，厚薄不一。明白？在底片下垫上一种纸；曝光。光从透处过，把纸染黑：从厚薄不一处部分地过，把纸按不同色阶染成灰；从厚处过不去，纸留白。

===Original===

There is your photograph, a little black a little white and many different greys. Understand?”
“Yes, Santità.”
“Your photograph is an image of the form, the contours, the modelling, the morbidezza, of the object before your lens. It lacks one thing. It has not colour. The process has tralated colour into monochrome. Do you see that?”
“Yes, Santità.”
“Now white means a blend of all colours; and black means the absence of all colours. Then grey should mean some colours, of this quality or that, of this quantity or that, according to the clarity or the density of the grey. Understand?”
“Yes, Santità.”
“Your negative is black and white and many greys.”
“Yes, Santità.”
“Then understand that all colours lie hidden in the black and white and greys of the negative. In the black, lie all colours: it produces the positive white. In the white lie no colours: it produces the positive black. In the various greys, lie various colours﻿—why are you jumping about? Keep still and listen, wriggling lizard that you are! What do you want to do?”
“To liberate those poor colours.”
“So does everybody. At least, everybody wants to photograph in colours: so they paint on the backs of the films; and they play the fool with triply-coloured negatives. Only one man in the world knows that the colour already is there﻿—already is there, my boy﻿—stored in the black white grey negative; and that the black white grey ordinary negative will give up its imprisoned colours to him who has the key.﻿—Well now: take the second envelope. The key’s there; and it’s yours. (Don’t stare like that!) There are three other things as well, which may be useful. (Don’t say a word!) Read all those papers until you understand them. They’re quite simple. Then practise. When you can do the trick, you will want a little help to do it greatly, to make it useful. (Get off the floor!) Then take the third envelope to Plowden﻿—it’s mentioned in the first﻿—and he will give you two thousand sterling. (Don’t touch that foot!) That will be enough if you are industrious. Now you are trusted, Iulo mio. Be good always; and be kind to everybody. No don’t move. We are going into the gardens with Flavio. You stay here till you feel better.﻿—Ptlee-bl ptlee-bl ptlee-bl,” Hadrian mewed to His delighted and excited and persequent cat.

===Chinese===

这就是你的照片，一点黑、一点白、许许多多不同的灰。明白？”
“明白，圣下。”
“你的照片，是你镜头前那件物体的形、轮廓、立体、柔腴的影像。它缺一样。它没有颜色。这道工序把颜色翻译成了单色。你懂这个么？”
“懂，圣下。”
“如今，白意味着诸色之和；黑意味着诸色皆无。那么，灰就该意味着某些色——或这一种、或那一种，或多些、或少些——依那灰的明暗厚薄而定。明白？”
“明白，圣下。”
“你的底片是黑的、白的、许许多多灰的。”
“明白，圣下。”
“那么要明白：一切颜色都藏在那底片的黑白灰里。黑里藏着一切颜色：它晒出正片上的白。白里没有颜色：它晒出正片上的黑。各种灰里藏着各种颜色——你蹦什么？站稳了听，你这条乱扭的蜥蜴！你想做什么？”
“把那些可怜的颜色放出来。”
“谁不想。至少，人人都想照出彩色相：于是他们在胶片背面涂颜料；拿三层彩底耍把戏。全世界只有一个人知道：颜色早就在那里了——早就在那里了，我的孩子——就存在那黑白灰的底片里；那寻常的黑白灰底片，会把囚禁的颜色交出到持有钥匙的人手里。——好：拿第二个信封。钥匙在那里；归你了。（别那样瞪眼！）另外还有三样东西，也许有用。（一个字也别说！）把那些纸读到全懂为止。都很简单。然后练习。等你玩得转了，要把它做大、做成有用的，还须一点帮手。（从地上起来！）然后拿第三个信封去找普劳登——头一个信封里提到了——他会给你两千镑。（别碰那只脚！）你若勤快，这就尽够了。如今你受信托了，我的尤洛。永远行善；待人人仁厚。不，别动。我们同弗拉维奥到园子里去。你在这里待着，等心里平了再走。——咪溜-溜-溜-溜，”哈德良朝祂那只欢腾兴奋、紧追不舍的猫咪声说。

## XXIV / 二十四
===Original===

It was the festival of St. George, Protector of the Ninefold Kingdom. Hadrian noted with pleasure that it was what the Italians call one of His “fortunate days.” His head was clear, His limbs were supple, His body lithe: He felt young, exuberant, potent. His soul seemed balanced, elevated. His whole poise was one of gentle incisive simplicity. He had that upright rather dominant gait, by no means arrogant, which marks the happy able man. The Sacred College came early in the morning, directly after His mass, to congratulate Him on the anniversary of His pontificature; and Ragna took occasion to whisper that the Northern Emperor left Palazzo Caffarelli for the Quirinale at dawn. Everyone knew what that meant.
When, later, Hadrian descended in state to the Sala Regia, He was on the alert. The introducer-of-sovereigns announced﻿—the Ninefold King﻿—the President of the United States of America﻿—the Northern Emperor﻿—the Japanese Emperor﻿—and a posse of subsidiary kings, princes, and sovereign-dukes, who came with the world’s congratulations. The pontifical paraphernalia lay on the high red throne: but Hadrian stood at its foot to receive His guests. His garb was white, absolutely simple and fresh; and His pose was apostolic, frank and genial. These enormous potentates towered above Him in the splendour of their grandeur; and, as Cardinal Carvale, the fantastic dreamer, said to Cardinal Van Kristen, they radiated from Him as from a source of light.
After the ceremony of reception was finished, Their Majesties, Augustitudes, Highnesses, and Honours, lingered, chatting with the pontifical court. Some of them had a few words with the Supreme Pontiff. The Northern Emperor came and said, “I know that Your Holiness will felicitate me on a dispatch which I have just received from my brother Prince Henry, who announces that my glorious German navy has taken Kronstadt.”
Hadrian replied; and added “Be merciful, Augustitude.”
William then did a politely ferocious scowl, intended to indicate imperial impatience; and continued in a lower tone, “I am also anxious to assure Your Holiness that I myself deeply regret the absence of my cousin and imperial brother, Victor Emanuel. All that I could say has been said to persuade His Augustitude to join me on this auspicious and never-to-be-forgotten occasion. I wish that to be known.”

===Chinese===

这一天是圣乔治庆日，九重王国的主保。哈德良愉快地注意到：这是意大利人所说的祂的“幸运日”之一。祂的头脑清明，四肢柔韧，身体轻捷：祂自觉年轻、丰盈、有力。祂的灵魂仿佛平衡而高举。祂整个人的气度，是一种温和、敏锐的单纯。祂步态挺直而略带统摄，绝无倨傲——那是幸福而有能的人的步态。清晨，枢机团在祂弥撒之后即来庆贺祂就任教宗的周年；拉尼亚乘机低声说：北帝黎明时分离开了卡法雷利宫，往奎里纳莱去了。人人知道这意味着什么。
随后哈德良盛装降临王政厅时，祂是警醒的。掌君主引见官唱名——九重王——美利坚合众国总统——北帝——日本天皇——还有随行的一班次要国王、亲王、主权公爵，携全世界的祝贺而来。教廷的仪具陈在高高的红宝座上：但哈德良立在宝座脚下迎客。祂一身白，绝对素净而新鲜；祂的姿态是宗徒式的，坦白而亲切。这些庞然的巨主，以其盛装的威严高塔般耸在祂之上；而正如那位幻想家卡瓦尔枢机对范·克里斯滕枢机说的：他们的光，是从祂身上、从一个光源放射出去的。
接见礼毕，诸位陛下、至尊、殿下与贵人们流连着，同教廷宫廷闲谈。有几位同至尊教宗讲了几句话。北帝走过来说：“我知道教宗陛下要为我刚收到的一封电报向我道贺：我弟弟亨利亲王来电称，我光荣的德国海军攻下了喀琅施塔得。”
哈德良答了；又添一句：“发发慈悲罢，至尊。”
威廉于是摆出一副客气而凶狠的皱眉，意在表示皇帝的不耐烦；接着压低声音说：“我还急于向教宗陛下保证：我的堂兄、帝兄维托里奥·埃马努埃莱的缺席，我个人深引为憾。能说的话我都说了，劝祂至尊来同我共赴这吉庆难忘的盛会。我希望这一点为人所知。”

===Original===

“It only is a personal obstacle, not a political, which prevents the Southern Emperor from coming here?”
“Most Holy Lord, it is not even a personal obstacle. Victor Emanuel has the most profound and much-to-be-admired and preeminently-well-merited veneration and reverence for Your Person. It is﻿—well, really it seems almost childish﻿—but he has persuaded himself that﻿—”
“That the Roman Pontiff owes the King of Italy a visit?”
“Precisely, Holy Father. There is some history of an approach which His Augustitude’s royal and martyred father made to the Conclave of 1878﻿—”
“And for a mere idea, Victor Emanuel, will continue alienate from Us! Yet, ideas are very fine things, to be respected, to be cultivated, in this material age. They are so rare, so singular. And constancy, fidelity to an idea, above all things is singular and rare, in this age of compromise from which the world only now emerges. Victor Emanuel is not to be blamed, but praised.” Suddenly a bright light came in the Apostle’s eyes. “Well, then, the next step is obvious. If the son will not come to the Father, then the Father must go to the son.” And an impulse to instant movement appeared to urge Him onward.
The Northern Emperor splendidly rose to the occasion. “It would be one more grand deed added to Your Holiness’s many grand deeds. I trust that I may have the never-sufficiently-to-be-valued honour of accompanying You.”
“But We walk:” said Hadrian.
“I also will gladly walk:” said William.
The Pope darted a rapid glance round the hall. The King of Portugal was talking to the Japanese Emperor; and the Basil of the Hellenes was listening to the Prince of Montenegro-and-New-Serbia. The Ninefold King, with one arm paternally resting on the shoulder of the young King of Spain, was telling (as his own) an extremely funny story, (which he had heard five minutes before from Cardinal Semphill), to the President of America. Cardinals and sovereigns clustered round them, ploding with laughter at each admirably detailed jocosity. “We can escape this way;” the Pope said to the Emperor. Outside the hall, a pontifical page ran for the white three-cornered hat; and the two descended the Scala Regia, with its Ionic columns flanked by pontifical guards, and made their way into the Square of St. Peter’s. There was a cleared roadway; and they quickly walked between long lines of magnificent Italian soldiery.

===Chinese===

“那么，拦住南帝不到这里来的，只是一桩私人的芥蒂，不是政治的？”
“至圣的主上，连私人的芥蒂也不是。维托里奥·埃马努埃莱对陛下的圣躬，怀着最深、最堪钦佩、也最理所应得的了敬与虔恭。只是——唉，说起来几乎孩子气——他说服了自己，认为——”
“认为罗马教宗欠意大利国王一次拜访？”
“正是，圣父。有一段旧事：祂至尊的先父、殉难的王，曾在一八七八年的选举闭门会上有所接近——”
“而维托里奥·埃马努埃莱竟为一个观念，继续同我们疏远！可是，观念是很好的东西，在这个唯物的时代，值得尊重、值得栽培。它们这样稀少、这样奇特。而对一个观念的恒常与忠贞，在这妥协方退的世界里，尤属奇特而稀有。维托里奥·埃马努埃莱不当受责，当受褒。”宗徒的眼里忽然亮起一道光。“那么，下一步就明白了。儿子不肯到父这里来，父就必须到儿子那里去。”一股即刻行动的冲动仿佛催着祂向前。
北帝漂亮地接住了这个球。“这将又是教宗陛下许多伟业之外添上的一桩伟业。我恳求给我一份受之不尽的荣幸：陪您同去。”
“可我们是步行：”哈德良说。
“我也欣然步行：”威廉说。
教宗向大厅飞快地扫了一眼。葡萄牙国王正同日本天皇交谈；希腊人的巴塞琉斯在听黑山-新塞尔维亚亲王说话。九重王一只胳膊父爱地搭在年轻的西班牙国王肩上，正把一个极其好笑的故事（五分钟前刚从森普尔枢机那里听来的）当作自己的，讲给美洲总统听。枢机与君主们围成一团，每逢一个精彩的细节便轰然大笑。“我们可以从这边溜出去；”教宗对皇帝说。厅外，一名教廷侍童跑去取那顶白色三角帽；两人下了王政阶梯——两旁爱奥尼柱间立着教廷卫兵——走进圣彼得广场。道路已清空；两人在长长的意大利雄兵行列之间快步而行。

===Original===

Rome occupied the sidewalks; and sank to its knees as the Supreme Pontiff, shedding benedictions, went swinging lightly and swiftly by. The German Gentleman made no attempt to take salutes until Hadrian said, “Oh do notice these dear Romans. They will be pleased. And you know that you profoundly admire the bersaglieri.”
The Emperor responded, “I am as proud to salute the Romans as I am to salute the noblest Roman of them all﻿—to use the words of Your Holiness’s divine Shakespeare.” And he strode on, saluting, while the Pontiff blessed.
As they passed the Palazzo Venezia, Hadrian said, “Victor Emanuel really behaves extremely well. Three-quarters of his army are in the field; and here is a parcel of foreign sovereigns practically occupying his capital in﻿—no, not homage﻿—in courtesy to Us.﻿—”
“And also out of respect, Holiness.”
“Out of respect then and courtesy to Our Apostolature. It is no affair of his; and yet he lines the streets with troops, while he himself﻿—oh, it’s really very decent of him!”
“Victor Emanuel is a truly great man;” the Emperor commented. The Pope assented.
They entered the Palace of the Quirinale; and went straight through the ambassador’s hall to the Southern Emperor’s study. William remained in the antechamber. Victor Emanuel in a light-grey flannel suit was reading proofs of his numismatic catalogue. He stood up pale and stiff, when his groom-of-the-chambers came in and whispered a word. Hadrian followed on the instant, entering with candid gentle dignity, extending an English hand. Not a word was said. Victor Emanuel, shining with the light of the purple which he had not yet worn, took the outstretched hand: held it: felt his own gripped and held. He bent his head﻿—then his knee. Reconciliation was complete.
“May I have the honour and the happiness of presenting my wife to Your Holiness?” he said, a minute later. He went along the corridor and gave two raps on a further door. “Darling,” he cried; “please come.”
The exquisite Empress Elena appeared. She started slightly at first: but bravely came on, imperially mysteriously pale and radiant as “the chorus of nightly stars and the bright powers which bring summer and winter to mortals, conspicuous in the firmament.”
Hadrian at once won her with “And the lovely children.”
“Oh yes, the kiddies!” Victor Emanuel said.
“Do you know that We owe one immense emotion to your boy?”

===Chinese===

罗马占满了人行道；至尊的教宗一路挥洒着祝福，轻盈迅捷地摆臂走过，人群纷纷跪倒。这位德意志先生不打算答礼，直到哈德良说：“哦，理理这些亲爱的罗马人罢。他们会高兴的。再说，您不是深深钦佩神枪兵么。”
皇帝答道：“向罗马人敬礼，同向他们全体中最高贵的那位罗马人敬礼一样，是我引以为荣的——借用教宗陛下那位神圣的莎士比亚的话。”于是他大步向前，一路敬礼，教宗一路祝福。
行过威尼斯宫时，哈德良说：“维托里奥·埃马努埃莱行事实在极好。他的军队四分之三在战场上；而这里一伙外国君主，实际上占据着他的首都，为的——不，不是朝贺——是对我们表示客气。——”
“也是出于敬意，陛下。”
“那么就是出于对我们使徒职分的敬意与客气。这根本不是他的事；他却沿街布满军队，自己却——哦，他实在非常够意思！”
“维托里奥·埃马努埃莱是真正伟大的人；”皇帝评道。教宗首肯。
他们进了奎里纳莱宫；径直穿过大使厅，到南帝的书房。威廉留在前厅。维托里奥·埃马努埃莱穿一身浅灰法兰绒，正读他的钱币目录的校样。侍从官进来低语一字时，他站起身来，苍白而僵硬。哈德良随即跟到，以坦白温和的尊严走进，伸出一只英国人的手。一言未发。维托里奥·埃马努埃莱——他尚未穿过的紫色的光辉照亮着他——握住那只伸出的手：握住：也觉出自己的手被握住、被执着。他低下头——随后屈下膝。和解完成了。
“我能有这份荣幸与幸福，把我的妻子引见给教宗陛下么？”一分钟后他说。他沿走廊过去，在远处一扇门上叩了两下。“亲爱的，”他喊道；“请过来。”
娇美无双的埃莱娜皇后出现了。她起初微微一惊：但勇敢地走上前来，帝后式地、神秘地苍白而光彩，如“夜星的合唱、给凡人带来夏冬的光明之力，在天穹上赫赫可见”。
哈德良立刻用一句“还有可爱的孩子们”赢得了她。
“哦是，孩子们！”维托里奥·埃马努埃莱说。
“你们可知道，我们欠你们家男孩一场巨大的激动？”

===Original===

and Hadrian narrated the incident in Prince Attendolo’s garden.
Mother and father proudly laughed. “Yes, we heard about that, of course; and I wondered what would happen if ever we ourselves should meet Your Holiness by accident, as the children did:” the Empress said.
“Well, we have met, and now Your Augustitudes know:” laughed Hadrian.
“Filiberto is a queer little chap,” Victor Emanuel continued: “he says the most extraordinary things;﻿—came running into the stables the other morning crying because some dog had barked and startled him. ‘Stamp at ’em,’ I said; ‘and after all, you can run faster than a dog,’ said I to hearten him. ‘Yes’ says he ‘but you see, father, when I do run, I’m always putting out one leg at the back for the dog to bite!’ ”
“But I can tell you something better than that,” the Empress put in. “He was a bad boy in the chapel at benediction on Sunday. I’m afraid, Holiness, that this is rather a naughty story﻿—”
“Tell it instantly and relieve your sinful soul, daughter;” the haughty pontiff commanded.
How the three roared! She continued, “He persisted in trying to balance a pile of prayer-books on the ledge of his chair-back; and every now and then they came down with a crash. At last I took him on my knee; and told him that the holy angels were looking at him, and that they would go and tell the Lord God what a wicked little ruffian he was. And then he said﻿—he said, ‘Dirty little sneaks!’ ”
“Oh, oh, the exquisite boy!” Hadrian shouted with laughter.
“Well, I’ll go and fetch him;” said the Southern Emperor, running-out of the door, just as the Northern Emperor came-in by the other, prepared to play the part of peacemaker. That, now, was not necessary; and England, Germany, and Italy, chattered like children till the children came. Their father did not return. His men were having a bad time, trying to beat the record for getting a sovereign into his habit of ceremony.
The fair Prince Filiberto solemnly approached the Pope. “Are You the White Father which formerly I have seen in somebody’s forest?”
“Yes,” said Hadrian.
“Are You quite good now?” the boy continued, with great black basilic eyes.
“No,” said Hadrian, feeling the horror of the end of youth confronted with the flower of innocence.
“Are You truly contrite for having been a naughty boy﻿—no, man I mean?”
“Yes,” said Hadrian.

===Chinese===

于是哈德良讲了阿滕多洛亲王园子里那桩事。
父母骄傲地笑了。“是，我们当然听说了；我还纳闷，倘若我们自己像孩子们那样偶然遇见教宗陛下，会出什么事呢：”皇后说。
“喏，我们遇见了，如今两位至尊知道了：”哈德良笑道。
“菲利贝托是个古怪的小家伙，”维托里奥·埃马努埃莱接着说：“他说最不寻常的话；——那天早上跑进马厩里哭，因为哪条狗叫了，吓着他了。‘朝它们跺脚，’我说；‘再说，你到底跑得过狗，’我给他打气。‘是，’他说，‘可是您知道，爸爸，我一跑，总有一条腿翘在后面给狗咬！’”
“可我有个更好听的，”皇后插嘴。“礼拜日降福时他在小堂里不乖。恐怕，陛下，这故事有点顽皮——”
“立刻讲出来，好卸下你有罪的良心，女儿；”傲岸的教宗命令道。
三个人笑得多欢！她接着讲：“他一个劲要把一摞经书摞在椅背的横档上；时不时哗啦塌下来。末了我把他抱到膝上；告诉他，圣善的天使正看着他，要去告诉天主，他是个多么淘气的小坏子。于是他说——他说：‘告密的小脏鬼！’”
“哦，哦，这精美的孩子！”哈德良放声大笑。
“好，我去把他领来；”南帝说着跑出门去，正撞上北帝从另一扇门进来，预备扮演调停人。此刻用不着了；于是英格兰、德意志、意大利，像孩子似的闲扯，直到孩子们来。孩子们的父亲没有回来。他的手下正忙得不可开交，要抢在纪录之前，把一位君主塞进他礼仪的行头里。
金发的菲利贝托亲王庄重地走向教宗。“您就是从前我在谁家的林子里见过的白爸爸么？”
“是，”哈德良说。
“您现在完全乖了么？”孩子接着问，睁着大大的黑眼睛。
“不，”哈德良说，感到青春之终面对天真之花的战栗。
“您真的痛悔了自己顽皮——不，我是说做了坏男人？”
“是，”哈德良说。

===Original===

“Are You sitting on my father’s sofa because he has forgiven You?”
“Yes,” said Hadrian, thinking what a frightful old fool He must appear.
“I liked You when I saw You in that forest; and I like You now: but mother told me that the White Father was not my father’s friend.”
“Mother made a mistake, little son;” said the Empress, leaning forward in sudden confusion. “The White Father is father’s best friend.”
“Oh, how I am glad for that: because now You can be also my friend!” the prince cried, scattering his deliberate English to the four quarters of the globe.
“Most willingly,” said Hadrian, taking the rose-brown hand, and drawing the child towards Him. Innocence put up its pretty lips. The Apostle lost one breath;﻿—and stooped and kissed the stainless brow. Then He turned to greet the girls.
“This child once asked my husband a very awkward question,” the mother said, presenting the Princess Yolanda. “The King of England was coming here; and Victor was showing her His Majesty’s incoronation portrait. Ah, but how she admired it! And she said, ‘Father why don’t you wear a hat like that king?’ ”
The Supreme Pontiff looked at the blushing child. “You would not call it a ‘hat,’ Princess, now that you are grown up?”
“No, Papa Inglese﻿—a crown.”
“You would like your father to have a crown? Tell him that there are two waiting for him, one at Monza, and another in the Lateran.”
The Roman Emperors escorted the Pope returning to Vatican. On the way, carriages met them, and disgorged sovereigns: state-coaches met them, and emitted cardinals: courtiers alighted from horseback and emerged from motorcars. The return became a procession of the powers, led by the Power of the Keys. They had crossed the Ponte Santangelo, and were about to turn to the left by the Castle, when a dishevelled man in black contrived to break out from the ranks of the people. He got through the bersaglieri and stepped into the middle of the road: pointed a revolver at Hadrian; and fired. The bullet struck His Holiness high up on the left breast, piercing the pulmonary artery just above the lung.
The slim white figure stopped﻿—wavered﻿—and sank down. The whole world seemed to stand still, while the human race gasped once.
A frantic woman in a fox-coloured wig pitched out of the opposite crowd; and grovelled. “Love, Love,” she howled hideously: “oh and I loved him so! Oh! Oh! I really did love him.

“您坐在我爸爸的沙发上，是因为他饶恕了您么？”
“是，”哈德良说，心想自己这样一副老糊涂相该多么怕人。
“我在那林子里见了您就喜欢您；现在也喜欢您：可是妈妈告诉我，白爸爸不是我爸爸的朋友。”
“妈妈说错了，小儿子；”皇后忽然慌乱地俯身说。“白爸爸是爸爸最好的朋友。”
“哦，那我真高兴：因为现在您也可以做我的朋友了！”王子喊道，把他那一字一顿的英文撒向四面八方。
“极其情愿，”哈德良说，握住那只玫瑰棕色的小手，把孩子拉近。天真仰起它好看的小嘴。宗徒倒抽了一口气；——俯身亲吻那无瑕的额头。随后祂转身招呼女孩子们。
“这孩子有一次问了我丈夫一个非常难答的问题，”母亲说，把约兰达公主引上前来。“英格兰国王要来；维托里奥正把祂陛下的加冕像指给她看。啊，她多么爱慕那张像！她说：‘爸爸，你怎么不戴一顶那样的帽子？’”
至尊教宗望着脸红的孩子。“如今你长大了，还会叫它‘帽子’么，公主？”
“不，英国爸爸——是王冠。”
“你愿意你父亲有一顶王冠？告诉他：有两顶正等着他，一顶在蒙扎，一顶在拉特兰。”
两位罗马皇帝护送教宗回梵蒂冈。一路上，马车迎面而来，吐出君主们：仪车迎面而来，送出枢机们：朝臣们下马、钻出汽车。归途变成了列强的行列，由钥匙之权领头。他们过了圣天使桥，正要在城堡旁左转，一个披头散发的黑衣男人从人群里挣脱出来。他穿过神枪兵，站到路中央：朝哈德良举起左轮枪；开了火。子弹击中教宗陛下左胸高处，穿透肺动脉，恰在肺的上方。
那纤细的白色身影停住——晃了晃——倒了下去。整个世界仿佛凝住，人类倒抽了一口气。
一个戴狐皮色假发的疯女人从对面人群里扑出来；伏在地上。“爱，爱，”她凄厉地嚎：“哦，我是那样爱他！哦！哦！我真的爱过他。

===Original===

Yes I did, I did, I did, I did﻿ ﻿…” she yelped to the sun in the firmament of heaven. The discord resembled the baying of a dog which breaks the cadence of Handel’s “Largo” on arch-lutes.
God’s Vicegerent moved﻿—looked at her from a distance, gently, even curiously. “Daughter, go in peace,” He said and turned away. She remained there grovelling, longing to touch Him, forlorn, gorgonized.
The Roman Emperors also kneeled to right and left, fiercely looking among their aides for the help which did not come, which could not come, from man.
The assassin was in a hundred tearing hands. Screeches shot out of his gullet when they silently and inevitably began to tear him to pieces. Roman knives flashed over the parapet; and slid into Tiber: hooked hands, like the curving talons of griffins, were the weapons for this work. But the Supreme Pontiff beckoned him; and the gesture was unmistakeable﻿—universally authoritative. Shaken and violently shaking, jagged, lacerated, a disreputable wreck of Pictish ready-made tailoring, Jerry Sant staggered forward, staggered like one fascinated. Cardinals and sovereigns drew away from him, and the mob hemmed him in.
“… for they know not.﻿ ﻿…” The Apostle raised himself a little, supported by imperial hands. How bright the sunlight was, on the warm grey stones, on the ripe Roman skins, on vermilion and lavender and blue and ermine and green and gold, on the indecent grotesque blackness of two blotches, on apostolic whiteness and the rose of blood.
“Augustitudes, Our will and pleasure is﻿—”
“Speak it, Most Holy Father﻿—”
“Augustitudes, We name you both the ministers of this Our will.” And to the murderer He said, “Son, you are forgiven: you are free.”
Down Borgo Nuovo came guards, chamberlains, curial prelates, cardinals, from Vatican. The English and American cardinals took their vermilion on their arms, and ran like lithe long-limbed schoolboys. The faithful young Sir John outran them all. He kneeled to Hadrian, Who said,
“Dear John, take this cross﻿—and Flavio.” The Southern Emperor unclasped the chain and rosy pectoral cross; and handed them to the gentleman-of-the-apostolic-chamber, who took them and fainted away. Out of Santo Spirito, came one with the stocks of sacred chrism. Cardinals Van Kristen and Carvale, panting, kneeled before the Ruler of the World.

是的，爱过，爱过，爱过，爱过……”她朝高天上的太阳尖声叫。这不谐之音，像一条狗的吠，打断琉特琴上亨德尔《广板》的节律。
天主的副摄政动了动——远远地、温和地、甚至好奇地望了她一眼。“女儿，去罢，平安，”祂说完转过身去。她仍伏在那里，渴望碰一碰祂，凄惶，僵作石。
两位罗马皇帝也左右跪下，凶狠地在随从里寻找那从人而来的救助——没有来，也来不了。
刺客落在一百只撕裂的手里。他们无声而不可遏止地开始把他撕成碎片时，他喉咙里射出尖叫。罗马的刀在栏杆上闪亮；滑进台伯河：钩曲的手，像狮鹫弯爪，便是这件活计的兵器。但至尊教宗向他一招手；那手势明白无误——带着普遍的权威。杰里·桑特——抖着，又被剧烈地摇着，破破烂烂，血肉横开，一身皮克特成衣的寒碜残骸——踉跄上前，像被摄住了一样踉跄。枢机与君主们从他身边退开，人墙把他围住。
“……因为他们不知道。……”宗徒由帝王之手扶起，微微欠身。阳光多么明亮，照在温热的灰石上，照在成熟的罗马皮肤上，照在朱红、淡紫、蓝、貂、绿与金上，照在两块不体面的怪诞黑斑上，照在宗徒的白与血的玫瑰上。
“二位至尊，我们的意愿与乐意是——”
“请讲，至圣的父——”
“二位至尊，我们任命你们两位，为我们这一意愿的执行人。”又对那凶手说：“孩子，你被赦免了：你自由了。”
新博尔戈街那头，卫兵、礼宾侍从、教廷神长、枢机们从梵蒂冈奔来。英美两国的枢机把朱红撩上臂弯，像四肢修长的学童那样飞跑。忠诚的年轻人约翰爵士跑过了他们所有人。他跪到哈德良跟前，祂说：
“亲爱的约翰，收下这十字架——还有弗拉维奥。”南帝解开链条和玫瑰色的胸十字；递给这位宗座宫务院的先生；他接过来，晕了过去。圣神医院那边，来了一个捧圣油瓶的。范·克里斯滕和卡瓦尔两位枢机喘着跪在世界之主面前。

Percy drew out the hidden pontifical pyx: took the Sacred Host therefrom; and held It. “The profession of faith, Most Holy Lord,” he bravely whispered.
“I believe all that which Holy Mother Church believes. I ask pardon of all men. Dear Jesus, be not to me a Judge but a Saviour.”
Cardinal Sterling gravely intoned the commendation of a Christian soul. The splendid company of angels, the senate of apostles, the army of white-robed martyrs, the lilied squadron of shining confessors, the chorus of joyful maids, patriarchs, hermits, Stephen and Lawrence, Silvester and Gregory, Francis and Lucy and Mary Magdalene, Mary﻿—God’s Own Mother, all the saints of God who daily are invited to attend the passing of the poorest Christian soul, were invoked for the Father of Princes and Kings. “And mild and cheerful may the Aspect of Christ Jesus seem to thee﻿—” The singer’s voice failed. Cardinal Carvale went on with no interval: imparted absolution, and the sacrament of the dying. “Saints of God advance to help him: Angels of The Lord come to meet him, receiving his soul, offering it in the Sight of The Most High.” The splendour of mortal words reverberated from the ancient fortress wall, in the great silence of Immortal Rome.
When the Earthly Vicar of Jesus Christ had received Extreme Unction and Viaticum, when He had had done for Him all that which Christ’s Church can do, He required to be lifted on His feet. The Roman Emperors rose, raising Him. The vehement ferocity of their aspect terribly contrasted with their tender movement. The torments of powerless power, of intimidation inflicted in the supreme moment of exultation, rent these grand strong men﻿—and graced them. The bloodstain streamed down the Pope’s white robes with the red stole of universal jurisdiction. The slender hand with the two huge rings ascended. The shy brown eyes fluttered; and were wide, and very glad. Then the tired young voice rang like a quiet bell.
“May God Omnipotent, ✠﻿✠﻿✠ Father, ✠﻿✠﻿✠ Son, ✠﻿✠﻿✠ and Holy Ghost, bless you.”
It was the Apostolic Benediction of the City and the World.
The hand and the dark eyelashes drooped, and fell. The delicate fastidious lips closed, in the ineffable smile of the dead who have found out the Secret of Love, and are perfectly satisfied.
So died Hadrian the Seventh, Bishop, Servant of the servants of God, and (some say) Martyr.

===Chinese===

珀西掏出那枚隐藏的教宗圣体盒；从盒中请出圣体；捧着它。“请发信德，至圣的主上，”他勇敢地低声说。
“我信圣母教会所信的一切。我求众人宽恕。亲爱耶稣，莫做我的审判者，做我的救主。”
斯特林枢机庄重地诵起基督徒灵魂的托付。光辉的天使团、宗徒的元老院、白衣殉道者的军队、持百合花的精修圣者光明的分队、喜乐贞女的歌队、圣祖、隐修士、斯德望与老楞佐、西尔维斯特与额我略、方济各与路济亚与玛利亚玛达肋纳、玛利亚——天主自己的母亲，凡每日受邀来看顾最贫穷的基督徒灵魂过往的一切天主圣人，都被请来为君王与亲王之父转求。“愿基督耶稣的圣容，温和而欢愉地向你显现——”诵者的声音断了。卡瓦尔枢机无缝接上：行了赦罪，行了临终圣事。“天主的圣人们，前来助佑他：主的天使们，前来迎接他，接收他的灵魂，呈于至高者面前。”属人言语的光辉，从古老的堡垒墙上荡回，在不朽罗马的伟大寂静里。
基督在世的代表领了病人圣油与临终圣体，教会能为祂做的一切都做了之后，祂要求扶祂站起。两位罗马皇帝起身，扶起祂。他们面容上猛烈的凶狠，同他们轻柔的动作惨然相对。无力之力量的苦楚、在凯旋顶点所受的威吓之痛，撕扯着这两位高大强壮的人——也给他们加了荣。血渍流下教宗的白袍，如普世管辖权的红色圣带。戴着两枚巨戒的纤手升起。那双腼腆的棕眼睛颤了颤；睁大了，充满欢喜。随后那疲惫的年轻嗓音响起，像一口安静的钟。
“愿全能的天主，✠✠✠圣父，✠✠✠圣子，✠✠✠及圣神，降福你们。”
这是给这座城与全世界的宗徒祝福。
手垂下去，黑的睫毛垂下去，落了。那挑剔的薄唇合拢，带着死人寻得了爱的奥秘、且完全满足时那种莫可言说的微笑。
哈德里安七世就这样死了：主教，天主众仆之仆，并且（有人说）殉道者。

===Original===

So died Peter in the arms of Caesar.
The world sobbed, sighed, wiped its mouth; and experienced extreme relief.
The college of Cardinals summed Him up in the brilliant epigram of Tacitus. “*Capax imperii nisi imperâsset.*” He would have been an ideal ruler if He had not ruled.
Religious people said that He was an incomprehensible creature. And the man on the motor said that the pace certainly had been rather rapid.
Pray for the repose of His soul. He was so tired.
**Feliciter**

===Chinese===

彼得就这样死在凯撒的怀抱里。
世界抽噎，叹息，擦了擦嘴；随即感到极度的轻松。
枢机团用塔西佗那句漂亮的隽语给祂下了总评。“*Capax imperii nisi imperâsset.*（他若不曾统治，原堪为君。）”
宗教界人士说，祂是一个不可解的造物。而摩托车上那人说：车速确实是快了些。
请为祂灵魂的安息祈祷。祂太累了。
**Feliciter**（功德圆满）
